# Changelog

All notable changes to `wisp-agents` are documented here.
The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and the project uses [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **Run on another computer sets the project up there**, as before: that computer finds its own checkout of the repo (or clones it from the origin), adds it to its projects, and the task runs in the same subfolder. A folder with no git remote says so.
- **Pictures and videos in answers show again**, served from the task's own computer. Click one for a full-screen viewer that goes through the answer's other pictures and videos with ← / →; hover for **Copy image**, **Download** and **Show in folder**. A screenshot the agent saved in /tmp is kept in `~/.wisp/shots`, so it still shows after a restart.
- **👍 / 👎 on each finished turn**, with an optional line on what went wrong after 👎, and **Send feedback** (⌘K, *Tell us* at the foot of home, `/feedback …` or `/bug …` in any prompt box). It goes to the wisp team with the harness and model, never your prompt or code; offline, it waits and goes later.
- **`/btw …` in the reply box** asks the agent a side question while it works; the answer shows under the turns, and × dismisses it.

- **Queue follow-ups again.** While a task works, the reply bar's Queue switch makes follow-ups wait behind the run; the queue shows over the box, where you can drag to reorder, edit, combine, Send now or cancel. A Stop keeps them, held.
- **Start later from New task.** Pick a time on the clock, or start the prompt with one ("in 5 min …", "tomorrow at 9 …", "remind me … in 1 hour"); `/draft …` keeps it in Drafts, `/after <task> …` starts it once that task is done.
- **Rerun… lets you change it first**: the prompt, mode, harness and model, and which computer it runs on.
- **Run on** in New task picks which computer a task runs on, and the harness picker shows that computer's harnesses.
- **Thinking is a slider again** in the harness picker, from Default to the harness's top level.
- **A task reopens where you left it**, after switching tasks or reloading.
- **Paste a task ID** into a prompt and it becomes a mention of that task. ⌘K also finds tasks by the topics agents tagged them with.

- **⌘K finds past work by its words.** Type three letters or more and tasks whose prompts or answers hold them show too, with the words found.
- **A compare is one card in the list.** Its runs sit under one head that counts what finished, with **vs Compare** to see them side by side.
- **Ship and Merge say when comments aren't sent yet.** Comments you wrote in Changes and haven't sent are counted before you ship or merge.

- **vs in New task.** The + beside the harness runs the same task on another harness too (up to four side by side); each extra run sits behind a "vs" that removes it. They land as one compare, ready for Compare runs.
- **Compare's Plan row.** What each run took of its plan's window, and what overlapping runs on one account took together. wisp now traces each window's move to the turns that ran while it moved.
- **The Watch tab opens by itself** when the agent opens a page while you're looking at its task.
- **The trigger form's Harness is the runner picker** New task uses: pick a harness and its model, or Use launcher default.
- **No output for 12 min.** A run that has gone quiet says so in its working panel, and keeps counting until something happens.
- **↑ brings back what you sent** in New task and in a reply (the last 50, kept across reloads); **Shift+Tab** cycles Plan, Edits and Full auto.
- **A simpler first New task.** Until your first task the box is the prompt, the mode, the harness and Send; More options shows the rest.

- **Compare runs.** `wisp --compare codex,opencode "…"` runs the same ask on each harness as one group; a run's menu then has Compare runs: the numbers side by side (time, cost, tokens, cache, steps, files, tests, questions asked), a plain sentence against run A, each run's answer, and Keep this (the other runs are archived and their worktrees deleted, after you confirm).
- **Watch beside a task.** When an agent calls `wisp_watch`, its page (a progress page it draws, or the app it's running) shows in a Watch tab beside the task and updates as it works, with how to run the thing yourself under it. Watch is in the task's menus too.
- **v1's trigger form is back.** Automations from ⌘K (Recipes, Triggers) opens as v1's dialog, and New trigger has the kind tiles, Send to, Project, When with its presets and what it understood, the GitHub, webhook and Slack fields, Run (a prompt or a recipe), Mode and Harness.

- **⌘K Recipes and Triggers** open Automations on that tab, as before.
- **Merge PR and Review PR in a task's menus**: Merge asks its confirm on the task's PR strip; Review opens the PR pane (P).
- **Run app starts the app, and Stop app stops it.** From a task's menu: Run app opens its Preview and starts the app with its command; while it runs the menu says Stop app.
- **The open task's More has everything again**: Rename, Copy task ID, Copy resume command (paste it in your own terminal to carry on the conversation), Open project in editor, Save for later, Restore to before latest turn, and what the right-click menu offers. Long menus fit the window.
- **Everything v1's task menu had, on a right-click.** Rerun, Fork, Side discussion, Share, Ship it, Changes, Shell, Run app, Save as recipe (a new recipe editor, from the task), Add to crew (the task becomes the new member's first job), Set goal and the goal's other actions, and Keep worktree, with their keys. The menu slides to fit the window, and the open task's More has them too.
- **See why Auto picked what it did.** Click Auto's pick in New task: its reasons, odds, cost and time, a model worth a try and others to run instead, *Never pick this model for this kind of task* (with Undo) and How Auto picks. It shows before you type, too.
- **The desktop app runs wisp's own server.** The app starts the same `wisp` binary Homebrew and npm ship, no bundled Node, and stops it when you quit, however the app goes.
- **Panes beside tasks.** In My Stuff, an app's ⋯ → Pin beside tasks puts it in a tab of the panel beside every task, after Changes, PR, Shell and Preview; the app is told which task is open. Unpin from the tab's bar or My Stuff.
- **A crew member's card beside the rail.** Hover a face (or focus it and press →) to see who it is, what it's up to and the jobs it's on, each one click away; its delegated jobs no longer repeat in the desktop list.
- **Pick an element in Preview.** The run's page now goes through a small local proxy so wisp can add its picker; click Pick, then an element, and its selector, HTML and styles go in the reply box.
- **Panes on Home.** Pin an app from My Stuff on Home (its ⋯ → Pin on home, or Add on one of Make a pane's starters): it shows in a tile under Needs you, side by side when there are several, and on a phone as a row that opens it full screen. It asks before it uses wisp (Allow…), and every task it wants to start or follow up waits for your click.
- **A crew member's jobs for other tasks sit under them** in the phone's Crew section, as list cards (3 at most, then "2 more · open Ada"), and leave the list until they need you.
- **Move and link on the Org page.** Drag a card to put it where you want (kept on every device), or drag from its handle onto another member to make them a delegate; Reset layout puts the chart back.
- **Folder suggestions where you type a path.** Settings → Plugins (with Folder…, which in the desktop app opens the system's folder dialog) and Projects (with Browse… in the desktop app): type `~/co` and pick, or press Tab to go into a folder.

- **Comments you sent show as cards.** A follow-up that carried comments on a task's changes shows each one in the transcript (file:line, the quoted line, your note) above what you typed, instead of the raw `<comments>` text.
- **Machine load says how busy the CPU is.** Settings → Usage → Machine load's *Now* reads "71% memory free · CPU at 50% of its cores · 0 runs of 8 going", as v1 did.
- **Turn every plugin off**, from the note under Install a plugin in Settings → Plugins.

- **Open a task's folder in your editor or terminal app** from the side panel's ⋯ (with Copy path and Copy branch name). The editor is the one Settings → General names.

- **Preview in a task's side panel.** Run the app from the task's folder (wisp guesses the command from the project) and see it there, with its logs, Restart, Stop and Open in browser.
- **Comments on lines of a task's changes.** Point at a line, press +, say what should change: comments wait over the reply box and go with your next follow-up.
- **Usage charts and the crew's track record.** Settings → Usage → Open usage shows spend or turns per day, by project and by harness · model, over 7, 30 or 90 days; Crew track record shows each member's jobs.
- **Spare allowance on Home.** When a weekly window resets within a day with a fifth or more left, Home says so and offers that account.
- **Cast a plugin.** Settings → Plugins: describe a change to wisp and an agent makes it as a plugin, installed off until you switch it on.

- **Org.** The crew as a chart of who hands work to whom: select a member and pick their delegates in the chart (+ adds, ✓ takes off); the column beside it lists who they hand work to and get work from.
- **Import crew members, and Make a pane.** Paste a shared member's .crew.json or a link, review who joins, add them paused; or describe a pane and an agent builds it, or start from one wisp ships.
- **The working panel is back on a task:** who's working, for how long, the last steps, and Stop, which asks first and says what else stops.
- **A member's Task panel tint** (their glow, none, or a colour) in the crew editor, and the crew in the phone's list.
- **Stage, commit and revert from Changes.** The side panel's Changes is v1's again: Stage a file or a hunk, Revert a hunk (it's hidden at once) or a whole file, mark files Viewed, and commit from the panel (Enter; an empty message uses the task's title).
- **⌘K looks and reads like v1's.** One sheet near the top: your tasks first with their project's dot and "project · state · age", then the crew, then Actions (New task, Start from an issue…, Add project…, Manage projects…), projects last.
- **Add to your crew, as v1 had it.** Start from a ready-made member (Verifier, CI watcher, PR reviewer…), roll a face, name them and give their job, pick when they check in (or type it in words) and what else wakes them, then where and how they run.
- **Sandboxes.** `/sandbox fix the login` in New task runs the task in a box of its own on your wisp Grid: your project goes in (uncommitted changes too), the agent can install and break anything there, and the task shows in your list as Sandbox. A delegate (its place: Sandbox), a crew member's jobs (a sandbox per job, or their own) and a goal's judges can run in one too. Each sandbox goes with what it was made for.
- **Plugins you install can have a Settings pane, safely.** An untrusted plugin's UI runs in a sealed frame with no access to wisp, your cookies or the network; it describes what to show and wisp draws it with its own components, and anything it does goes through the permissions it asked for.
- **Discord on a cloud wisp checks every interaction at the grid.** A forged or unsigned `/wisp` is refused before it can wake your box.
- **Terminal tasks.** With Settings → General → *Terminal sessions* on, or a prompt that starts with `/terminal`, a task you send opens in the agent's own CLI in a terminal inside wisp, already working on your prompt. wisp still keeps its folder, worktree and title; a follow-up from wisp's box is typed into the terminal, or opens it again on the same conversation once it ended; when it ends, its turns come back into the task. Crew jobs, schedules, goals and held tasks still run in wisp.
- **Discord `/wisp`.** `/wisp` with its options (crew, project, machine, model, mode, completed as you type for linked people only) starts or steers a task like a mention; a report's choices are buttons; a cloud wisp takes `/wisp` and button presses through its grid, which answers Discord at once while the box wakes.
- **Settings → Computers → Sandboxes.** Sign in to Claude once for every sandbox (on your own subscription; the token is sealed on the grid, never kept here), env vars every sandbox gets, and each sandbox with whose it is, Open, Sign in to Codex there, and Delete.
- **Your other computers' tasks in your list.** A machine wisp keeps (another computer, a sandbox) is reached through this wisp, so its tasks show and open like yours.
- **The file view's ✦ and Go to definition.** ✦ puts the file (or the lines you selected) in the reply box to ask about it; ⌘-click or F12 on a name opens where it's declared, at its line. New task's @ offers the picked project's files.
- **Trust a plugin you installed** (`plugins.trust`, ADR-0048): installed plugins are untrusted whatever they say, and an untrusted plugin's process starts without your environment (keys, tokens).
- **Projects show everywhere.** The top bar's chips, New task's row and Home now list your projects (they were empty on a real server), and picking a chip filters the list.
- **The Projects dialog.** Add a folder or a GitHub URL (cloned for you), see how many tasks each project has, rename it, give it a colour, remove it, and add a repo found on this computer with one click.
- **Your project order.** Drag a project (or Alt+←/→) in the top bar, New task's row or the Projects dialog; every place keeps the same order.
- **Install a plugin from Settings → Plugins.** A folder or a git URL; it comes in off and says, in words, what switching it on lets it do. Remove takes it out. `wisp plugin install <folder or URL>` and `wisp plugin remove <id>` do the same.
- **The file view.** ✎ on a changed file (or Open on a file in the reply box's @ menu) opens it beside the task: edit, then Save or ⌘S. It never saves over a change made since you opened it, or while the agent is working. Its why-gutter shows which task wrote each line; a click opens it.
- **Email attachments reach the task.** Files you attach to a mail go with the task it starts (or the reply it follows up), up to 1 MB each; any left out are said in the answer.
- **Edit a crew member.** Edit… on a member's page (and + on the rail) opens their editor: name, role, brief, how often they check in, jobs at once, harness, mode, folder, and Wakes: what else starts their jobs (another member reporting, a PR, a channel message…).
- **The Repo tab.** Beside Tasks, a project's committed agent history from everyone who worked on it, grouped by month and filtered by words or a path; a row opens its report, and Resume carries on from there on today's code.
- **Settings → Setup.** What's set up here (crew, agents, workflows, triggers… with counts), Share setup (the whole setup or some kinds, as `wisp.yml`, to copy), and Import: paste a formula, see what it adds, changes and removes, untick what you don't want, then Apply.
- **Ask a crew member in a task.** A reply that starts with `@Ada` goes to Ada, not the task's agent: it's her job, and her answer shows under that message. Reply to it there and the same job carries on, its next answer right under yours.
- **`wisp sdk types ts|py [FILE]`** writes the typed commands of the running wisp for the TypeScript or Python SDK, the same text `generateTypes()` and `generate_stubs()` make.
- **A PR comment follows its branch's task.** `/wisp …` on a PR with no task of its own goes to the newest task working on that PR's branch in the project.
- **Install a plugin while wisp runs** (`plugins.install`, ADR-0046): from a folder or a git URL into `~/.wisp/plugins`. It starts off and says what it asks for; switching it on is the consent, and it stays on across restarts. `plugins.uninstall` removes it.
- **A crew member's page.** Picking a face on the rail opens their thread: every job oldest first (quiet check-ins folded into one line), what they're doing in the head, Pause or Resume, Check in now, their notebook and brief (edit them in place), and a box to give them a job. The list beside it holds only their jobs.
- **The grid's own pages.** Signed in at your grid's address: home (your cloud wisp, your computers, your published apps) and your account (signed-in devices, how you sign in, sign out, delete), with every button working. When the grid routes Telegram, the account page has the form to connect your bot and say who may send.
- **A new trigger from the app.** Automations → Triggers → New trigger: pick what starts it from every source the plugins offer, grouped by plugin, fill in that source's fields and the prompt. Test on the last 5 events shows what it would have done.
- **My shares.** Settings → Cloud lists what you've shared through wisp Cloud, with who may open it, its views, imports and version, and Copy link, Open and Stop sharing.
- **The reply box's harness · model.** The same searchable picker as New task; your pick goes with the next replies. The picker shows each harness's logo.
- **Ask near the limit, Wait for the reset and Check Codex while work waits** in Settings → Usage.
- **New task starts with your team.** The team in Settings (`runtime.subagents`) is picked for you, and New task sends the team you pick.
- **Slack, Discord, GitHub and email reach wisp.** Mention the bot in Slack or Discord (or DM it), comment `/wisp …` on an issue or PR of your repo, or mail your cloud computer's address, and the message reads like New task: a task starts and answers there, in its thread, issue or reply. Only people you linked (or you) may speak; a channel can't ask for more than Edits. Slack's questions come with buttons.
- **Ask near the limit and Wait for the reset.** Before a task would take a usage window past your line (Settings → Usage, 90% by default), wisp asks first; automations wait for the reset instead. A task at its limit can carry on by itself after the reset (Wait for the reset), and Check Codex while work waits reads Codex's usage every 10 minutes while something waits on it. The CLI's `--yes` goes ahead anyway.
- **Mail on a wisp Grid.** A grid with `mail` gives each cloud computer a secret address; only mail the sender's own domain signed (DKIM, checked by the grid) reaches it, and replies go only to the thread they answer.
- **The SDKs, for v2.** `wisp-agents/client` (TypeScript) and `wisp` (Python) run any command and follow tasks live, with types generated from your wisp's own catalogue (`generateTypes`, `generate_stubs`).
- **Pick the harness and model in New task.** The harness · model button opens a searchable picker: harnesses on the left, their models on the right, and the thinking level for the model you pick.
- **Auto says what it will run, and learns from your pick.** As you type, New task shows Auto's pick (harness, model, thinking) with why in its tooltip. Pick your own after that, and Auto learns from it. A task Auto routed shows a dot on its harness logo, and the tooltip says why Auto picked it, how likely it is to work and what it should cost.
- **Settings → Memory.** Learn from tasks on or off. What's remembered, with Open memory: preferences and lessons for every project and for each project, each one removable, with a box to add more, and the recent retros with what each one learned.
- **Start from an issue.** The Issue button in New task lists the project's open GitHub issues, or the Linear and Jira issues assigned to you. Search as you type. Picking one fills the prompt (a draft it replaces comes back with Undo). Connect Linear… and Connect Jira… open Settings → Connectors with the connector filled in.
- **The cold cache and Compact now.** On a large idle task, the reply box says when the prompt cache has gone cold and how much the next reply re-reads. While the cache is warm, the task's ⋯ menu offers Compact now, with what it costs now and what the same reply would cost once the cache is cold.
- **Usage limits and refusals, on the turn.** A turn that hit a limit offers Wait until the reset (it carries on by itself then), the other harnesses, and Get extra usage. A refused turn offers the harnesses that run another vendor's model.
- **Drain watch, window changes and Lean runs.** Settings → Usage warns when an account drains fast (Copy report, Dismiss) and lists window changes, each dismissible. Settings → Agents has Lean runs, and a Claude task's ⋯ menu sets it for that task.
- **Publish from My Stuff.** An app's menu has Publish to your grid, Copy public link, Open public link, Update the public link and Unpublish.
- **Crew tint.** Picking a crew member on the rail gives the task list and its top bar a very light wash of their colour. Settings → General → Crew tint turns it off.
- **Settings, every pane.** Agents (each harness with its version, models and state; extra Claude and Codex accounts; a CLI's sign-in steps; opencode's keys; Install; Re-check; the notice when a harness's program changed; git and the GitHub CLI), Connectors (MCP servers every task's agent gets: add a local command or a remote URL with its keys kept in the secrets file, import the ones your harnesses already have, each checked by starting it and listing its tools, OAuth sign-in for remote ones), Machines (computers over SSH, and what another needs to reach this one: SSH server, Tailscale, keys), Cloud (sign in to a wisp Grid, this computer on it), Automations (recipes and triggers, run now), Usage (the week's spend, turns, how many worked and questions; each plan's windows; where unattended work stops), Privacy (usage stats, and asking before a repo's own programs run), Plugins (v1's Spells: every plugin and its switch) and All settings (every setting a plugin declares, searchable, with what locks it).
- **The task page, whole.** Every answer block draws again: the follow-up chips (a click puts the text in the reply box, ⌘-click sends it), the agent's question with its numbered answers, Blocked by permissions with Allow & retry and Deny, one-click actions that run in the task's folder and show their exit code and output (and offer to send a failure to the agent), the terminal and test results with Run again, cards for another task or something in My Stuff, and before/after. Under each turn: why it stopped and what next (signed out: Sign in; a usage limit: Send again or hand it to another harness; a refusal: hand it to a harness on another company's model), what holds it with Start now and Skip, what changed in the CLI since the last turn, and its receipt (cache share, its share of the plan's week, Past your plan). The side panel's PR pane (where the PR stands with Merge or Fix conflicts, checks with the passed folded, reviews, the conversation, files) and Shell pane (a login shell in the task's folder, End shell, Start again). Older turns fold behind Show earlier; a step with output opens to it; rename a task by clicking its title.
- **Settings as v1 had it.** One list in v1's order, the 900 × 680 sheet, full screen on a phone; Take over in (In wisp or a terminal app); Hide .env values; Webhook triggers; Subagents run on (Haiku or Sonnet for Claude Code's own subagents); the usage tiles with the label over the number; and every harness with its mark in Settings → Agents.
- **v1's keys.** J and K move through the list, O or Enter opens, E marks done, A archives, L saves for later, M renames, Delete deletes; on an open task S ships, ⇧R reruns, ⇧F forks, T opens it in its harness, Ctrl+` opens its shell, V the full transcript, D the changes, P the PR, ] the side panel, / the reply box, 1–9 answers its question, C collapses and U goes back to the top.
- **@ and / in the prompt box.** @ offers your tasks, crew members and what's in My Stuff, and sends the one you pick as a mention; / offers the commands that box knows.
- **Drop or paste files on the prompt box.** They upload at once and go with the prompt; a pasted screenshot gets a name.
- **Scheduled tasks and reminders open.** They show in the list's Scheduled section again, and open to their page: change what it will say (saved when you click away), Start now, Change start time…, Delete.
- **My Stuff.** What you and your agents kept, from the sidebar: search, kinds, by day, cards with their menu (open or launch, copy, download, star, its task, show in folder, remove), and Add files.
- **A task's queued follow-ups.** Over the reply box, each with when it goes, and Send now, Edit, Combine with the next, Move and Cancel.
- **Notifications.** A task that finishes or asks tells you: a toast in the app, and a system notification when wisp isn't in front.
- **Undo a turn.** wisp keeps where the folder was as each turn starts; the turn's rollback puts the files and the branch back there.
- **Fable in the model picker.** Claude Code's newest and most capable model, Fable 5.2 (`--model fable`), is the first choice after Default. Auto ranks it a frontier model, and Settings → Auto's never-pick and prefer rules name it like any model.
- **`wisp tui`.** wisp full-screen in your terminal, in wisp's colours: dark, or light when your terminal's background is light (`WISP_THEME=dark|light|contrast` to choose), with 256 colours when the terminal has no true colour (always in Apple's Terminal, which shows 24-bit colour as grey; `WISP_TUI_COLOR=truecolor|256|16` to choose) and ASCII glyphs on a dumb terminal. Your tasks are on the left in the app's sections (Needs you, Running, Done), live. The open task's transcript streams in the middle, with the request, each tool step as one line that opens to its output or its diff (`✓ Edit src/auth.ts +12 −3`), and the answer's blocks drawn for real at the pane's width. The side pane (`d`) shows the task's changes side by side when there's room, or its files, terminal or setup (`p`). Answer a question with `1`–`9`, a tool approval with `y`, `a` or `n`, and interrupt a turn with Esc. `o` opens the task in its harness ("Open in Claude Code", what v1 called Take over) and brings you back after. The composer grows to 10 lines: Enter sends (queued while a turn runs, shown at once, and put back in the box if wisp refuses it), Alt-Enter adds a line, Shift-Tab cycles plan → edits → auto, `@` mentions a task, file, crew member, machine or My Stuff item, `/` runs any command, a long paste collapses to `[pasted N lines]`, a pasted image path is attached, ↑ walks your history, Ctrl-E opens `$EDITOR`, and each task keeps its own draft. Ctrl-K (or `:`) finds any task, view, command or setting. `g` then `c`, `a`, `m`, `s`, `,` or `h` goes to crew, automations, machines, My Stuff, settings or repo history, each a table with its actions on keys, and `g b` blames a file: every line beside the task that wrote it and what that task was asked. `?` lists every key. The mouse picks rows, opens steps, scrolls, and drags the borders to resize. Under 100 columns it shows one zone at a time, and it works at 80×24.
- **wisp in CI, in a container, and in your commits.** `uses: wisp-agents/action@v1` with a `prompt` (or a declared `task`, or a `formula`) runs it in a GitHub Actions job, waits for it, writes the report to the job summary, posts it on the pull request, and fails the job when the task fails (`fail-on`). The `ci/image` container has wisp, git and gh and runs as a user. `wisp hook install` adds a git hook that links each commit made in a wisp task to it (`Wisp-Task: <id>`), even when wisp isn't running, keeping a hook you already had.
- **Telegram in v2.** Turn on Telegram in Phone settings, paste the bot token and send the link code: questions and finished tasks come to the chat with buttons, a reply to wisp's message answers or follows up the task, and with starting allowed `/new [@project] <task>` starts one (`/projects` lists them). A message sent while wisp was off for more than 10 minutes doesn't start anything, forwards never run, and everything sent is scrubbed of secrets.
- **Updates, the load guard, mentions and what a task ran with.** wisp checks for a newer version once a day (Settings → Updates or `update.check`), on the channel you pick (stable, beta, nightly), and installs it with npm or Homebrew (`update.install`): restart wisp to use it. Automations, crew check-ins and Later wait while free memory is under `machine.memoryFloor`, the CPU over `machine.cpuCeiling`, the runs at `machine.maxRuns`, or the battery under `machine.batteryHold`, and go as soon as there's room. Pause them with `machine.automations.pause`. `@`-mentioning another task in a prompt tells the agent how to read it (or puts its latest report in, for a harness without wisp's tools), and mentioning something in My Stuff gives the agent its path and text. `wisp task setup <id>` shows the harness's version and says "changed since: 1.4.0 → 1.6.0" when a task's harness or MCP server has been updated since it ran. The worktree settings (cleanup after N days, the most folders kept, what Done does with folders, asking before trusting a project) are real settings now (`wisp settings`, formulas).
- **"Check the error above."** In a terminal, `wisp "check the error above"` (or any prompt about what you just ran, or `--context`) attaches what just happened there. That's the terminal's last 200 lines (`--lines N`) from tmux, WezTerm, kitty, iTerm2 or Terminal, read with the terminal's own command and never by running yours again, plus your last commands with their exit codes, the folder, and the git branch and status. Secrets in it are masked before it leaves, and one line says what was attached (`--show-context` shows it, `--no-context` leaves it out, and a setting turns it off for good). `eval "$(wisp shell-init zsh)"` (or bash, fish) records each command's exit code and time in a private file on this machine for 7 days, which also lets a trigger start on `terminal.failed`, when a command fails in a tracked shell.
- **wisp Grid: sign in with GitHub, Google or your company's OpenID provider, boxes as containers, sandboxes and teams from the terminal.** A grid with `auth.providers` signs people in at `/signin`. Each sign-in carries state and PKCE, an ID token's signature, issuer, audience, nonce and expiry are checked, an account is never matched by email, and `auth.allow` (accounts, verified emails or domains, GitHub orgs) and `auth.signup: closed` decide who gets in. `wisp-grid serve` runs a real grid from `grid.json`. With `hostd.driver: "container"`, each box is a container run by `wisp-grid-boxd`, the only part that runs as root: gVisor, no capabilities, its secrets in a 0600 file, its home in boxd's own folder, and a grid that was broken into can't ask for more than a box. `wisp sandbox new [name]` makes a box of its own on the grid for one task, kept as a machine with its own token, and `wisp sandbox ls` and `rm` list and delete them. `wisp team ls`, `new`, `show`, `add` and `rm` manage team spaces.
- **Triggers: one engine, any event, and crew members that wake on them.** A trigger now starts on any fact wisp records (`on: task.finished`, `task.failed`, `task.held`, `task.asked`, `task.done`, `goal.met`, `goal.failed`, `crew.reported`, `pr.opened`, `pr.merged`, `machines.online`, …) or on a source: `schedule` (in its time zone, once per minute across restarts and daylight-saving changes, and once, not once per missed run, after a computer slept), `once`, `ci.failed`/`ci.passed` (GitHub, through `gh`), `issue.opened`/`issue.labeled`, `files.changed` in a project (never inside wisp's own worktrees, never what `.gitignore` ignores), `chat.message`, `webhook` (at most 1 MB, its secret compared in constant time) and `manual`. It can start a task, wake a crew member, run a workflow or follow up the task the event came from. Shape when it goes with `match`, `if` (the workflows expression), `when` (days and hours, skip or queue outside them), `debounce` (ten pushes, one job), `concurrency` (skip, queue, replace, parallel:N), `rate` (held with a reason, never dropped), a daily `budget`, `cooldown` and `approval` (never asked in Full auto). An event is handled once, even after a restart; a trigger never fires on its own tasks, and a chain of triggers stops 3 deep. Three failures in a row pause it and tell you. `wisp trigger add <name> <on> [prompt] [--cron …] [--match k=v] [--rate 5/h] …`, `wisp trigger ls`, `rm`, `fire <name> [--payload @file]` (from a GitHub Action step too), `test <name>` (a dry run against its source's last events: nothing starts), `log <name>`, `resume <name>`, `approve <name> <job>`, `wisp trigger sources`. Crew members get `wakes:` (a member's report can wake another: `on: crew.reported`, `match: {member: ada}`), a loop of those is refused when you apply it, `wisp crew wake <member> --why …` and `wisp crew graph` show who wakes whom, and `@member` in a message from someone your channel lets in wakes that member. A member that answers "nothing to do" counts as skipped, not failed. v1's triggers and a member's `on: [finished, done, ci]` import as the new sources.
- **Events that survive a restart.** Facts like a finished turn, a question, a held turn, an opened or merged PR, a review, a reminder that fired and a crew job filed now go into an event log kept for 30 days. wisp and plugins that react to them pick up where they stopped after a restart or a crash, and lose nothing; a fact that repeats within a day is recorded once. `wisp events tail [--type pr.*] [--subject <task>] [--follow]` shows them, `wisp events why <seq>` shows what caused one, and `wisp events retry <seq>` delivers again one a plugin gave up on. Agents read them with the `wisp_events` MCP tool, and a task's agent sees only its own task's events. The `events.sinks` setting forwards matching events to a webhook, signed with HMAC-SHA256 the way GitHub signs; a failing webhook is retried with backoff, then paused until you resume it. Also fixed: an MCP server probed while wisp was busy could be recorded as not answering, because its output was cut off after 300 ms; a probe that times out now says "timed out after 5 s".
- **wisp Cloud: sign in, share, and reach your machines.** `wisp cloud signin [grid]` signs in to a wisp Grid with a code you approve in your browser; your cloud box shows up as a machine. `wisp share agent.reviewer --to cloud` shares a setup (with what it references), and `wisp share <task> --to cloud` shares a task, as a link with a page anyone can read without wisp. Set who opens it with `--access anyone|people:a,b|teams:t|password`, add `--expires 7d`, and put it in the gallery with `--publish`. Sharing again makes a new version at the same link. `wisp open <link>` shows a setup's plan and applies it; `wisp shares` lists your links with their views and imports, `--updates` tells you what you imported has a newer version, and `--revoke` stops a link. A module can come from the grid: `source: grid://ada/review?ref=v1`, pinned in `.wisp/lock.yml`. `wisp computer on` makes this computer reachable from your other wisps through the grid, with nothing to open on your router; pages on it show only if you turn on "Show local pages in previews". `wisp --on <machine> …` runs any command on another machine, and a cloud box asleep stays asleep until a command needs it. Every MCP tool takes a `peer`. `wisp grid deploy -f <file>` applies a formula on your cloud box. Readers for sessions started outside wisp in Cursor, opencode, Grok and Antigravity join Claude Code's and Codex's, so `wisp adopt` takes them in too. `wisp-grid dev` runs a grid on your own machine.
- **History, finished.** A repo's history now lives in its own plugin, `wisp.history`. Transcripts can go to the working tree, to git notes (`history: { transcripts: notes }`, so the tree stays clean) or to Git LFS, and a plugin can store them anywhere. `wisp mv` renames in wisp too, without destroying and recreating anything. `wisp plan` marks drift (what changed in wisp since the last apply) apart from changes in your files, and `--refresh-only` takes wisp's copy as the baseline. A variable with no value is asked for in a terminal. The same outside session is never adopted twice: adopting again only adds its new turns. `wisp blame <task>` says how much of what it wrote is still there, and tracking records its PR from GitHub. In `track: ask` mode a finished task offers **Save to repo**. An agent's `wisp_resume` is now a request you approve. With `history: { inject: true }`, a new task starts knowing the earlier tasks on the files its prompt names. `wisp open <task> [--in codex]` (v1's Take over) resumes its session in the agent's CLI: it restores the native session log for a task from the repo, starts a primed continuation in another harness, and brings what you did there back as the task's next turns. `wisp task setup <id>` shows what a task had (its skills, including ones the harness found by itself, by hash; its MCP servers with their versions and tools) and what it used. Every wisp command is also an MCP tool that agents can call, within their permissions.
- **Your setup is a folder, and your repo remembers the agent work that built it.** wisp reads every `*.wisp.yml` in `.wisp/` as one setup. Resources refer to each other (`agent: ${agent.reviewer}`, `${machine.studio.address}`), and wisp applies them in order and refuses a cycle, naming it. Use `variables` (set them with `-var`, var files, `WISP_VAR_…` or a workspace), `locals` and `outputs`, and make several resources from one with `count` and `for_each`. Small pure functions (`lower`, `join`, `file`, `env`, `secret`…) and conditionals work in any field. Modules can come from a path or from git, fetched and pinned in `.wisp/lock.yml` by `wisp init`. New commands: `wisp plan` (it shows drift from what's set up), `destroy`, `output`, `graph`, `fmt`, `mv agent.a agent.b` (references follow), `import agent.x` (what you made in the app, into the files) and `workspace`. `wisp export -o dir/` writes one file per kind, and `wisp export crew.ada --with-deps` takes what Ada needs. `wisp init` sets up `.wisp/` and a note in AGENTS.md. `wisp track <id>` saves a task into the repo: its spec, its report and its full transcript (scrubbed, compressed). With `history: { track: auto }`, done tasks are saved by themselves. Ship it's commits carry `Wisp-Task: <id>`, so `wisp blame` answers for a line, a file, a folder, a commit or range, a PR or a task. Also new: `wisp log`, `wisp tasks --touching auth/`, `wisp ls --repo`, `wisp resume <id>` and `wisp untrack`. `wisp mcp` gives any agent `wisp_history`, `wisp_blame`, `wisp_task` and `wisp_resume` without wisp running, and runs wisp starts in such a repo get it by themselves. `wisp adopt` turns your Claude Code and Codex sessions in this folder into wisp tasks; a follow-up continues the same session there, and `--track` saves them. `wisp skill install --agent claude` adds the MCP server and a SessionEnd hook that adopts sessions as they end (`history: { adopt: ask | auto | off }`).
- **Notifications, goals, your other computers and API keys.** A `channel` with `transport: ntfy` (off until `enabled: true`) gets a push for what its `notification`s ask for: `finished`, `failed`, `asks`, to a channel or any webhook URL. A tap on its Done button marks the task done, and only taps wisp signed count. A task with a `goal` keeps going until its judges say MET: they're the goal's own judges, or else the `goal` defaults. They vote (majority, all or any) and say what's missing for the next round. Past `maxRounds` it stops and asks you. A `machine` with a transport (`ssh`, `loopback`) becomes a peer that wisp reaches over the sync protocol: its tasks show here, and you can start work there. A `capability` with a `key` is an API key: a request with `x-wisp-key` can do what that set allows and nothing else. A `trigger` with `on: webhook` starts its task from `POST /api/triggers/<name>` carrying its secret, with the body passed to the task. A `pipeline` can carry on on another harness at a usage limit (`atLimit: next`) or run `bestOf` N with a `judge` picking the best. A `harness` with `from: npm` or `brew` is installed when it isn't there or doesn't match its `pin`. `wisp`'s `runtime.projectRun` runs a project's `run:` scripts in a terminal.
- **What you declare now does what it says.** Applying a formula used to keep most kinds as written and act on few. Now each one changes what wisp does. A **project** gives its tasks their agent and worktree default (a task with a worktree now gets one, made before its first turn), runs its setup in each fresh worktree (a failing setup holds the first turn and says why: Start now runs it anyway), puts its instructions in the system prompt, and a declared `trust` without hooks or MCP keeps the repo's own out. A **harness** that's off isn't offered and refuses tasks, a `pin` that doesn't match says so, its model and effort fill what a task leaves out, and `from: path` runs that program. An agent's **skills** and a task's **team** are in its system prompt, **connectors** are its MCP servers, and **tools** narrow wisp's own tools per agent or mode. A **capability** is a set the kernel enforces, and a **plugin** with `enabled: false` is switched off (removing it switches it back on). A **trigger** `on: finished` starts its task. A **pipeline** retries or escalates a failed run and starts a review after a finished one. **Apps** and **panes** land on My Stuff. `wisp run --formula team.yml "task"` runs one task under a formula and keeps nothing of it.
- **Everything set up as code, history you can ask about, tasks you can share.** `wisp apply` is now all or nothing: if a change fails, the ones before it are undone. A formula declares every part of a setup: projects (worktree policy, setup scripts, trust, instructions), harnesses, accounts, teams, skills, machines and their limits, connectors, wisp's tools, capabilities, channels and notifications, recipes, triggers, the goal judges, budget stop lines, pipelines, plugins, apps and panes, and this device's look (`ui`, left out of exports unless `--include ui`). `wisp import` turns your v1 setup into a formula and shows its plan before anything changes. Ask your history what made each task: `wisp ls --agent reviewer`, `--crew Ada`, `--workflow nightly`, `--formula team.yml`, `--since 7d`; `wisp task history <id>` shows its reruns, forks and workflow, `wisp show` says where a task came from, and `wisp history export` writes it all as scrubbed JSONL. Set how long finished tasks stay in the list with `runtime.retentionDays` (they're archived, never deleted). `wisp share <id> --level spec|report|full` writes a signed `.wisp-task` file (or a secret gist), after showing every secret, path and name it takes out. `wisp open` it to run it on your code, rerun it with the sharer's setup, compare both, or import the whole conversation read-only.
- **Your AI dev team, as code.** Put your agents, crew, tasks, workflows and settings in a `wisp.yml` (or JSON or TOML) and `wisp apply -f wisp.yml`. You see the plan first (what's added, changed, removed, field by field) and it asks before anything changes; `--yes` in scripts, `--dry-run`, `--prune`. `wisp export` writes what you have back to a file that applies back as no change, `wisp diff` and `wisp validate` check a file, and `wisp schema` gives your editor completions. Name agents once (`reviewer: codex, high effort, plan mode`) and use the name anywhere. Declared tasks run on their schedule or with `wisp run nightly-deps`. Workflows run tasks in order with conditions, fan-out, retries, timeouts and a budget (`wisp run release-check --wait`). Every task now remembers what was asked, the commit it started from and the setup it ran with: `wisp task spec`, `wisp task export` to share it and `wisp run -f` to run it again on your code, and `wisp rerun <id>` (same code by default, `--from latest`, `--formula new-team.yml` to try a new setup on an old task, several formulas at once, `--last 20`). A formula from a URL that schedules work needs `--trust`.
- **Everything the app does, from your terminal.** `wisp "fix the login"` now stays with the task: you see each step as it runs, then the answer drawn for the terminal (stats, files, diffs, tests, checklists, trees, charts and links you can click), never JSON. When the agent asks something or needs an approval, type a number to answer; a reply box after the answer sends a follow-up to the same task. Ctrl-C leaves it running (`wisp attach <id>` picks it up again) and a second Ctrl-C stops it. `-d` returns at once, outside a terminal that's the default, and the next `wisp` command tells you when it finished, asks or failed. Every command of every plugin is there too, as `wisp <plugin> <verb>` with flags from its arguments (`wisp settings set theme dark`, `wisp crew create --name Ada`), `--json` for scripts, `wisp help`, `wisp help <plugin>`, `wisp completions zsh|bash|fish`, `wisp ls --watch` and `wisp show <id> --watch`. `NO_COLOR` and pipes get plain text.
- **v2 looks like wisp today, and answers every press at once.** The v2 app's frame (the crew rail, the list and its head, Home's prompt box, an open task with its details, overlaps and reply box) is drawn from v1's own values at every width from phone to desktop, and in the Mac app's title-bar mode, checked against screenshots of v1 by `qa/parity.mjs`. Every control in those screens now does something you see in the same frame: picking a mode, agent, when to start or who to delegate to changes its label, Worktree and Goal flip, a new task and a follow-up show at once (*Starting…*), Rerun and Fork open their new task straight away, Ship it and Restore show their button working, and a refused action rolls back with a note saying why (`qa/feedback.mjs`). Plugins' top-bar, row and task-head extensions show in the new frame again.

### Changed

- **wisp starts without waiting on your agents' CLIs**: a slow or stuck CLI no longer holds the server from starting; Settings → Auto's harness list fills in once they've answered.
- **Projects looks as it did**: where you are, the folder field with Add (Clone for a link), your projects with their colour, a name you edit in place and their task count, and the repos found on this machine. On a phone, dialogs stay cards, as before.
- **Start from an issue opens like ⌘K**, near the top and 640 wide, with each issue on two lines and why there's nothing to list above it.
- **Right-clicking a task in the list picks it**, so it shows which task the menu is for.
- **An app pinned beside tasks keeps running when you switch task**: it's told which task is open instead of reloading.
- **Settings look like v1's again.** Agents lists each harness's accounts in a box under it, with GitHub and "Installed something new?" at the end; Machines opens on one row with Manage, then Reach this computer from anywhere and what SSH needs (commands copy with a click); Cloud and Connectors put their sign-in and Found rows in v1's boxes; Worktrees keeps its columns and v1's filter; Updates is at the end of General again.

- **Try it two ways is no longer a button in the prompt box's bar.** It did what **+** already does: with Claude Code picked, add a run with **+** and set it to Claude Code too: its picker has the *What's different* group, Subagents and Skills, as before; Compare still says what differed. Spells that name `launcher.tryTwo` still load, with a warning.
- **Stop asks first, for an agent and a subagent alike.** Stop on a working task, a subagent's run, the goal check, *Waiting on …* or a row of Activity → Using (Stop, and Later once you pick a time) opens a small confirm by the button (**Stop** / **Stop subagent**, or Cancel). It says what else happens: the agent's running subagents stop too, a stopped subagent sends its lead no report, and queued follow-ups stay held.

### Fixed

- **A new question brings an archived task back** to the list at once.
- **Compare runs keep the prompt's title**; one run's answer no longer renames the task.

- **Rerun from a task's menu made a new task again**: it had stopped working against a real wisp.
- **What you're typing survives a reload or an update**, in New task and in a reply. Text shared to wisp lands in New task again.
- **Quitting the Windows app stops wisp's server.** Closing the window quits, and the server can no longer outlive the app.
- **Updating the Windows app from v1 removes v1's leftovers.** Its bundled Node and old server files are deleted on install; your tasks and settings in `~/.wisp` stay.
- **Quitting the desktop app stops wisp's server again** (on macOS and Windows it could stay up): a plugin switching off while the list was being read could lock the server's plugin registry.
- Installing a plugin from `~/code/my-plugin` (as the box suggests) works: `~` is your home folder. The same for a project added as `~/…`.
- The backdrop behind a dialog is as light in dark mode as in light mode, as before.

- **Settings on a phone match v1.** Usage's four tiles stay in one row; Auto shows its intro, which harnesses it may use as chips with their logos, and what it learned in v1's words; Worktrees' rows, Keep and Remove buttons and filter are v1's size; Machines puts the grid address on its own line when Sign in and add it doesn't fit beside it; Connectors keeps Not now · Review on the right; Plugins' suggestions and Notifications' channel example are spaced and set as v1's. Buttons draw v1's 16 px icons, and ghost buttons are v1's weight and padding.
- The *Ask before a repo’s hooks and setup run* setting's name fits on a phone instead of being cut off.

- **A doc block's Read all stays under your finger on a phone.** Pressing it shrank the button and dropped its centring, so it jumped right by half its width as you tapped.
- On a phone, a setting's controls that don't fit beside its text go under it (Agents: Add key, Signed in · Sign out no longer crowd the row).
- Switches in Settings are buttons again, so keyboard and screen readers treat them as v1 did.

- A goal isn't checked while Claude Code's background subagents are still working: the turn waits for their reports.
- An opened step shows its whole title, not the label cut for the row, for every harness.
- A task's transcript loads from the topic the server actually names (`session:<id>`).
- `wisp add .` stores the project's folder itself ("/repo", not "/repo/."), and a Windows path without its `\\?\` prefix, whoever adds it.
- A picture or video loading in a task no longer moves the page: at the end you stay at the end, elsewhere what you were reading stays put (the Mac app too).
- No Allow & retry after a turn that already ran in Full auto: what its harness still refused can't be allowed, and the card says so.
- A goal isn't checked while its agent waits for the wake-up it set itself: it's checked after that turn, now if you cancel the wake-up, and it pauses if the wake-up never goes.
- Stop in This Mac → What's using it asks first, and says what stops.
- An opened step in a task's transcript shows its whole title.
- The crew page's details sit beside the thread on a wide window again; My Stuff and Settings line up with v1 (the head bar, the search, the empty shelf; the nav's spacing, fields on white).
- Settings no longer shows "unknown command terminal.apps": the terminal apps on this computer are listed, and `npm run check` now catches a command the UI calls that no plugin handles.
- Menus and selects inside a dialog open in front of it.
- Discord calls wait their turn per channel when Discord says to slow down, instead of failing.
- Slack answers one message at a time per thread (two quick messages, or a click right after one, no longer race), while other threads go on side by side.
- Leaving a subagent's page goes back to its lead's task.
- **Settings kept after a reload.** Your settings were saved but every pane read them from a place the server never filled, so after a reload Settings showed the defaults. They now read back as you left them, including what another plugin pins.
- **wisp serve started without its tasks.** One setting's choices listed an empty value, so the task runner and every harness were refused at start.
- **Menus and pickers inside Settings and dialogs** opened behind them.
- **Goal on, Done when empty.** Send now stops, puts the caret in Done when and offers Send without a goal, instead of starting a task that doesn't keep going. A goal with no prompt says Say what to do first.
- **Settings switches that were refused.** Accept tasks from channels, wisp blocks, Auto-fix PR conflicts, Watch agents work and Interactive answers saved `on`/`off` into settings that take true or false, so every click was rolled back.
- Toasts sat half off the left edge of the window; they're bottom centre again.
- On a phone every task's state pill was green, Failed included.
- Scheduled tasks and reminders never showed in the list: it read the wrong rows.
- Opening the side panel rebuilt the task page: open steps closed and the focus jumped to the reply box.
- Files picked for a reply or a new task were dropped when it was sent.
- Ship it never loaded: its manifest listed two commands twice, and wisp refuses such a plugin at start. A check now validates every built-in plugin's manifest.
- Reloading a plugin's page (`/view/stuff.page`, any view id with a dot) showed "not found".
- Later's actions (add, edit, snooze, cancel, plans) and Share's preview sent the task's key where wisp reads its id, so they did nothing.
- **An opened step in a task's activity shows its whole title.** A long step ("Running a Python script, then searching for …") stayed cut after you opened it, both on screen and in the title wisp saved; now an opened step shows its full title, wrapped. Steps from before this fix stay cut.
- **Goal on with an empty Done when no longer sends without asking.** Turning on Goal in New task or a task's reply box and pressing Send with Done when left empty used to start the task (or send the message) without a goal, so it stopped after one round. Now nothing goes: Done when gets the focus, your text stays, and a toast says the goal is empty, with **Send without a goal** to send it anyway. With Done when filled but nothing in New task's box, Send says *Say what to do first* instead of doing nothing.
- **A goal is checked only once its agent is done, not while it still waits.** With Claude Code's own subagents still at work, a turn that ended with a full report had its goal checked at once (and the subagents cut off a few seconds later); now the turn waits for them and the goal is checked on the report that follows. A turn whose agent scheduled its own wake-up (`resume_later`) isn't checked either: the goal stays on and is checked after that wake-up's turn. Cancel the wake-up and the goal is checked at once; mark the task done (or let the wake-up lapse) and the goal pauses for you.
- **The phone's Back from a subagent's run goes back to its lead.** In the Android app, Back from a delegate's run (or a crew member's job opened from a task) opened the lead and then closed it in the same press, landing on the task list. One Back now opens the task it came from, and the next one goes to the list, as the Back button in the task's head already did.
- **A task that delegated a lot opens fast on the phone again.** The phone already fetched only a task's latest turns, but it still got every subagent's whole run with them: a lead with 48 subagents sent 46 MB through the tunnel. Now it gets only the subagents its shown turns delegated to, each with just the turn its card shows (3.5 MB for that task). Opening a subagent shows that turn at once and loads the rest of its run.
- **A picture loading in a task no longer moves the page.** Pictures and videos in an answer load once they're near, with no room kept for them, so at the end of a task each one pushed the end off screen (by up to its height) as it arrived. The view now stays at the end, before the page paints; read higher up, it holds what you're reading.
- **No more Allow & retry that can't allow anything.** In Full auto, Claude Code still refuses a few commands with its own safety check (a heredoc it can't parse: “could not be checked for dangerous rm operations”), and no rule lifts that. The *Blocked by permissions* card offered Allow & retry for them anyway, which did nothing useful. It now lists what was refused with a note that wisp can't allow it, and no buttons.
- **A subagent's title on a phone is one line, like its lead's beside it.** Opening a subagent's task on a phone, the lead's title in the head was cut short on one line while the subagent's own title wrapped to two; now both are one line, cut short.

### Removed

- **v1's routes, events and spells are gone from the server (ADR-0031).** `/api/events` is the sync stream only (it takes the sync hello), `/api/spells*`, the v1 route adapters with a v2 command, v1's event names, the JSON files written next to the store and the v1 relay are removed. The CLI uses v2's commands (`wisp usage`, `wisp spell` and `wisp computer` are gone; `--try` points to `--compare` and `wisp rerun --formula`). `wisp import` still brings a v1 home over, once.
- **Drag projects into your own order.** Drag a project chip in the top bar or New task's *in* row, a row of the searchable project list (+N, with nothing typed), or a row of Projects (Manage projects) to put it where you want it. The order is one: it shows the same everywhere projects are listed, including the command palette and a crew member's *Works in*. A project added afterwards goes first. A project's right-click menu has **Sort by recent use** to go back. On a phone, hold a chip before dragging it. Spells can turn dragging off per place (`movable` on `topbar.projects`, `launcher.projects`, `project.list`, `project.rows`).

## [0.58.0] - 2026-11-11

### Added

- **The task list tints to the crew member you pick.** With the crew rail, picking someone tints the task panel a very light wash of their colour, so the list beside the rail says whose jobs it holds. Each member’s editor now has **Task panel**: their glow (the default), none, or any of the eight crew colours. Settings → Appearance → **Crew tint** turns it off for the whole page, and spells can set `crewTint`.
- **What the harnesses keep on disk, and fixes for what runs leave behind.** `GET /api/machine` now measures every installed harness's own folders (Claude Code and Cowork's VM, Codex, Cursor, opencode, Antigravity, Copilot, Grok) with `du` alone, never opening a file. One click (`POST /api/machine/harness/clean`) removes only crash dumps and Claude Code's stale temp files; everything else says how that CLI cleans up after itself. Processes runs left behind are grouped by the command that started them and the task, and **File a fix** (`POST /api/machine/fix`) gives Bugsy, or a task in that project, the command lines and "kill what you spawn". On a Mac, each process's memory is now what Activity Monitor shows (its footprint), not RSS, which read about a quarter low.
- **See what agents cost your computer, and pause automations.** `GET /api/machine` reports each running task's memory and CPU (summed over every process it started), per crew member too, the processes finished runs left running (a test server, a dev server, a watcher) with Stop (`POST /api/machine/stop`), 24 h of memory, CPU and run history, and the worktree folders that can go, grouped by merged PRs, Done tasks, finished crew jobs and leftovers. **Pause automations** until a time (`POST /api/automations/pause`), and on a Mac on battery under 20% (`batteryHold`: off, 20 or 40) check-ins and the crew queue wait for the charger. A heavy task's card data now carries its memory and CPU. The This Mac pane that shows it comes next.
- **This Mac: see what your agents cost your computer, and act on it.** Settings → This Mac (This PC on Windows, This computer on Linux), or ⌘K → *What’s using my computer*, shows free memory, CPU and disk with the last hour, then each running task and crew member by RAM, CPU and folder size, with **Stop** and **Later** (in an hour, tonight or tomorrow morning: it stops and carries on then). Processes still running after their task finished can be stopped one by one or all at once, folders wisp can give back are grouped (merged PRs, Done tasks, finished crew jobs, folders no task knows) with their size and **Remove**, and **Pause automations** holds check-ins, triggers and goal rounds for an hour or until tomorrow 8:00. A chip in the top bar appears only when memory runs low, work is waiting, automations are paused or something was left behind (*12% memory · 9 running*), and a running task that's heavy shows its RAM on its row. Spells reach it through the `machine.head` slot and the `machine.*` parts.
- **Automations wait while your computer is short.** Check-ins, triggers, goal rounds and agents' own follow-ups now wait while free memory is under 15%, the CPU stays over 200% of its cores for a minute, or wisp already has a run per 3 GB of memory going (8 on a 24 GB Mac). They go on once memory is 5 points above the floor, the load is down and a slot is free, and their row says why (*Waiting for memory: 11% free, needs 20%*). What you start always runs; when memory is short the page says so. Subagents count but never wait. Settings → Usage → Machine load has the three knobs, and spells can set them (`memoryFloor`, `cpuCeiling`, `maxRuns`).
- **Done asks about the worktree.** Marking a task done in the page used to leave its folder on disk, with its `node_modules` and build output, and those of its subagents, delegated crew jobs and compare runs: gigabytes each, until the cleanup two weeks later. Now Mark done (the button, `E`, a row's ✓) opens a small dialog when any of them hold a folder, listing each with its size and state (clean, uncommitted files, commits not pushed, PR open or merged). **Remove** takes the clean ones; one holding uncommitted work goes only if you tick it. **Keep** keeps them all. *Always do this* remembers the answer in Settings → Worktrees → *When a task is done* (Ask, Remove clean ones, Keep them; spells can set `worktreeOnDone`), and even Remove asks before a folder with work in it goes. Done from anywhere else (a channel, `wisp_task`, Ship it) asks nothing and removes only clean folders with no open PR. A crew job that ends clean with no open PR loses its folder right away. Branches always stay, and a follow-up brings a folder back.
- **See when a banked Codex reset expires.** When wisp reads a Codex account's usage (Check Codex while work waits, or the read before held-back work), Settings → Usage → Plan budget now shows its banked resets under the bars: *1 banked reset, expires tomorrow 17:00*, or just the count when Codex doesn't list them. The day before one expires your phone gets one note for it, when ntfy or Telegram is set up. wisp only reads: it never redeems a reset. Spells can hide or restyle the line (`budget.banked`).
- **`on laptop` from Telegram, on wisp Cloud.** Tell a bot `on laptop fix the login` and it runs on that computer, even with your cloud wisp asleep. Off unless the grid has `channels.route` in `grid.json`. Make a new bot for this and connect it on your account page (*Telegram to your computers*): the grid holds its webhook with a secret of its own, and only the people you list can route. Not your cloud wisp's bot: anything on your cloud wisp can read that token, and with it what goes through here, so the page warns if it's the same one. `on <computer>` has to come first; anything else to that bot gets a line saying what it does. A computer takes these only once you turn on Settings → Notifications → *Accept tasks from channels* on it, off by default, and turning it on is refused from a phone: Plan unless the message asks, Edits at most, never Full auto, on that computer's own projects. Asleep or switched off, you get a plain line back and nothing waits. Reports and replies come back in the same chat, through the grid: the computer never holds the bot. A spell can force the switch off, never on.
- **Pick element tells the agent what it's looking at.** Clicking an element in Preview now puts its trimmed HTML and key computed styles (size, spacing, colour, font) in the reply box as plain text, with its selector and page, not just a line naming it. One pick stays under 2,000 characters, whatever the page holds, and leaves out what's in password and hidden fields. Nothing sends until you do. The cursor is a crosshair everywhere while you pick, also over the app's buttons and links, and only a pick you made can reach the box: a page can't write in it by itself.
- **Share to wisp from your phone.** A link, text, pictures or a PDF from any app's share sheet used to need copying across by hand. Now the Android app, and a Home Screen install of your cloud wisp on Android, open New task with it in the box and attached, ready to send (below whatever you'd typed, 10 files and 20 MB at most). Nothing starts and no file leaves your phone until you press Send, and a line marks it as shared from outside wisp so you read it first. On an iPhone, a guide shows how to build a Shortcut that starts a task on your cloud wisp with an API key.
- **Discord's `/wisp` works on a sleeping cloud wisp.** On a grid with `channels` in `grid.json`, paste the bot token and public key in the Phone dialog and copy the Endpoint URL it shows into the Developer Portal. wisp Grid checks each call's signature and answers Discord in time, the box wakes and does the work, and the reply lands in place. Mentions need a connection that stays open, so on a cloud computer only `/wisp` works, in threads too. Someone in a server who isn't linked gets a line only they can see, and it wakes nothing. On a cloud computer a message from any channel stays there: it never starts or follows up on a task on another machine. Message Content stays off.
- **Discord, two-way.** Turn on Discord in the Phone dialog (Settings → Notifications → Phone, or ⌘K, off by default): it walks you through creating your bot in the Developer Portal, pasting its token, adding it to a server and linking yourself, then shows that it's connected, in how many servers and who's linked, or what's wrong and what to do. Or give `PUT /api/remote` its token (`discord: { enabled, token }`) and add it with the link from `GET /api/remote/discord/invite`. Then mention it, DM it or use `/wisp`, whose options fill in from your own projects, crew, machines and models. A task starts and answers in a thread of its own, or in reply in a DM. Questions come with buttons, and `stop`, `done`, `status`, `btw` and `@ada` work there. People link with `link <code>` in a DM, by their Discord user id; anyone else gets one line in a DM and silence in a server. Tasks run in Plan unless you say Edits, Full auto is refused, tool approvals stay in wisp and the thread says what's waiting there, nothing posted pings anyone or hides a link, a Discord token is scrubbed like any other secret, and in a server lists and refusals show none of your names. It needs no privileged intent, so in a thread you mention the bot or use `/wisp`. It won't turn on on a cloud computer, which sleeps.
- **Mail a task to your cloud wisp.** On a grid with `mail` in `grid.json`, Settings → Notifications → Phone → Email gives you a secret address and asks which address you'll send from. A message from there wakes the box and starts a task from its subject and first lines, said as you'd type it in wisp, and the answer comes back as a reply in the same thread; replying follows up. Plan by default, Edits at most, and Full auto can't be asked for. Only your secret address, your one allowed address and a DKIM signature wisp verified itself, by your address's own domain, count together: anything it can't prove gets no answer, and Settings says why the last message was ignored. Your provider has to sign with the same domain as your address: consumer Gmail and iCloud do, while a company domain signed by its provider's domain won't pass, and neither will a subdomain signed by its parent. Quoted text and signatures never run, attachments aren't read yet, and tool approvals stay in wisp. Off by default. See `grid/README.md` for the SES and DNS setup.
- **Telegram reaches a sleeping cloud wisp.** On a grid with `channels` in `grid.json`, a box takes a webhook to its own `/_in/<token>` URL instead of polling. The grid answers Telegram at once, holds the message, wakes the box and delivers it once, in order. A channel never keeps a box awake. Messages that can't be delivered within the hour are dropped, and the box says so in the chat. Strangers messaging the bot can't push other people's boxes out: after three wakes with nothing for wisp, a wake waits its turn behind people, at most one every 10 minutes (opening wisp still delivers at once). If the URL leaks, `POST /api/channels/rotate` replaces it. A computer's Telegram keeps polling, and with `channels` off nothing changes.
- **Slack reaches a sleeping cloud wisp.** On a grid with `channels` in `grid.json`, turn on Slack in the Phone dialog on a cloud computer: the manifest carries your wisp's own private URL, and setup takes the signing secret and the bot token, with no app token. Slack posts its events to that URL, the grid answers within Slack's 3 seconds, holds the event and wakes the box, and the box checks Slack's signature and handles it once, so a message sent while wisp sleeps still runs, up to an hour later. Keep wisp open while Slack checks the URL: asleep, it answers 503 and Slack lets you retry. A leaked URL can be replaced with `POST /api/channels/rotate`, which gives you the new one for the app's Event Subscriptions. A computer keeps Socket Mode, unchanged.
- **Buttons for questions in Slack.** A question in a Slack thread shows a button per choice, and a click answers it: the buttons give way to what was chosen and who. Only people linked to wisp can click, an old button tells the clicker alone that it's stale and never answers another question, and a tool approval still has no buttons. An app made before this needs *Interactivity & Shortcuts* turned on in its settings; typing the answer works either way. On a cloud computer there are no buttons yet: Slack would need a second URL for clicks, so you type the answer.
- **Crew jobs that need you are in Needs you.** A job run for another task (delegated, an `@` answer, a check on a task) that asks you something or has an unread report now sits in the task list's Needs you with your own tasks, with its member's face. It used to show only on home. It leaves the list under its member meanwhile, and the project chips count exactly what Needs you holds.
- **Slack, two-way.** Turn on Slack in the Phone dialog (Settings → Notifications → Phone, or ⌘K, off by default): it walks you through creating your own Slack app from wisp's manifest, pasting its two tokens and linking yourself, then shows the workspace, the bot, how many people are linked, and what's wrong with the way to fix it when it isn't working. Then `@wisp in api with opus plan: fix it` in a channel, or a DM to the bot, starts a task and answers in a thread. Follow up, answer questions, `stop`, `done`, `status` and `btw` there, and `@ada` reaches your crew. People link with `link <code>` in a DM, by their Slack user id; anyone else gets one line in a DM and silence in a channel. Tasks run in Plan unless you say Edits, Full auto is refused, tool approvals stay in wisp, and in a channel lists and refusals show none of your names. On a computer it uses Socket Mode, so nothing has to reach it. Spells reach the section through the `phone.slack` part.
- **⌃Tab switches tasks in the desktop app.** A tap jumps back to the task you had open before, so two tasks are one key apart. Hold Ctrl and press Tab to step through your latest tasks (⇧Tab goes back, Esc stays put) and let go to open one. If you were in the reply box, you land in the new task's. A browser tab can't have it: Chrome keeps ⌃Tab for its own tabs.
- **Start a task from a GitHub comment.** Turn on GitHub in the Phone dialog (Settings → Notifications → Phone, or ⌘K, off by default; `github: { enabled: true }` on `PUT /api/remote`) and a comment of yours whose first line is `/wisp …`, on an issue or PR of a repo one of your projects has as its origin, starts a task, or follows up on the one already there, and the answer comes back as a comment through `gh`, as you. It reads like the launcher: `/wisp in api with opus edits: fix it`, `/wisp @ada look at this`, `/wisp stop`, `/wisp new …`. Only your own comments count, by account id; anyone else gets silence. Tasks run in Plan unless you say Edits, Full auto is refused, and tool approvals stay in wisp. On a public repo a report is one fixed line with nothing an agent wrote; *Full reports on public repos* turns full reports on there. The section says at a glance whether it's working: who `gh` is signed in as, how many repos it watches, and what's wrong with the way to fix it when it isn't. It runs only while the computer is awake. Spells reach it through the `phone.github` part.
- **Say what you'd type in wisp, from Telegram.** With the new *Say what you’d type in wisp* switch on (Phone → Telegram, under *Start tasks from Telegram*, off by default) a Telegram message reads like the launcher: `@ada in api on laptop with opus high plan: fix the flaky login test`. wisp replies first with what it chose, tells you the valid choices when something's ambiguous or not installed, and `stop`, `done`, `status`, `/btw` and `with sonnet` work in a task's thread. Crew members take jobs this way and answer in the thread that started them, queued jobs included, across a restart. Plain text starts a task, a group chat is capped at Edits, and with the switch on what leaves wisp has its secrets and @mentions taken out. Telegram and ntfy now run on a shared channel layer; with the switch off they behave exactly as before.
- **Sign in from the task that needs it.** A run that fails because its harness is signed out used to end on *Claude Code failed*, and you had to leave the task to log in. Now the turn says *Claude Code is signed out* with the sign-in right there, and once you're in wisp sends your request again, once, in the same conversation. If the CLI says it's signed in after all, the turn says that instead and offers **Try again**. Works for every harness wisp can sign in, on machines too; `/login` in the reply box does the same.
- **Right-click a project to edit or remove it.** It used to work only on the chips at the top of the sidebar, and only sent you to Manage projects. Right-click a project there, in New task's *in* row or in the list behind *+N*: **Edit project…** changes its name and colour in place, **Remove project** takes it off wisp and keeps its tasks and files. Both show at once and come back with a toast if the server refuses.
- **A task says what changed in the CLI between turns.** wisp now records on each turn what the CLI was: its version, its tool set and, for Codex, a hash of its model catalog. When one of them moves from one turn to the next on the same harness and account, the finished turn says so in one line under its prompt (*Since the last turn: Claude Code 2.1.316 → 2.1.318, WebFetch added.*), so a run that behaves differently has a reason to look at. They're possible causes, not proof, and the line says so: a server-side change (a model updated behind the same name) won't show. Spells: the `turn.whatChanged` part. API: `stats.cli` on turns (`version`, `tools`, `catalog`, `account`, each only when known).
- **Panes: pin a My Stuff app beside your tasks or on home.** An app's menu in My Stuff has **Pin beside tasks** (a tab in the side panel next to Changes, PR and Shell, told which task is open) and **Pin on home** (a whole-workspace tool under Needs you, such as a board of your tasks). An app can now use wisp itself: its `wisp-app.json` lists what it needs in `"uses"` (`tasks:read`, `tasks:write`, `prs:read`, `crew:read`, `usage:read`), you allow it once when you pin it, and its page calls `wisp.tasks.list()`, `wisp.tasks.get(id)`, `wisp.on('tasks', fn)` (live), `wisp.tasks.launch({ prompt, project })`, `wisp.tasks.followUp(id, text)`, `wisp.tasks.done(id)`, `wisp.prs.list()`, `wisp.crew.list()` and `wisp.usage.accounts()` after loading `/_wisp/pane.js`. wisp checks every call against what you allowed, answers with each task's title, state, branch, PR and the last answer's one-line summary (never the conversation), lets a pane start or change at most 6 tasks a minute, and serves an app that uses wisp with a policy that keeps its requests on its own address. `tasks:write` lets a pane ask: every task it starts and every follow-up it sends waits on wisp's own card, and only your click on Send sends it. Starting tasks uses your own defaults: a pane can't pick the harness or the mode. The card asking for access only opens from your own click, never while the task it's for, or the task that made the app, runs in Full auto. Take it back from the app's menu (**Take back access**). A spell can pin panes (`settings.panes: "Task board@home"`) but never allow them anything. API: `uses` and `allowed` on an app, `POST`/`DELETE /api/stuff/:id/allow`, the `panes` setting.
- **Compare shows honest numbers for each run.** Compare now totals every turn of a run instead of showing its last one, and adds tokens, cache, Agent steps (the tools it used, counted the same on every harness) and plan share in one table, which updates in place as runs finish. When runs overlap on one account wisp can only trace what they took together, so each share is an estimate marked ≈, split by cost, beside the total. Under the table one sentence sets each run against the first in plain numbers, and never says which is better. Spells can restyle or hide the table and the sentence (`compare.table`, `compare.note`).
- **A quiet run says how long it's been quiet.** After 12 minutes with nothing new, the working panel says *No output for 12 min* and keeps counting (*No output for 1 h 5 min*). It clears on the next activity and changes nothing else, and it stays away while a delegate or subagent is working or the task is asking you something. A spell can restyle or hide it (`turn.noOutput`).
- **Read what each judge did, like a task.** Click a judge's row in the goal band's votes, or the check line under *Done when*, and each goal check opens as a page: which judge, which round, its vote and why, the prompt wisp gave it, its messages and commands, and what it saw. Older rounds are there too, under *Earlier rounds*, and a goal without judges has its own check to read the same way. A judge that timed out or answered without a vote keeps what it did, and one still checking shows live. *Goal checks…* is in the task's menus and ⌘K too. A goal keeps these for its last 50 checks; they go when you delete the task or drop the goal.
- **API keys for your cloud wisp.** Script it with `Authorization: Bearer wgk_…` at `https://<handle>.<domain>/api/…`, no browser sign-in needed. Settings → Cloud → **API keys** (Connect, in a cloud computer) makes one, shows it once, and lists each key's name, last four characters, and when it was made and last used, with **Revoke**. A key opens that one wisp and nothing else: not your computers, sandboxes or account, and it can't manage keys.
