- **Compact now, for a large Claude task while its cache is warm.** At 200k tokens or more, the task's menus, ⌘K and the near-the-limit row offer one click that shrinks the conversation, with both prices beside it: *About $0.74 now, $3.84 once the cache is cold* (estimates at API list prices, its tooltip says; on a plan it counts against your usage window). It never runs by itself and has no setting. It isn't held near the limit, a goal or Auto's checks aren't touched, and the task stays where it was, unless the compact fails. In the task it's one quiet line, *Compacting* while it runs, under the answer you were reading, which stays open, and the task's row keeps that answer's verdict. Spells: the `hold.compact`, `hold.compactNote`, `menu.compact` and `turn.compact` parts, and a `{ "compact": true }` button. A `/compact` you type now says what it did (*Compacted from 480k to 12k tokens.*) instead of *The agent finished without a message*, and when there was nothing to compact it says that.
- **Bring in your Conductor worktrees.** Add or open a project and New task shows one line, *3 worktrees from Conductor*, with **Bring in** and **Not now** (with Undo on its toast). Bring in makes each one a task right where it is, on its own branch, with your uncommitted changes as they were and the Claude chat picking up on your next message. wisp runs nothing there and never removes one, whether you archive, delete or merge: the task's side panel says so.
- **Subagents on Haiku.** Claude Code runs every built-in subagent (Explore, Plan, page reads) on the task's model, so an Opus task's subagents spend Opus. Settings → Usage → *Subagents run on* (*Same as the task*, *Sonnet* or *Haiku*) sets `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` and `CLAUDE_CODE_WORKFLOW_SUBAGENT_MODEL` on Claude runs; an Agent call or custom agent that names its own model keeps it. When subagents on the task's model wrote 40% or more of a turn's output, the turn's subagent line offers *Run subagents on Haiku*, which opens that row (wisp never changes it for you), and a turn that ran with it says so in its tooltip. Spells: the `subagentModel` setting, the `settings.subagentModel` and `turn.agents.switch` parts. API: `defaults.subagentModel`, `run.subagentModel` on Claude turns, `same` on `stats.subagents`.
- **Old worktrees clean themselves up.** A finished task kept its worktree, `node_modules` and all, until you archived it, so a few hundred tasks could fill a disk. Now a task's folder goes once it's been idle for 14 days (*Settings → Worktrees → Clean up automatically*: 3, 7, 14 or 30 days, or Never), and sooner if the disk gets under 10 GB free. The days count from this update, not from each task's age. Its branch stays and replying to the task brings the folder back, with the files `.worktreeinclude` lists; setup doesn't run again. Uncommitted work, open PRs, tasks in use or in For later, and anything you mark **Keep worktree** (in a task's More, its right-click menu or ⌘K) stay. The same pane shows what each worktree takes and lets you remove one, clean up in one click, or remove folders no task uses.
- **A large idle task says when its cache has gone cold.** Replying to a Claude task of 100k tokens or more after its prompt cache has lapsed reads the whole conversation back in. The reply box now says so in one quiet line, *Cache gone cold: this reply re-reads about 480k*, with Fork… beside it, and the usage-limit card says the same when the reset comes after the cache has gone. It's only words: Send stays one click and nothing waits. The time comes from what the harness reports, so Codex and the others show nothing rather than a guess.
- **Side discussions can run commands and edit files.** Ask `/side` to run the tests or fix a line and it does, in the main task's folder, without a card. Before each write wisp compares it with what the main task changed: a file it hasn't touched goes through, other lines of a file it changed go through with a warning row, and the same lines (or anything wisp can't place) show a clash card in the side discussion with both versions, whatever mode the main task is in. *Apply and tell main task* writes it and sends the main task a message, queued if it's running, and rechecks first in case it moved; *Keep main's version* refuses it. Commands that rewrite shared work (`git reset`, `commit`, `git rm`…) count as clashes, and so does one that removes or writes over a file the main task changed (`rm`, `>`, `sed -i`, `patch`, `tee`, `cp`…), named on the card before it runs. Files it hasn't touched stay free. Claude Code and OpenCode only for now: other harnesses can't ask before a write, so their side discussions keep reading and say so. `/btw` still only reads.
- **Review by another harness checks what you ruled out.** The review before Merge now gets the lines in your own messages that rule something out or narrow the task (*don't touch migrations/*, *only change web/*, *leave the tests alone*) and the task's *Done when*, and checks the diff against each. A change that breaks one comes back as a High finding naming the path and your words (*Touched migrations/ — you said: don't touch migrations*), and the PR panel's review lists what it checked, a cross on any it broke. A harness's own `/review` only looks for bugs.
- **Lean Claude Code runs for wisp's own side runs.** The small Claude Code runs wisp makes for itself (goal judges, the judge, the scout, review by another harness, the retro, titles) now start with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC`: no telemetry, error reports or update checks, so 5 connections at start instead of 12 and about a second sooner, which keeps a box running many sessions from running out of NAT ports (anthropics/claude-code#106875). Nothing is lost there: they only answer with JSON or a short note. Tasks, crew jobs, delegated tasks, subagents and scheduled, automation and trigger runs start as before, because a lean run drops your claude.ai connectors (Google Drive, Docs, …), org memory (Team plans), `/ultrareview`, `/advisor`, push notifications and server-rolled features. *Lean Claude Code runs* in Settings → Agents: *Off*, *wisp's side runs* (the default) or *All Claude runs*, which says what's lost. Spells: the `leanRuns` setting (`off`, `on`, `all`) and the `settings.leanRuns` part. API: `lean` (`on`, `off` or `null`) on `PATCH /sessions/:id` for one task, `defaults.leanRuns`.

- **Make a pane: say what you want and an agent builds it.** Type *make me a pane that…* in New task, or use **Make a pane** in My Stuff's header or ⌘K. An agent writes the app into My Stuff from an empty folder, pins it, and the pane reloads in place each time the agent changes it. Pinning grants nothing: the pane does nothing with wisp until you press **Allow…** on its bar. Apps can load `/_wisp/pane.css` to look like wisp, in light and dark. API: `kind: 'pane'` on `POST /api/sessions`, `pin` on `wisp_save` (only in a Make a pane task).
- **Two starter panes: Agent board and Allowance board.** Add one from the Make a pane dialog; the click asks what it may use. **Agent board** shows every task across harnesses, accounts, machines and your crew in Working, Needs you, Review and Done columns. Drag a task to Done or press its Done button, and send follow-ups from its card (wisp's card still asks first). **Allowance board** shows each account's usage windows, when they reset, and how fast they're going when that can be told. It uses the new `usage:read` scope and `wisp.usage.accounts()`. API: `GET /api/stuff/starters`, `POST /api/stuff/starters/:id`.
- **Allowance board shows what each period cost.** Each usage window now keeps its periods, and the board shows them newest first: how much went Outside wisp, then what 1% of the window cost in wisp (in dollars on Claude Code, in millions of tokens on Codex), period by period. Costs worked out from running totals, on older Claude Code turns and Codex follow-ups, are marked ≈. After 2 full weekly periods on each side, a line says whether 1% buys less, the same or more than it did, and what changed in the CLI; before that it says nothing. API: `periods` and `shrink` on each window of `wisp.usage.accounts()`, and `GET /api/usage/periods`. A starter copy you or an agent haven't changed now follows wisp's newer version, with the one before kept for Restore; a changed one stays as it is.

- **Panes on home sit side by side.** Pin two or three apps on home and they fill its width as tiles instead of one under the other. Drag between them or under them to resize; wisp keeps the sizes, and a double-click resets them. On a phone or in a narrow window each pane is a row that opens as a full page with Back, and it only starts when you open it. A spell can set the layout with `columns`, `widths` and `height` on `split.panes`.

- **Repos set up for Conductor work in wisp.** A repo with `.conductor/settings.toml` (or `conductor.json`, which Conductor ignores once the toml exists) gets its setup script run in each new worktree and its run script offered in the side panel's Run, as is. The setup goes through the same trust card as any repo's: wisp lists it and waits for Trust and start, except in Full auto. Both get `CONDUCTOR_ROOT_PATH`, `CONDUCTOR_WORKSPACE_PATH`, `CONDUCTOR_WORKSPACE_NAME`, `CONDUCTOR_DEFAULT_BRANCH`, `CONDUCTOR_PORT` (the first of ten free ports in a row) and `CONDUCTOR_IS_LOCAL`. For the setup, `.wisp/worktree.json` comes first, then Cursor's `.cursor/worktrees.json`, then Conductor; `.wisp/worktree.json`'s `run` also beats Conductor's. A file wisp can't read for sure is refused whole, and the task and Run say why. Of several named run actions, wisp offers the one with `default = true`, else the first. Ignored: `scripts.archive`, `run_mode`, `auto_run_after_setup`, `environment_variables`, `.conductor/settings.local.toml`, `~/.conductor` and scripts kept only in Conductor's app.

- **The crew has its own rail.** In a wide window the sidebar now has a slim rail with a face for each crew member, and the task list sits beside it as a raised panel. Each face shows at a glance who's working (and on how many jobs), who's asking you, who has a report you haven't read and who's paused. Hover a face for its status; click it to open the thread. Org and **+** sit at the bottom of the rail. Narrow windows and the phone app keep the Crew section, and a spell can bring it back with `sidebar` `crewRail: false`.
- **wisp Grid has a real home page.** After sign-in you used to get one long column of equal rows, with *Delete* weighing as much as *Open*. Now your cloud wisp comes first, with its state, a big **Open**, its address and a picture of the app; your computers and published apps sit under it as cards; and *What your grid can do* offers the next thing to try, saying so when it's done (*4 published*, *Phone connected*). A new account also gets three first steps. Devices, sign-ins, sign out and delete moved to **Account** (`/account`), a tab in the header.
- **Compare pages on trywisp.dev.** [Conductor alternatives](https://trywisp.dev/guides/conductor-alternatives.html) sets seven apps side by side for people leaving Conductor, and links a page each for [Orca](https://trywisp.dev/guides/wisp-vs-orca.html), [T3 Code](https://trywisp.dev/guides/wisp-vs-t3-code.html), [Paseo](https://trywisp.dev/guides/wisp-vs-paseo.html), [cmux](https://trywisp.dev/guides/wisp-vs-cmux.html), [Multica](https://trywisp.dev/guides/wisp-vs-multica.html) and [Paradev](https://trywisp.dev/guides/wisp-vs-paradev.html). Each is built from the rival's own docs, says where the rival is better and when to pick it. Moving off Conductor links to them, and the guides list has a *wisp compared* section.
- **The crew rail picks what the list shows.** Click a face and the list beside it holds only that member's jobs, every kind, in the usual sections, and New task is *For Ada ×*: what you send is a job for Ada, as if you had typed `@Ada`. **All**, first on the rail, brings every task back, and takes a dot if something outside the pick needs you, so a pick never hides a question. A typed `@Other` wins, × sends one prompt as your own, and opening a task the pick hides goes back to All. Side by side only: stacked and in the phone app nothing changes.
- **Hover a crew member to see what they're on.** A face on the crew rail now brings a card instead of a tooltip: name, role and status, then their tasks in the order that matters (asking you, working, an unread report, recently finished), up to six, then *All N in the thread*. Click a row to open that task, or the head to pick them and open their thread, like a click on the face. The card waits while you move the pointer across, and rows don't move under it while tasks finish. With the keyboard, `→` goes into the card (`←` with the list on the right), `↑` `↓` walk it, Enter opens and Esc goes back. Spells: the `crew.rail.card`, `crew.rail.card.head`, `crew.rail.card.row` and `crew.rail.card.all` parts.
- **Try it two ways: one task, twice, on one harness, with one setting changed.** Pick a harness and click **Try it two ways** in the prompt box's bar (the ↻ button, named in full on a wide box). A copy of your setup joins the pill and its picker opens with a *What's different* group for Claude Code: **Subagents** (Task's model, Haiku, Sonnet) and **Skills** (On, Off). Skills off runs Claude Code without its skills and slash commands; plugins, agents and CLAUDE.md stay. Each run keeps its setting on follow-ups, forks, moves and Rerun, and **Compare** says what differed under the prompt (*Subagents: Opus → Haiku*). Ask near the limit now adds up the runs of one launch that share an account, here and in the page. It isn't on Auto or in Plan. For the API, `POST /sessions` takes `subagentModel`, `skills: 'off'` and `trial: true` (and the first two on each `compare` entry), and `PATCH /sessions/:id` takes `trialKept`; for the CLI, `--agent claude --try subagents=haiku` or `--try skills=off`. Spells can reach `launcher.tryTwo`, `compare.diff` and `picker.vary`. Anonymous usage stats get one count per launch, with nothing else in it.
- **Start from your Linear or Jira issues by signing in, with no API key.** New task → Issue → *Connect Linear…* or *Connect Jira…* used to land on an empty Connectors pane that said nothing about either. Now it opens the connector's form already filled in (Linear's `mcp.linear.app` or Atlassian's `mcp.atlassian.com` server), with *Add and sign in* focused, so one click and the browser's sign-in is all it takes. A Linear connector you'd already signed in to with the browser used to count for nothing (only an API key did), so the picker kept saying *Connect Linear…*. Now a signed-in connector lists the open issues assigned to you through that server's own tools (`list_issues`, `searchJiraIssuesUsingJql`), and on Linear, when none are assigned to you, the workspace's open issues under a heading that says so. A connector you already have for it opens to sign in again, and an API key still works as before. Linear and Jira are also among the examples on an empty Connectors list.

### Changed

- **Webhook triggers are off until you switch them on** (Settings → Automations → Webhook triggers), as in v1: a trigger's URL answers 404 until then, even with its secret.
- **wisp moved to the WispAgents org.** The repo is [WispAgents/wisp](https://github.com/WispAgents/wisp) and the Homebrew tap is [WispAgents/homebrew-tap](https://github.com/WispAgents/homebrew-tap): new installs use `brew install wispagents/tap/wisp-agents` for the CLI, and **`brew install --cask wispagents/tap/wisp` now installs the desktop app**. Existing `m0xoo/tap` installs keep updating (GitHub redirects, and Update now runs `brew upgrade wisp-agents`).
- **A worktree's copied folders cost no disk on a Mac.** What `.worktreeinclude` brings into a new worktree (a `node_modules`, a build cache) is now a copy-on-write clone on APFS, and on Linux on btrfs, XFS or bcachefs: it shares the checkout's blocks until one side changes a file. A 2.1 GB `node_modules` took 2.2 GB of disk per worktree and now takes under 0.1 GB. Other file systems and Windows copy as before. The worktree records how many bytes it shares (`prepared.cloned`).
- **Old worktrees go sooner, and never pile up past a cap.** A clean task whose PR is merged or closed loses its folder at the next hourly cleanup, a Done one after a day idle, and the rest after 7 days instead of 14 (Settings → Worktrees → *Clean up automatically*). New *Keep at most* (20, 40 or 80 worktrees, or no limit; 40 by default): past that many tasks' worktrees the oldest idle ones go, a few minutes after the new one that went over; left over folders don't count. Uncommitted work, kept worktrees, For later, open PRs and tasks in use still never go, even over the cap, and branches stay, so replying brings a folder back. Each cleanup also runs `git worktree prune` in your repos, so `git worktree add` stops reading hundreds of entries for folders long gone. Looking costs less while agents work: folder sizes are measured at most every 6 hours or when one comes or goes, and git reads only folders that changed. Folders no task knows still go only by hand. Spells can set it (`settings.worktreeMax`).
- **One answer box for questions and approvals: the reply box.** A question or approval card used to carry its own *Or say what to do instead (denies this call)…* box right above the reply box's *Or tell Claude Code what to do instead…*, and both were cut short on a phone. Now the card holds the question and its buttons, and you type your answer in the reply box. It answers the question, or denies the approval with your words, exactly as the old box did, in Plan, Edits and Full auto. A mode, harness or team you picked under the box waits for your next follow-up instead of turning the answer into a new run. A spell that hides `composer` or `composer.input` still loads, with a warning, and the box stays while a task waits on your answer.
- **Ship it and Merge say when comments are still waiting.** With comments you haven't sent on a task, both dialogs show one line, *2 comments not sent yet: they go with your next follow-up*. It never holds anything back: the buttons work as before and Merge doesn't turn red for it, and the comments stay in the reply box. Several warnings in Merge's box now sit on a line each.
- **Changes shows a diff without a click.** The first time you look at a task's files, the first one opens, or all of them when there are three or fewer. Generated, very long and already viewed files don't count. A spell can turn it off (`panel.changes` `autoOpen: false`).
- **The What changed line names the Codex model.** When Codex's model catalog moves between turns, the line now says which models were added, removed or changed: *Codex model catalog changed (gpt-6-luna changed)*, up to three by name. Turns from before this still read *Codex model catalog changed*. API: `stats.cli.models` holds one hash per model.
- **Codex's stop line reads fresh numbers.** With an Automations stop line set for Codex, held-back work checks Codex first when the last reading is over 15 minutes old: nothing is spent, and a failed check changes nothing. The Allowance board's line on whether 1% buys less says nothing for Codex across December's plan change, when chats and Codex share one allowance, and Codex's bars no longer freeze if its limit moves to a new id.
- **A pane can no longer send a prompt on its own.** With `tasks:write` allowed, `wisp.tasks.launch()` and `wisp.tasks.followUp()` used to send straight away. They now open a card in wisp itself, never in the pane, showing the project or task, harness, mode (Full auto says so) and the exact prompt with invisible and direction characters spelled out as `<U+202E>`. Cancel has the focus, Send wakes after 600 ms, and only your click sends. One card at a time; if a dialog of yours is open, the pane is told to try again. A pane's task also now runs in your default mode: it always ran in Full auto, whatever your default was.
- **A pane's write access lasts as long as its code.** If an agent or an update changes any file of the app, `tasks:write` stops working and its bar says *Changed since allowed*, until you allow it again or the code goes back to what you allowed. Read scopes stay. API: `GET /api/stuff/:id/grant`.
- **A pane's access can't grow during a Full auto run.** Allowing a new scope is refused (*Not while … runs in Full auto.*) while the task the pane is for, or the task that made the app, runs in Full auto. Taking access back always works, and other Full auto tasks, such as crew jobs, don't count.
- **The Haiku offer says where it pays off.** The offer under a turn now reads *Run subagents on Haiku: best on large repos and read-heavy work*: Haiku saved 75% on a 52k-line repo and nothing on a 10k-line one.
- **Panes stay where wisp put them, and say what they can't promise.** A pane's frame can no longer send what it read away by navigating itself to another address, and an app that doesn't use wisp can no longer frame wisp's page to steal your clicks. Secrets in a task's title, summary or live line are redacted before a pane gets them. The Allow card no longer says an app can't reach the internet, which wasn't true: browsers don't apply these rules to WebRTC, so it now tells you to treat what a pane reads as visible to whoever wrote it, and says so plainly when the app's code uses WebRTC.
- **Full auto no longer asks before a repo's hooks, MCP servers and worktree setup run.** Full auto already skips every approval, so a task in it runs them as before the trust card, unasked, and a new worktree sets itself up at once. Plan and Edits still wait on the card, and a run held on it starts when you switch the task to Full auto.
- **The picked crew member stands out in the rail.** The tile the list is showing (All or a member) now has a long pip on the rail's outer edge and a firmer outline, so it reads at a glance in light and dark. Hovering another face only tints it and shows a short pip, so hover and the pick no longer look alike.
- **A task is named after what you asked, not after what the agent did last.** A task used to take its first answer's headline as its name (*Connectors pane fixed and merged*), so the list read as a log of outcomes. Now the agent's report names the subject of your request (*Connectors pane*), and wisp uses that; an agent that doesn't say keeps the name from your prompt's first line. A task you renamed, and a compare task, keep theirs.
- **A crew member's finished-job toast and notification open that task, not the member's thread.** Its **Open** (and a click on the desktop notification) opens the job as a task, under its member with the member as a crumb, like opening it from the list.
- **The desktop app is now Tauri, and about a third of the size.** It's the same page and the same server, on the system's own web view (WebKit on a Mac, WebView2 on Windows, WebKitGTK on Linux) instead of a bundled Chromium, with Node beside it. Downloads against 0.57.0's Electron builds: Mac Apple silicon 42.9 MB zip (was 130.3 MB) and 35.9 MB dmg (was 130.6 MB), Mac Intel 44.3 MB zip (was 137.1 MB), Windows x64 26.6 MB (was 114.4 MB), Windows Arm 22.7 MB (was 108.1 MB). The download names haven't changed. It needs **macOS 13.3 or later** (Safari 16.4, which the page needs); `install.sh --app` says so on an older Mac. **Updating from an Electron build** is the usual *Update to …*: the new app takes its place and opens, with your tasks, projects, crew and settings, all in `~/.wisp`. What the old window kept for itself starts fresh: text you typed and didn't send, the list's width, which files you'd viewed in Changes, and the commands you'd allowed a spell to run, which it asks about again. On Windows the old copy in `Programs\wisp-agents` and its entry in *Apps* go, and your Start menu, desktop and taskbar links open the new one; Electron's profile in `%APPDATA%\wisp-agents` is left. From npm, `wisp` no longer installs Electron (`npm i -g wisp-agents` has no dependencies at all): it downloads the app's shell from the release once, checks it and opens it on your Node, or opens a tab if it can't. Homebrew has nothing left to strip.

### Fixed

- **The harness you pick for a crew member's job is the one it runs on.** With a member picked on the rail (or a prompt starting `@Ada`), the launcher's harness picker was ignored: the job always ran on the member's own harness, model and thinking level. The picker now shows the member's, and what you change there is used for that job only (`crewRun` on `POST /api/sessions`); the member keeps theirs, and the next job starts from it again.

- **Claude Code offers Fable.** The model picker listed Default, Opus, Sonnet and Haiku only, so Fable (Claude Fable 5.2, Anthropic's most capable model) couldn't be picked, typed (`with fable`) or set on a crew member. It's now first after Default. Auto counts it as frontier and Never pick / Prefer rules on `claude:fable` work; with no benchmark score yet it starts on probation, so Auto suggests it rather than run it unasked, and Best-of-2 on Claude alone pairs it with Opus. A Fable 5.2 turn is priced at $8 / $40 per million tokens in Compact now, and its refusals hand over to another vendor.
- **A quick answer names its task too.** A one-line answer (*Not yet, #728 is still open*) has no report, so its task kept your whole prompt as its name (*Did you merge the title generator work or not yet? Just answer*). Now the agent ends even a plain answer with the subject of what you asked, and the task takes it (*Title generator merge*); a crew member's quick answer does the same. The answer still shows as it is, with no empty report or *Nothing new* above it.
- **A task that delegated in the background no longer says it's done before its delegates are.** A lead that sent subagents off with `wait: false` and ended its turn landed in Needs you, unread, with a "finished" notification, then started again by itself when their report came back. Now it stays in Working, with *Waiting on @name* on its row and a panel with Stop under its latest turn, and lands, with one notification, once the follow-up that reads their reports ends. Stopping it, or deleting the last delegate it waited on, lands it quietly. A spell can restyle or hide the panel (`delegate.waiting`).
- The Tauri app on macOS no longer quits when a notification shows and macOS returns no list of delivered ones.
- **A Claude turn that worked no longer shows the refusal card.** Once Claude Code had a refusal anywhere in a run, every later answer of it said *Claude Code turned this task down*: the turn after a refusal the CLI retried and got past, and the turns a finished background shell or subagent starts. Now only a turn that itself ended in a refusal says so, and a subagent's refusal isn't the task's.
- **A Claude run on a Console sign-in says so.** When Claude Code's claude.ai login stops working and an `ant auth login` or keyless Console profile is on the machine, Claude Code quietly runs on that profile and bills the Console org, and nothing in its output says so. wisp now reads `claude auth status --text` as a task starts (once a minute per account, beside the run, cut off after 5 s) and, when it shows a `Profile:` line, marks the turn *Console sign-in* and counts it under Settings → Usage → Plan budget's *Past your plan*. It's a notice only: the run is never held, asked about or slowed. API: `turn.overage.kind` and budgets' `overage.kind` can be `profile`.
- **A forwarded Telegram message no longer runs as your own words.** Someone else's text forwarded to your bot could start a task, up to Full auto in a private chat, or follow up on one, answer its question or stop it. A link code in a forward could link the chat. Now a forward does nothing: you get one line saying nothing started and to say it in your own words, and a chat that isn't linked hears nothing. Replies, and your own words beside one, work as before.
- **A task started from a channel at a cap stays under it.** A Slack or GitHub message, or a Telegram group's, runs at Edits or lower, but its agent could call wisp's own tools, which need no approval: start a Full auto task on another machine, raise another task, read other projects, hire a crew member, all with no prompt. Now anything it starts carries the cap; going above it, to another machine, a sandbox or another project is refused with a line the agent reads, and so are changes to your crew and connectors, public links, and tasks it didn't start. wisp_session, wisp_sessions, wisp_stuff and wisp_stats keep to its own project. Choosing a higher mode on your own follow-up in wisp lifts the cap for that task. Each agent also gets a token of its own, so it can't speak for another task, and on a capped task Codex's sandbox stays off the network whatever your config says, and opencode waits for you before a command or a web fetch. Tasks you start work as before.
- **A reload right after Send no longer brings the prompt back.** Reloading or closing wisp while a new task was still starting cut the request off, and the page took that for a failure: the prompt came back as a draft although the task was running, ready to be sent twice. It now stays gone; if the task really didn't start, ↑ in the prompt box still has it.
- **A task from GitHub or Slack says when it stalls on you.** When a task started there stops for an approval, a clash or a connector sign-in, which can only be answered in wisp, the thread now says so at once: *Waiting for your OK in wisp: run `npm publish`*, once per ask, whatever the Phone events say. That covers a subagent, side discussion or delegated crew job holding the task up, which used to say nothing, and every approval in a turn followed up from a comment. A public GitHub repo gets only *Waiting for your OK in wisp.*, even with full reports on.
- **A channel no longer claims a model that won't run.** The Claude CLI runs `haiku` on Sonnet in Plan, so a start from Telegram or a GitHub comment that said *Haiku · Plan* was wrong. The ack, a crew member's and a follow-up's now name Sonnet and say why, and a finished turn's report says which model ran when it isn't the one the task asked for (never on a public repo, which keeps its fixed line).
- **A reply to Slack or Telegram survives a broken connection, and GitHub's or Slack's first reply isn't lost.** A post whose connection broke was dropped, so a task could start in silence and its question arrive first. It now goes again, up to three tries, and can on rare occasions show twice. If it still can't get out, Settings says so and the first reply goes ahead of the next thing said in that thread. A follow-up or a crew mention that went, whose reply didn't, no longer says *Couldn't send that*: resending it ran it twice. On GitHub that reply no longer cancels the turn's report either.
- **A tap on an ntfy button is no longer lost while wisp is off.** wisp began reading each time from the moment it started, so an answer tapped while it was stopped, or while a cloud computer slept, never arrived. It now remembers where it stopped reading, and catches up with whatever ntfy still holds (12 hours on ntfy.sh). A question that was answered in the meantime stays answered.
- **A Slack trigger only answers the people you name.** Anyone in its channel could start a task. A Slack trigger now takes a list of member IDs (`users`; in Slack, their profile → ⋯ → Copy member ID), new and edited ones must name at least one, and everyone else is skipped. A trigger you already have keeps working as it did, and is marked `open` until you name someone.
- **Safari: the Enter that picks an IME word no longer sends your message.** With a Japanese, Chinese or Korean input method, confirming a word sent the whole box. Also in Safari: a row you scroll to in a long task list lands in view, the dashed loop in a flow block draws, code and key hints use the same font as Chrome, and the terminal's scrollbar shows.
- **Publish, Share and `/sandbox` work from a cloud computer.** A box couldn't reach its own grid: the rule that keeps boxes off the host also dropped the grid's public address, so each of the three waited a minute or two and failed with *Couldn't reach grid.trywisp.dev*. A box can now call the grid's public HTTPS like anyone on the internet, and nothing else on the host opens. Grids pick this up from `grid/deploy/firewall.sh`.
- **The Mac window buttons clear the list's panel.** In the desktop app the green button straddled the edge of the list's panel. The top of the sidebar is now a strip in the crew rail's colour: the window buttons, the sidebar button beside them, search and your account on the right, with the task panel under it. Beside the panel, the rail now has the main area's colour: dark in dark mode, white in light, and the panel alone stands out.
- **A subagent no longer gets the whole conversation's output on its receipt.** A resumed Claude turn whose last turn stored no per-model counters took `modelUsage` for its own, so it could read *246k of 246k output* when the subagent wrote 18k. That turn now shows only the main thread's tokens, with its subagents' calls and model, and the next turn is exact. Older stored turns no longer show a subagent output figure, and it no longer counts toward the Haiku offer or Settings → Usage.
- **A secret in a report no longer reaches your phone's channels.** Notifications to ntfy and Telegram went out as written, so a token an agent printed in its report landed on ntfy's public server, or in your Telegram chat. wisp now takes secrets out of everything it sends there, with a line saying so, whatever your settings. Reports with no secret read exactly as before.
- **Preview's bar no longer cuts its address to a fragment.** On a narrow phone or window the bar read *Running · loca…*. Where the address won't fit whole it now says *Running* or *Starting* alone; the address stays in Logs and Open in browser, and wider screens show it as before.
- **A stopped task says why on its row.** A crash, a usage limit and a refusal all read a red *Blocked*, the same as an agent that stopped to explain. The list, ⌘K, older turns and shared tasks now say *Failed* in red, *At limit* or *Refused* in amber; *Blocked* stays for the agent's own report.
- **The editor's head stays readable while the agent works.** A long path used to squeeze Back to 16px, and the file name and *Agent working: save when it stops* both came out cut. Back now keeps its size and the word stays *Unsaved*, with the reason as the tip on Save and on the word (touch has no tips, so there it says *Agent working*). In a tight head, the language offer is its spark alone. ⌘K now says *Open file…*, like Changes, and the Rename toast no longer points at a note that's gone.
- **A pane opened as a page keeps its name on the row.** On a phone or in a narrow window, a pane with all its access showed its name about 8px above Back, Reload and Unpin. It now sits level with them, and only moves up when a line under it says what the pane still asks for.
- **Reading a task stays put.** A run finishing, a queued follow-up starting or a compact no longer throws you to the new turn, and the answer you're reading stays open. New output only follows you at the end of the task: scroll up and it stops at once. Your own send moves the view once, and a *Latest* pill over the reply box takes you to anything that landed while you were reading further up.
- **An overlap warning no longer knocks you off the end of a task.** The warning that another task changes the same files comes a couple of seconds after a run, and at the end of a task it covered the last lines and stopped the next answer from following: a *Latest* pill showed instead. The view now stays at the end.
- **Long file names show whole in Changes.** The Stage and Viewed controls left a name about 140px in the default 480px panel, so `changes-open-first.test.js` lost its end. A name that doesn't fit now takes the whole line and the counts and controls drop under it, in the same columns as every other row; the other rows stay one line. On a phone the counts move to the controls' line, which gives the name their room.
- **The page says when it has lost wisp.** With wisp gone, a task kept saying *working* and its timer kept counting until you tried to send. After 4 seconds *Reconnecting to wisp…* now shows on the task's header, or at the top of home and the list, on every width and in the phone app, where the old line sat behind the task page. It goes as soon as wisp is back.
- **Claude Code tasks in Plan mode can ask you and tag themselves.** Plan mode refused wisp's own tools (*Cannot call mcp__wisp__ask_user while in plan mode*), so a planning agent couldn't put a question to you. Now it can ask, tag the task, schedule a follow-up, show its watch page and read your tasks, stuff and crew; what starts or changes something stays refused until you leave Plan.
- **A working task counts *1 action*.** The working panel said *1 actions* until the second one.
- **Compare runs use the account you picked.** The account picked for a second or third runner in Compare was ignored: the run took your last pick or the first free one while its pill showed your choice. Each run now goes on its own pick, and a limit check looks at that account.
- **With no connection, wisp says so in its own words.** A failed request showed the browser's own *Failed to fetch* (or *Load failed* in Safari). Toasts and panels now say *Can’t reach wisp. Check the connection and try again.*, and a request given up on says wisp took too long.
- **A reply wisp doesn't answer comes back to you.** A follow-up sent while wisp wasn't answering left the task on Working with the text gone and no message. After 20 seconds the text is back in the box, the task goes back to how it was and a toast says *wisp took too long to answer. Try again.* Sending it again is safe: if the first one got through after all, it doesn't run twice. Queued follow-ups stay as they were.
- **The cold line waits for the bar on a phone.** On a phone and in the app, *Cache gone cold* sat above the folded reply box and made it 20px taller, 44px in the app, the moment a task went cold. It now arrives with the bar when you tap the box and goes with it, so a resting box stays one line and never moves.
- **The usage-limit card says when a cache may have gone cold.** Tasks that woke at a reset could go cold well inside their cache time and re-read 170k tokens with no warning. The card now says *Cache may have gone cold by then* however soon the reset is, *will have* when it's past the cache time, and nothing once it's already cold: the line used to show twice, on the card and in the reply box.
- **Changes stays current after Ship it.** Changes kept showing the Commit box, Stage boxes and *2 uncommitted* until a reload, and Commit then said there was nothing to commit. They now go as soon as the ship ends, in every open window, including when the push fails after the commit landed.
- **Deleting your Grid account shows *Account deleted* again.** With your cloud wisp running, the account page refreshed itself halfway through and landed on the sign-in page, though the account was deleted.
- **The keyboard stays in the reply box after Send.** Sending from a finished task moved its row from Needs you to Working and left the keys nowhere, so the next word went to wisp's shortcuts: typing *edit* raised a toast, opened Changes and asked to stop and take over the run you'd just started. The box keeps the keys now. On a touch screen Send still lets the keyboard drop.
- **A goal check's page shows everything the judge said.** The words in a judge's last message before its vote were dropped from the transcript of its check; they now show as its last message.
- **A side discussion keeps its link back to a long-titled task.** With a long main task title the line above its first message read *Side discussion of …*, with no title and nothing to click. The title is what's cut now, the link stays, and its focus ring isn't clipped.
- **A failed run says why, at once.** The screen said *Something went wrong*, and the reason took over a second to appear behind a typed-out command. Now it reads *Claude Code failed* with the error's first line, in the task's row too, and the output shows straight away. The command was made up, `claude run`, and its **Run** button started the harness in the task's folder: failed runs and the usage-limit and too-many-devices screens no longer show it, on old tasks too. **Try again** stays, and terminal blocks with a real command keep **Run**.
- **A side discussion's clash card keeps its answers in view on a phone.** It no longer repeats the main task's lines when the diff shows them, or a second *what to do instead* box above the reply box, so *Keep main's version* isn't under the reply box when a phone opens it.
- **App data isn't stopped for a slow disk.** An app's query on a file that took a while to open came back as *took over 5 s and was stopped*; its time limit now starts once the file is open.
- **Publish keeps secrets off the public link.** `.env` files, credentials, keys and source maps went up with an app and anyone could open them. Now they're never saved to My Stuff or published, and a wisp Grid drops them on receipt and opens no dotfile (`.well-known` stays), even for an older wisp. A key or token in the app's own files stops the publish with the file and line, never the value; public keys (Firebase, Mapbox, Stripe `pk_`, a Supabase anon key) and placeholders go through. A published app's card, and the answer an agent gets, now say it plainly: anyone with the link can read and change its data, so no personal data in it.
- **A second side discussion no longer blames the main task.** When one side discussion had changed a file, another side discussion's card said those lines were the main task's, and Apply sent the main task a message about a file it never touched. The card and the warning row now name the side discussion, Apply and Keep leave the main task out of it, and lines the main task did change stay its own.
- **A goal with one judge reads right.** The check told the agent *0 of 1 judge say met*; it now says *0 of 1 judge says met*.
- **A Claude Code follow-up no longer counts the whole task again.** On `--resume` the CLI's cost and per-model counters carry on from the last run, and wisp took them for that turn's, so every follow-up's cost, receipt and model split held all the turns before it: Usage, crew usage and the budget's units added them up again (cost read about 3.6x high on real tasks). A turn now counts what it spent, subagents included, and Usage's tokens are the receipt's. Turns from before this keep their old cost; their tokens read from the turn's own usage.
- **A Codex follow-up no longer counts the whole thread again.** `codex exec` reports the thread's running token totals, so each follow-up counted every earlier turn too, and Ask near the limit, Drain watch, usage and the receipt read high. Each turn now counts its own tokens. Turns from before this keep the numbers they stored.
- **The reply box in a phone browser stays one row.** Up to 720px wide, *Side question · …* and a read time are one line above the buttons, ending in `…`, instead of a word-wide column that tripled the box. Typing `/btw` moves neither Send nor the input sideways, and no button squeezes: the bar scrolls sideways with Send pinned, as in the app.
- **Archiving a task right after it finishes removes its clean worktree.** In the seconds a CLI takes to exit after its result, the task already shows as done, but archiving it left the worktree on disk for good. Archive now lets that CLI go, as Ship it does, and removes the worktree at once.
- **Files that arrive during a run open in Changes.** With the panel open, new files stayed shut until a reload, and a first look at only a lockfile or a large file left every later file shut. They now open as a reload would show them: all of three or fewer, or the first. Once you open, fold or view a file, nothing else opens by itself.
- **Grid rate limits hold across the minute.** All counters were wiped at once every 60 seconds, so a client that timed it got twice its limit in a burst, and a limit could reset for someone a moment after they reached it. Limits now count the last 60 seconds for each client.
- **The keyboard stays put in Preview.** Run, Restart, Stop, Logs and Escape while picking dropped focus to the page, so the next Tab started over from the top. Each now keeps its place: Logs and Restart stay on their button, Run lands on the bar, Stop and an exit land on the command, Escape goes back to Pick.
- **Issue sits with the recipes on New task.** It used to wrap alone under the project chips, even with No folder picked. It's now at the right end of the Recipes row, and only shows with a project picked; ⌘K *Start from an issue…* still works anywhere.
- **`/sandbox` no longer fails on a project that takes a while to pack.** A sandbox left idle while wisp packed your project was stopped by the grid. The upload woke it, but the very next step was refused with *Sandbox is offline*, and after three tries the task was never made. wisp now lets requests through while a cloud computer wakes.
- **An app whose database was stopped while it was being made no longer stays broken.** If the first query ran into the time limit before `seed.sql` finished, the file stayed half made and every later query failed with *no such table*. A seed that didn't finish now starts over on the next query, and an app's database left empty this way gets its seed. A `seed.sql` too slow for the limit (5 s) now says the query was stopped each time, rather than leaving half its data.
- **A task or crew member can have as many delegates as you pick.** Delegates stopped at 6 (12 counting copies): a seventh crew member had to replace one, and extra rows from the API, a preset or a follow-up were dropped without a word. Add delegate, the Org chart, shared crew files and subagents an agent makes on the fly no longer have a limit, and one delegate call reaches every member, not the first 12. Copies of one delegate still go up to 5. A run too long for the computer to start now ends with an error that says so, instead of staying on Working.
- **The phone app's reply box stays one tidy row.** *Side question · Claude Code keeps working* and a read time now take one line above the buttons instead of wrapping into a one-word column over Queue and making the box three times taller. Queue is a 44px target, typing `/btw` moves neither Send nor the input sideways and the buttons stay readable, a long file name no longer squeezes its icon, and under a live transcript the working panel is one centred row with less empty space around a turn's stats.
- **Follow up on a crew member's job near your usage limit asks you, instead of failing.** In a member's thread, a follow-up that would take its account past your line showed only a red error and lost what you typed. Now the box keeps your text and shows the same row as a task's reply box, with **Send anyway**.
- **Start anyway on a job for a crew member on another computer starts it there, instead of held.** Near your usage limit, the yes you gave a member who works on another computer (or in a sandbox) didn't go with the job, so that computer started it held in *Needs you*. Now the job carries your yes, and without one that computer asks you first, as yours here does.
- **Start anyway on *@Ada @Quill …* gives each member one job.** Near your usage limit for only one of the members you named, the others started at once and the row asked about the last one; Start anyway then gave the ones that had started a second job. Now every member is checked before any starts.
- **Send anyway on a message to a crew member sends it, instead of starting the task held.** Near your usage limit, a job you gave a crew member yourself (*@Ada …* in the prompt box, or the box in their thread) was always created held, *Held before it started*, even after **Start anyway**, so you had to open it and press Start now. It's now asked about like any task of yours: the hold row shows over the box (their thread's box has it too), and Start anyway starts the job at once, also when it waits for a free slot first. Check-ins, triggers and other automations still start held.
- **A review that saw only part of the PR says so.** Review by another harness used to cut a long diff off silently, and could say *Nothing to fix* about changes it never got. It now keeps whole files and hunks, and the PR row says **Partly reviewed** instead of Reviewed, on findings and failing tests too. The PR panel opens the review with a box naming the files it didn't read, Merge's confirmation warns, and your don't-touch rules lose their green ticks because those files weren't checked. A whole review adds one quiet line, *Reviewed all 12 files.*
- **Saving settings on Windows no longer fails now and then.** When something held `config.json` (or another file wisp saves) open for a moment, such as antivirus, the search indexer or another reader, the save failed with *EPERM: operation not permitted, rename* and the change (a new connector, a setting) was refused. wisp now waits out the lock, for half a second at most, before giving up.
- **Open on a toast or notification closes the image viewer or dialog in front.** **Open** on a reminder, a *finished* or *has a question* toast, *Side discussion started* or *Sent to …* (and a tapped system notification) opened the task behind the image viewer or any dialog that was showing, so it seemed nothing happened until you closed it.
- **Open on the Saved to My Stuff toast now closes the image viewer.** Saving a picture from the viewer, then clicking **Open** on the toast, opened My Stuff behind the viewer, which stayed on screen until you closed it.
- **The desktop app's first window opens within 5 seconds, even with a slow shell setup.** On first open the app reads PATH from your login shell. A `~/.zshrc` that took long kept the window from opening until it finished, because the 5-second limit never stopped an interactive zsh.
- **New task steps aside for a question's answers.** In the Android app, the floating New task button could sit on the Yes or No of a question answered from its row, and a long answer wrapped centred. The button now fades away while a row's answers are on screen, and answers read from the left like the open card's.
- **The task list stays where you put it.** Sending a follow-up threw the list to Working, a run finishing threw it back to the top (from 2963px to 0), and launching from New task scrolled it down to the new row. The list now only moves when you scroll it, press J or K, or open a task from search or a toast whose row is out of view, which used to leave the row off-screen. A launch whose row doesn't show says *Sent to Claude* with Open instead. Stacked and on a phone, a launch still brings the row into view.
- **Project pickers are a plain list again.** Since Make a pane, every project picker (New task's projects, the top bar's, a crew member's and sharing's) drew each project as its own bordered, centred box inside a framed list: Make a pane's starter list used the picker's class name. It has its own now.
- **A quick answer reaches your phone.** When an agent replied in plain text with no report card, Telegram and ntfy said *Ready for review.* and left the answer in wisp. The answer is now the message, cut at 1000 characters.
- **A spell's card in a task is full width again.** Since the spare-allowance row on home, a spell panel shown as a card above or below a task (`card.top`, `card.bottom`) was squeezed into a 13px column, one word per line, and home's spare-allowance row sat low in its box: the two shared a class name. The row has its own now.
- **Delegating to many subagents no longer freezes wisp.** Starting subagents on harnesses that report edits after the fact (Codex, Cursor, OpenCode and others) ran one blocking `git stash create` per subagent, then one `git show` per subagent for each file, and the server answered nothing meanwhile: the page stalled for longer the bigger the repo. A delegate call now asks git once for the folder and once per file: 14 Codex subagents went from 42 blocking `git` calls to 3.
- **A harness you just signed in to reaches the picker however often the list is asked for.** Each look at the harness list starts a check, and a check that finished was thrown away if a newer one had started, so with looks arriving faster than a check takes, the list kept saying signed out.
- **The editor keeps your place.** A file now opens at its first line, not its last, with the caret there. *Load theirs* and a rename stay on your line. Closing a picker (⇧F12, ⌘P, Ctrl+Space, problems), Rename or *Cancel* on Discard gives the keyboard back to the file, where it used to fall to the page; Esc gives it to the file's row in Changes.
- **Settings → Connectors lays out again.** Its heading wrapped one word per line, Add connector showed as an empty 13px square, and the connector rows spilled out of the pane over the hint: the pane shared class names with the Changes panel, whose Viewed checkbox style squeezed it. Connectors now has class names of its own; spells that restyle `settings.connectors`, `connectors.row` or `connectors.import` keep working.
- **Settings → Cloud lays out again.** Since the crew rail's hover card, your cloud account sat in a floating 320px card over the pane's own words, and the grid address box under *Reach this computer from anywhere* (Settings → Machines) and *Add an account* was crushed behind its Sign in button: the card and the Cloud pane shared class names. The Cloud pane has names of its own now, and a test fails when one class is styled as two things in two stylesheets.
- **wisp Grid no longer goes down as a computer's tunnel closes.** A computer hanging up or reconnecting, or the grid shutting down, could land its keep-alive ping on a tunnel already closing, which threw where nothing caught it and stopped the grid. That ping is now skipped.
- **A long question shows its answers when it opens.** When an agent's question or approval had more text than fit, its answers sat under the reply box, so you had to scroll past everything to reach them: on a phone, and on a laptop-sized window. They now stop above the reply box and the text scrolls under them. Cards that fit look the same.
- **Quitting wisp while a cloud computer wakes stops the wake.** The wake and tunnel checks went on asking the grid for up to 90 s after wisp had stopped, keeping the process alive and changing the machine's state after the server was gone.
- **A crew check-in's follow-up comes back even when it had nothing to report.** A check-in that scheduled a follow-up (resume_later) and said it found nothing new was archived as its turn ended, and wisp drops what an archived task scheduled, so the follow-up never ran. Such a check-in is now filed under Done, still quiet, and its follow-up goes when it comes due.
- **Links that open in your browser work again in the desktop app.** Since the move to Tauri, clicking a link that opens a new tab did nothing: a preview's *Open in a new tab*, Preview's *Open in browser*, PR and GitHub links, links in answers. The click was sent to an opener the page isn't allowed to call. Now it goes out the same way `window.open` does, to your browser, once.

## [0.57.0] - 2026-11-03
- **A phone tap can't repeat after a restart.** ntfy sends taps of the same second again, and wisp forgot which it had handled when it restarted, so a Done could close a task you had reopened since. wisp now keeps the last ids with its place in ntfy.
- **wisp stops at once just after it starts.** Compressing the phone's bundle at startup held up the exit, so stopping wisp or a Grid box in its first seconds took 4 s, and 10 s or more on a busy machine.
- **A stopped or crashed run no longer leaves wisp's MCP helper running.** If the harness died while a question, a permission prompt or a delegation was waiting, its helper kept polling every 1.5 s for good, even across a restart, which could never answer it. It now exits with the harness, and a wait ends once wisp is gone: at once if a restarted wisp refuses it, or after 5 minutes down. The agent is told the question wasn't answered, and a permission prompt is refused. OpenCode's gate does the same.

### Added

- **Usage receipt on every turn, and Past your plan.** Each finished turn's trail bar now says what the run took, quietly: *38k tokens · 71% cache · 3% of weekly* (tokens read and written, Claude's cache reads and writes counted in; the share of the account's longest window, as wisp traced it). When a run is billed beyond the plan (Claude Code's extra usage, Codex credits, or Claude Code using an API key instead of your plan), the turn says *Past your plan* (or *API credit*), and Settings → Usage → Plan budget shows how many runs did that in the last 7 days, with *Open it* for the last one. An account that reports only a weekly window (Claude's plans without the 5-hour one) shows no empty 5-hour row, waits for the weekly reset at a limit, and tells you once that the 5-hour window is gone. Spells: `turn.receipt`, `turn.overage`, `budget.overage`. API: `turn.overage`, `overage` on `/agents` budgets, `cacheReadTokens`/`cacheWriteTokens` on Claude turns' stats.
- **Tokens per turn by account.** With two or more accounts on a harness, Settings → Usage (⌘K → Usage) shows, per model, each account's median turn over the last 7 days: output and input tokens and the share read from the cache, with a line such as *Work uses 1.9× the output tokens per turn of Personal (n=14 / 11)*, once each account has 5 finished turns on that model. When Claude Code ran with different tools on the two accounts (one's settings deny WebFetch, an MCP server only one has), the line says so: *…; Personal has no WebFetch*. *Copy report* puts both accounts' numbers in one block for a bug report to the vendor, and a turn's receipt tooltip has the same medians. It's a diagnostic only: it never suggests an account, and wisp never switches accounts. Spells: `budget.compare`. API: `compare` on `/agents` (`missing` per account), `details.tools` (`hash`, and `list` the first time in a task) on Claude turns.
- **wisp on your iPhone's Home Screen (wisp Grid).** In iPhone Safari on your cloud computer, Share → *Add to Home Screen* now gives a full-screen wisp with its own icon: it opens from its cache at once, then refreshes, reloads onto a new build after a grid update, and keeps clear of the status bar, the notch and the home indicator in portrait and landscape. Home shows *Add wisp to your Home Screen* once, with ×, in an iPhone's browser on a Grid. Alerts there come from the ntfy app (Settings → Phone): iOS lets a home-screen app notify only through Web Push, which wisp doesn't send yet. Signed out of the grid, it drops its cache and goes to the sign-in. Desktop browsers and the Android app are unchanged (the manifest is only added on a Grid). Spells: `home.install`.
- **Where a turn's tokens went: by model and by subagent.** When Claude Code ran subagents, the turn's trail bar says so beside the receipt: *3 web-fetch agents · Opus 5.5 · 27k of 41k output*, with every subagent type and the tokens per model in its tooltip (from the run's `modelUsage`, and each Agent call's messages). When the run's tools had no WebFetch but a `web-fetch` agent ran, it says *Claude Code ran page reads as an agent on <model> on this account* (anthropics/claude-code#106743: an experiment that runs page reads as an Opus subagent drains limits faster). Settings → Usage → Where it went adds *Where it went: subagents*: each subagent type's share of the account's longest window. Spells: `turn.agents`, `budget.subagents`. API: `models` and `subagents` on Claude turns' stats, `where.subagents` on `/agents` budgets.

### Changed

- **A hand-off to another harness keeps your rules and your effort.** When a task goes to another harness (*Hand to …* after a refusal or at a usage limit, or picking another harness for a follow-up), the turns it hears first now open with *What still holds*: the lines from your own messages that rule something out or narrow it (*don't touch the migrations*, *only change server/auth*, *leave the tests alone*, *no new dependencies*) and the task's *Done when* while its goal is open. The task also keeps its thinking level, as the new harness names it (Claude Code *max* goes to Codex as *xhigh*, its highest), instead of the new harness's default; picking a level with the switch still wins.

### Fixed

- **A Codex model at capacity moves the task on in Auto.** When Codex ends a run with *Selected model is at capacity* (openai/codex#59045), Auto now treats that model as unavailable, not as failing the task: it rests that model for 10 minutes (other Codex models can still run), the run doesn't count against it, and the task moves on with *… is at capacity · moving to …*. Before, the run counted as a failure and the task either stopped on the error or was handed off as if the model had got the task wrong.

## [0.56.0] - 2026-11-03

### Added

- **Hand a refused task to another agent.** When a harness turns a task down (Claude Code's usage-policy refusal on security, reverse-engineering or game work, or a short "I can't help with that" from a run that changed nothing), the task says so and offers *Hand to <another harness>* under the hero: one click sends the same prompt and its files there, with the task's turns so far. It only offers a harness on another company's model: after a Claude refusal, Copilot CLI, opencode, Cursor or Antigravity are offered only on a GPT, Gemini or other non-Anthropic model, which the hand-off pins (and the same for OpenAI). wisp never hands it over by itself, and never retries on another account of the same harness.
- **wisp asks before a repo's hooks, MCP servers and worktree setup run.** Claude Code runs a repo's `.claude/settings.json` hooks and `.mcp.json` servers without asking when wisp runs it, whatever the mode, Plan included, and wisp runs `.wisp/worktree.json` / `.cursor/worktrees.json` setup on each new worktree. Now the first run in such a repo, and the first after one of them changes, waits on a card listing each hook, server, helper command, plugin and setup step with its command and *Open file*: *Trust and start* (remembered per repo, its worktrees included), *Run without them* (Claude Code with only your own settings and wisp's MCP server, no setup; that task keeps running that way) or *Skip this run*. Repos with none of these files run as before. Subagents and side discussions never wait: they run without them until the repo is trusted. Take over no longer marks such a repo's worktree as trusted in Claude Code, Codex or Copilot, so their own trust question shows. Turn it off with *Ask before a repo’s hooks, servers and setup run* in Settings → Privacy (on by default). It lists what a repo asks to run; it can't tell you whether that's safe. Spells: the `settings.projectAsk` and `project.without` parts and the `projectAsk` setting. API: `held.project` on a held turn, `action: "without"` on `POST /sessions/:id/held`.
- **Review by another harness before Merge.** Turn on *Settings → General → Review by another harness* and, when a task's PR is open, a harness other than the one that wrote it (Codex reviews Claude Code's work, Claude Code reviews Codex's, on the plans you already have) reads the diff against the base and lists what it would fix, and the project's tests run again on the PR's head. The PR row shows *Not reviewed*, *Reviewing*, *N issues*, *Tests fail* or *Reviewed*; the PR panel lists each finding with *Send to agent* and *Review again*; Merge's confirmation says when the PR isn't reviewed or what the review found, but Merge stays possible. Pick *Another harness than the author* or one harness by name. It runs on that harness's own signed-in account and never switches accounts; past *Automations stop at* it waits. Off by default; spells can set it (`review`) and restyle the chip and section (`split.pr.review`, `panel.pr.review`).

### Changed

- **Tasks whose agent scheduled a check-in move from Needs you to Scheduled.** When a task finishes and its agent has said it will look again later (a follow-up it scheduled), the task waits under *Scheduled* with the check-in's time instead of sitting in *Needs you*, and it no longer counts in the tab title or a crew member's dot. It keeps its unread dot and one-click done there, and home's *Coming up* lists the check-in. Tasks asking you, due reminders and tasks with only your own reminders ahead stay in *Needs you*.

### Fixed

- **Accented letters and emoji in a long prompt or request no longer turn into `�`.** The server decoded each piece of a request body on its own, so a character whose bytes landed on both sides of a network chunk was garbled; it now decodes the whole body at once. The 2 MB body limit is also counted in bytes, as intended.
- **A machine that refuses an attachment says why.** When sending a file to another machine or a cloud computer was refused with an answer that isn't wisp's JSON (a proxy's *Request Entity Too Large*, a gateway's error page), you saw a JSON parse error (`Unexpected token 'R'…`) as a 500; now you see that answer and its status, as other requests to a machine already did.

## [0.55.0] - 2026-11-03

### Added

- **.env values are hidden on screen.** When a task's activity, a diff or Changes shows a `.env` or `.env.*` file (not `.env.example` or `.env.sample`), its values read `KEY=••••`: the edits wisp captured, the git diff in Changes, a diff or code block about one, and what a command like `cat .env` printed. *Reveal* above it shows that one view as it is, until *Hide*. The server masks before anything goes to the page, so the browser, the phone app and wisp Grid all get it; the file itself, rollback and Revert are untouched, and the agent still reads it. Turn it off with *Hide .env values* in Settings → Privacy (on by default). Spells: the `env.masked`, `env.reveal` and `settings.maskEnvFiles` parts and the `maskEnvFiles` setting.
- **Home says when plan allowance would go unused.** When a Claude Code or Codex account's weekly (or longer) window resets within a day with a fifth or more of it left, split view's home shows a row under Needs you: *38% of Codex’s weekly window left · resets Thu 21:00*, with *Use Codex*, which picks that harness and account in the prompt box for you to send. × hides it until that window resets. It's advice only: wisp never switches accounts or spends a reset by itself. Turn it off with *Spare allowance* in Settings → Usage (on by default). Spells: the `split.spare` part with its `hours` prop (24), `split.spare.start` (*Use …*), `settings.spareCard` and the `spareCard` setting.
- **wisp says when a plan's usage window changes.** When a Claude Code or Codex account's window appears, goes, changes its length or moves its reset off its schedule (*Codex’s weekly window now resets Sun 09:00 (was Thu 21:00)*), Settings → Usage shows a line in that account's block and split view's home a row under Needs you, each with × to hide it until that window changes again. The first sighting of an account announces nothing, the 5-hour window's moving reset never counts, and what wisp last saw is kept in `budget.json`, so a restart doesn't announce it again. Advice only: wisp never switches accounts. Turn it off with *Window changes* in Settings → Usage (on by default). Spells: the `split.windowNotice`, `settings.windowNotice` and `settings.windowNotice.line` parts and the `windowNotice` setting. API: `budget[].notices` on `GET /agents`, `POST /agents/:id/windows/dismiss`.

### Changed

- **Crew members and subagents can change the crew too.** `wisp_crew_add`, `wisp_crew_edit` and `wisp_crew_remove` work from a crew member's job and from a subagent, not only from a task you started, so a lead can hire help for a job. A member's job can't remove that member.

## [0.54.0] - 2026-11-02

### Added

- **Phone app: dictate a prompt.** In the Android app a mic next to the paperclip, in New task and in a task's reply box, types what you say into the box as you speak, using the phone's own speech recognition. Tap it again to stop. wisp asks for the microphone the first time; refused, it says how to allow it. Browsers and the desktop app are unchanged. Spells: the `composer.mic` and `launcher.mic` parts and the `mic` icon.
- **wisp says when a harness's program changed.** The first time wisp runs Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, opencode, Copilot or another harness it keeps where its program is, its size, sha256 and version. If that changes, the task and Settings → Agents say so (“Codex changed since 2 Nov: 0.1.2 → 0.1.3”) with *Trust this version*: a newer version in the same install folder is a plain line, another folder or the same version with other bytes is a warning. Nothing is blocked unless you turn on *Ask before running a changed harness* (Settings → Agents, off by default): then a run on a changed harness waits for *Trust and start* or *Skip this run*. Spells: the `harness.change` and `settings.harnessAsk` parts and the `harnessAsk` setting.

### Fixed

- **Codex limits are told apart.** Codex sometimes answers “429 Too Many Requests” while your plan still has room: with its usage windows known and none full, Auto now rests it one minute instead of ten, and tries it again once when nothing else can run. When Codex refuses because too many devices or sessions are signed in, the task says so and what to do (close Codex elsewhere, then Rerun) instead of a bare error. A real usage limit still shows the card where you pick wait, another account or hand-off; wisp never switches accounts by itself.
- **Claude Code no longer spends extra calls on progress summaries.** With `CLAUDE_CODE_AGENT_PROGRESS_SUMMARIES` exported in your shell, every Claude Code run wisp started (tasks and Take over terminals) made extra model calls to summarise its subagents' progress. wisp now leaves it out of the runs it starts; a Shell beside the task keeps your environment as it is.

## [0.53.0] - 2026-11-02

### Added

- **Phone app: attach a photo or a video straight from the camera.** In the Android app the paperclip in New task and in a task's reply box opens *Take photo*, *Record video* and *Choose files*: a photo or video you take goes in the tray and uploads like a picked file, with no camera permission to grant. Browsers and the desktop app are unchanged, apart from a video in the tray now showing a video icon. Spells: the `composer.attachMenu` part and the `camera` and `video` icons.

### Fixed

- **A terminal in wisp starts at its real size.** On a busy computer, a program started in a wisp terminal (Take over, `/terminal`, Shell) could see a 0×0 screen on its first look and draw itself wrong until the next resize: the size is now set before the program starts.

- **Changes keeps a long folder readable.** In the side panel's Changes, a file deep in a long folder squeezed the folder to a sliver that showed one dot of its ellipsis, so the row read as *./a-file…*; the folder now keeps room for *…/*.

## [0.52.0] - 2026-11-02

### Added

- **Side discussions: talk a task over on a page of its own.** Type **/side** and a message in a task's reply box (or pick **Side discussion…** in its More, its right-click menu or ⌘K) to open a side discussion about it: a task of its own, with full answers (blocks, apps, follow-ups) where /btw gives one quick reply under the turn. It reads the main task's folder as it is now, and every message you send it comes with the main task's latest state (what it's doing, what it changed, what it last said), so it can always check on the work, even while the main task runs. It changes nothing: it runs in Plan, anything that would change something is refused without asking, wisp's tools that act (launch, delegate, crew edits…) aren't offered, and its page has no Rerun, Take over, Ship it, revert, commit or run buttons for the main task's folder. Archiving the main task keeps its worktree while a side discussion runs; deleting it stops them. Its bar says *Side discussion of …* (click to go back), and the main task's says *N side discussions*. New `POST /api/sessions/{id}/side`; summaries carry `sideOf` and `sides`. Spells: the `side` action, the `card.sideOf`, `card.sides`, `card.sidePill` and `turn.sideStart` parts and the `discuss` icon.

### Fixed

- Goals: picking a judge's harness opens its picker over the Judges editor instead of behind it.

## [0.51.0] - 2026-11-02

### Added

- **Sandboxes: a task in a fresh box of its own.** Type **/sandbox** and a task in New task, and it runs in a new box on your wisp Grid instead of on this computer or in your cloud computer. Your project goes along as it is here, uncommitted changes included, so private repos need no login there. Deleting the task deletes its sandbox. Inside, the agent has root (`sudo apt-get`, under gVisor), and what it installs comes back when the sandbox wakes. It also has a real browser it drives through the new Browser connector. You watch that browser live, and can click in it, in the task's new **Browser** tab in the side panel. A project's `.wisp/sandbox.sh` runs once when a sandbox gets it. You sign in to Claude once for all sandboxes in **Settings → Cloud → Sandboxes** (in a cloud computer: **Settings → Connect**). wisp runs `claude setup-token`, and the token goes straight to your grid, sealed there and never kept on your computer. The same card has **Env vars** every sandbox starts with, and lists every sandbox with what it's for. Subagents can run in a sandbox of their own: pick **Sandbox** as a delegate's place. Crew members can run each job in a fresh sandbox, or keep one of their own: *Their jobs run on* → **A sandbox per job** / **A sandbox of their own**. Only Claude Code (or Auto) runs in sandboxes for now. Previews of a box's pages now load inside wisp on a computer too (their cookie works in a frame of another site). Grid: up to 10 sandboxes per user (`sandboxes.perUser`), 2.5 GB each (`hostd.container.sandbox`); see grid/README.md → Sandboxes. Spells: the `settings.cloud.sandboxes` and `panel.browser` parts, the `browser` panel tab and the `box` icon.

- **Drafts, and tasks that start after another.** Type **/draft** and a task in New task (or in any task's reply box) to save it without starting it: it waits in the new **Drafts** section, above *For later*. Click it to edit the prompt, then **Start now**, **Start later…** or **Delete**. Type **/after**, pick a task (a menu searches your tasks as you type; pasting a task's ID works too), then the prompt: the new task waits in *Scheduled* and starts once that task is marked done (at once if it already is). From a task's reply box it runs in that task's project, on its harness. If the task it waits for is deleted or archived first, it moves to Drafts. API: `draft: true` or `after: <task id>` instead of a time on `POST /later`. Spells: the `section.drafts` and `drafts.row` parts.

- **Run the task's app and see it beside the task.** The side panel has a **Preview** tab (after Shell) on a task with a worktree: **Run** starts the app from the worktree, with `PORT` set to a free port wisp keeps for the task, and shows it in the panel. The command comes from `run` in `.wisp/worktree.json` (with an optional `port`), else a guess from package.json (`npm run dev`, else `npm start`, said to be a guess), and you can edit it before you run. While it runs, a slim bar says where (*Running · localhost:41664*) with **Pick element**, **Logs**, **Restart**, **Stop** and **Open in browser**; until the app answers, its output shows in its place, and if it exits it says the exit code and its last lines with **Run again**. **Pick element** outlines what's under the pointer in the page; a click adds a line naming it (*In the preview, `<button class="primary">` "Save" (selector: `main > form > button.primary`):*) to the task's reply box. Stop ends everything the command started; deleting or archiving the task, or quitting wisp, stops it too. Also **Run app** / **Stop app** in the task's More, its right-click menu and ⌘K. New `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /api/sessions/{id}/run` and `GET /api/sessions/{id}/run/log`; spells reach it through the `panel.preview`, `panel.preview.bar`, `panel.preview.run`, `panel.preview.pick` and `panel.preview.logs` parts and `panel`'s `tab: "preview"`.
- **Start tasks from Slack, Linear or any webhook.** Automations → Triggers has three new kinds: **When a webhook is called**, **When someone mentions wisp in Slack** and **When a Linear issue is created**. Each gets a secret URL (**Copy**, **Regenerate**) to paste where the calls come from: anything that can POST (its JSON or form body fills the prompt through `{{body.ref}}` and `{{text}}`, optionally signed with an HMAC secret), your Slack app's Event Subscriptions (the signing secret is checked and old requests are refused; mentions and messages in one channel, optionally only with a keyword; `{{text}}`, `{{user}}`, `{{channel}}`), or a Linear webhook (its signature checked; issues created, optionally only with a label; `{{title}}`, `{{url}}`, `{{description}}`). They start in Plan mode, at most one task per trigger every 30 seconds, and the trigger's editor lists its last five calls with what came of them (started, skipped or rejected, and why). Off by default: Settings → Automations → **Webhook triggers**; off, the URLs answer 404. On wisp Cloud the URL works as it is; on your computer, put a tunnel in front of it. New `POST /api/hooks/{token}`; spells set the switch with `webhookTriggers` and reach the rows through the `settings.webhookTriggers`, `automations.hookUrl` and `automations.deliveries` parts.
- **Start a task from an issue.** New task has an **Issue** button at the end of its project row (in the phone app, in the bar after Delegate; behind *More options* until your first task) that lists the picked project's open GitHub issues, newest first: *#123 title*, its labels and who it's assigned to. Type to filter, or past the 50 newest to search GitHub. Picking one fills the prompt with *Fix #123: title*, the link and the issue's text, the caret at the end, so you can add to it before you send; a draft it replaces comes back with **Undo**. With a Linear connector that has an API key, the same picker lists the Linear issues assigned to you; without one it offers *Connect Linear…* (Settings → Connectors). When gh isn't installed or signed in, or the project has no GitHub remote, the picker says so with the button that fixes it. Also in ⌘K: *Start from an issue…*. New `GET /api/issues?project=&q=&source=github|linear`; spells reach it through the `launcher.issue` and `issues.picker` parts and the `issue` icon.
- **Stage and commit from Changes.** In a worktree task's **Changes**, each file has a **+** before Viewed to stage it (**−** on a tint once it's all staged, a dot on the **+** when part of it is), and each hunk a **Stage** / **Unstage** beside its Revert, so you pick what goes in a commit as in VS Code's Source Control. A commit row under the summary takes a one-line message (the task's title when you focus it empty), says *2 staged*, and **Commit** (Enter) commits those, or everything when nothing is staged; the toast says *Committed abc1234*. Every flip is instant and comes back with a red toast if git refuses; nothing is staged or committed while the agent works. ⌘K on a task has *Commit staged changes…*.
