Spells reach the toggle, the hunk's button and the commit row (`panel.changes.stage`, `panel.changes.stageHunk`, `panel.changes.commit`); the API is `POST /sessions/:id/changes/stage` and `/changes/commit`.
- **Review Changes like a pull request: revert a hunk or a file, tick files Viewed.** In the side panel's **Changes**, hover a hunk of a diff and click **Revert** on its first line (always shown on a phone) to put just that part back as it was before the task; a file's `⋯` has **Revert file…**, which asks first, then brings a changed or deleted file back from where the branch left the base, or removes a file the task added. Both happen at once with *Reverted* and **Undo** on the toast, which puts the file back exactly as it was. wisp won't revert while the agent is working on the task, or a part that changed since you looked (Changes reloads instead). Each file also has a **Viewed** box, as on GitHub: ticking it folds the file and dims its row, the summary counts *2 of 7 viewed*, and a file whose diff changes afterwards is unviewed again (kept per task in this browser). New `POST /api/sessions/{id}/changes/revert`; spells reach it through the `panel.changes.revertHunk`, `panel.changes.viewed` and `panel.changes.viewedCount` parts.
- **Worktrees bring what git ignores, and set themselves up.** A task's new worktree now gets the ignored files the repo's `.worktreeinclude` lists (gitignore-style lines, as Claude Code reads them; without one, the root's `.env` files), and runs the repo's setup commands from `.wisp/worktree.json` (`{ "setup": ["npm ci"] }`) or a Cursor `.cursor/worktrees.json` (`setup-worktree`), in the worktree, with `ROOT_WORKTREE_PATH` set to your checkout. A step that fails stops the rest and the task's branch line says *Worktree setup failed* (its tooltip says why); the branch's tooltip lists what was copied and run. Spells reach the warning through the `card.setupWarn` part.
- **Your plan's usage before you start.** The harness picker in New task (and a reply's) now says how much of each harness's plan is gone, its fullest window (*5h 40% used*), not only when it's near your line; the tooltip says when it resets.
- **@ a file in a prompt.** Typing `@` and part of a name in New task (with a project on this computer picked) or in a task's reply box now offers the folder's files too, under **Files**, after the crew and My Stuff; `/` in what you type looks in paths. Picking one puts its path in (`@src/auth.js`), the way the harnesses read a file mention. New `GET /api/files?project=`; spells reach it through the `mention.picker` and `mention.file` parts.
- **Jira in the issue picker, and Sentry and PagerDuty triggers.** New task's Issue picker lists the Jira issues assigned to you when a Jira or Atlassian connector has its site, email and API token (Jira's search with `assignee = currentUser()`, still open), beside GitHub and Linear; `source=jira` on `GET /api/issues`. Automations' new trigger offers **Sentry alert** and **PagerDuty incident**: a webhook trigger with its service's signature header (`Sentry-Hook-Signature`, `X-PagerDuty-Signature`, including PagerDuty's `v1=` signatures during a secret rotation) and a prompt that reads the alert or incident's title and link.
- **Language support in the editor.** With a language server for the file (TypeScript and JavaScript, Python, Go, Rust: found on your PATH, in the repo's `node_modules/.bin`, or installed by wisp), the editor asks it: ⌘-click or F12 goes to the exact definition, **⇧F12** lists every reference, resting the pointer on a name shows its type and docs, **F2** renames it across the task’s files, **Ctrl+Space** completes the word you’re typing, and problems show as you type: wavy line numbers in red or amber with the message as their tooltip, and **n problems** in the editor's head listing them. Without a server, definition and references use the text search, and the editor offers **Install TypeScript support** (or Python): `typescript-language-server` or `pyright`, into `~/.wisp/lsp`, once for every task. Renaming writes the files, so it waits for a saved file and an idle agent. New `POST /api/sessions/{id}/lsp` and `POST /api/lsp/install`; spells reach it through the `panel.editor.problems` and `panel.editor.hover` parts.
- **Search the task's files, and go to a definition.** The editor's **Open file…** (and ⌘P in the editor) finds text inside the files too: an **In files** group with each line, the words marked, opening the file at that line. In the editor, ⌘-click a name (Ctrl-click on Windows and Linux) or press F12 to open where it's defined: the one place at its line, or a list when several define it. New `GET /api/sessions/{id}/grep` (`q=` text, `def=` a name).
- **Find past work by what was said.** ⌘K now searches inside every task's prompts and answers, not only titles: type three letters or more and an **In prompts and answers** group follows the rest with the task, where the words were (*You asked* or *It answered*) and the line around them. Every word you type must be in one prompt or one answer. New `GET /api/search?q=`.
- **Open any file of a task.** Changes has **Open file…** (and ⌘K *Open a file…* on a task): the task folder's files by name, the ones it changed first, open in the editor. The lines the task added are marked in the editor's gutter, and the marks follow the lines you add or remove. New `GET /api/sessions/{id}/files`; spells reach it through the `panel.changes.open` part.
- **Edit a changed file in wisp.** Each file in the side panel's **Changes** has a pencil (and *Edit here* in its ⋯): the file opens over the list, highlighted, with line numbers, Tab and Shift+Tab to indent, and your browser's own undo. **Save** (⌘S) writes it to the task's folder (its worktree, or the project folder without one) at once, and Changes shows your edit in the diff. It won't save while the agent is working on the task (its next edit would land on yours), and if the file changed since you opened it, it offers **Load theirs** or **Keep mine** instead of overwriting. Esc goes back to Changes, asking first if you have unsaved work; switching to another task keeps the editor with its task. New `GET` and `PUT /api/sessions/{id}/source`; spells reach it through the `panel.changes.edit`, `panel.editor`, `panel.editor.code` and `panel.editor.save` parts.
- **A shorter New task on your first run.** Until you've sent a task, the box is the prompt, the mode, the harness and Send, with **More options** for the rest (another computer, compare, delegate, goal, worktree, start later). Once you open it or send a task, the full bar stays. Spells reach it through the `launcher.more` part.
- **Comment on a line of a diff, like a review.** In the side panel's **Changes**, hover a line of a diff and click the **+** that comes up over its sign (on a phone, tap the line number), say what should change, and the comment waits above the reply box with your other comments on the answer, numbered, with a mark on that line. Shift-click another line (on a phone, tap a second number while the card is open) to comment on the lines between. It goes out at the top of your next follow-up naming the file, the line numbers (as the file is now; a removed line by its old number, and it says so) and the quoted code, and the sent prompt shows it as a card (*server/auth.js:42–48*). Clicking it in the tray opens Changes on that file and flashes its mark. The tray now has **Send comments** while the box is empty. Spells reach it through the `panel.changes.comment`, `panel.changes.commentMark` and `composer.sendComments` parts.
- **Pull to refresh your computers.** On the Android app's first screen, pull the list down from the top and let go to ask your grids again at once, instead of waiting for the next refresh: a computer you just switched on shows *Online* right away. A circle follows your finger, fills as you pull, and spins until the grid answers.

- **Download on the phone.** The Android app now has **Download** on pictures and videos in answers, in the full-screen viewer, and on files in My Stuff (it was hidden there because the app couldn't save files). Pictures go to *Pictures/wisp* and videos to *Movies/wisp*, where the gallery shows them. Other files go to *Download/wisp*. No storage permission is needed. On Android 8 and 9 the file opens the share sheet instead. Over a cloud computer the download uses the app's sign-in. If the app is older than the computer's wisp, the button stays hidden.

- **Add a goal to a task that's already going.** The reply box has a **Goal** button (the target) beside the team picker. On a task without a goal it opens the same *Done when* line as New task: press Enter there to set the goal on its own (an idle task is checked at once), or send a message with it and the goal is checked after that follow-up's turn. While a goal is going the button is lit and opens **Edit goal…**; after one is met it starts a new one. Esc closes the line, and its text survives a reload. API: `goal: { text }` on `POST /sessions/:id/reply`. Spells: the `composer.goal` and `composer.goalLine` parts.

- **Judges for a goal.** A goal can have judges: up to five agents, picked like Delegate (any harness with its model and thinking level, or a crew member on theirs), that each check the work on their own after every round. Pick how their votes add up: **All** must say it's met, a **Majority**, or **Any** one. The goal is met only when they agree that way; *can't be done* takes a majority of them, and a judge that can't answer counts against All. Add them from the **Judges** button in the launcher's *Done when* line (remembered for the next goal, like delegates) or in *Set goal… / Edit goal…*. The goal band shows each judge's face tinted by its last vote and the count (*2/3*), its tooltip each judge's reason, and a round sent back says *2 of 3 judges say met (all must agree)* with what the dissenting judges want next. `goal: { text, judges, vote }` on `POST /sessions` and `PUT /sessions/{id}/goal` (judges left out keep the ones it had; `[]` clears them). Spells reach it through the `goal.judges` and `launcher.goalJudges` parts and the `judge` icon.
- **wisp on a slow connection.** The phone app's first open of a computer (and its first open after an update) no longer pays a round trip per file: wisp's ~120 modules come in one request that its service worker unpacks before the page loads them, and the 30 stylesheets are one file. On a 3G link (2 s round trips, 400 kbit/s) the task list shows in 18 s instead of 60 s, and in 6.7 s instead of 18.8 s at 600 ms and 1 Mbit/s. The page's scripts go without their comment lines (78 KB less over the wire, line numbers unchanged). Before the list is in, its rows show as placeholders the shape of the real ones, and a task opened before its copy arrives shows its bar, prompt and answer as placeholders (with its real status pills) instead of *Loading…*. The phone's saved snapshot keeps the tasks you read lately and the ones fetched ahead, so after Android stops the app they open at once. An open task is fetched once at a time (a running task's updates no longer pile up on a slow link, and an older answer can't paint over a newer one), and a task's load that hangs on a dead connection gives up after 45 s and tries again. Spells reach the placeholders through the `list.placeholder` and `split.placeholder` parts.

- **Waiting work goes when a plan resets early.** OpenAI hands out banked Codex resets that land with no notice, and until now work waiting for a plan (follow-ups held by *Automations stop at*, tasks waiting at a limit with *Wait for the reset*, runs held by *Ask near the limit*) waited for the reset time the CLI last gave, days later for a weekly window. Now, when a run's report or a live read shows a window reset early (much less used, and a new period before the old one ended), that work goes at once. Crew members and triggers skipped for the plan stop saying so, the phone hears how many tasks started, and the window's line in Plan budget reads *Reset early 10:12 · resets Sat 10:12*. Settings → Usage → **Check Codex while work waits** (off by default): while work waits on a Codex account, wisp asks Codex's app-server how its windows stand every 10 minutes (`account/rateLimits/read`: no model turn, nothing spent; it never redeems a reset credit). Claude Code has no such read, so an early Claude reset is seen with its next run. Codex's session files can end with a line for another limit (`premium`) with no windows; wisp now skips it instead of reading no windows. Spells set it with `liveLimits` and reach the row through `settings.liveLimits`.
- **Automations stop at, per harness.** Settings → Usage → Plan budget now has a row for Claude Code and one for Codex under *Automations stop at*: **Same** (follow the row above), Off, 50%, 75% or 90%. OpenAI is moving ChatGPT and Codex to one shared allowance, so a lower Codex line (say 75% while Claude Code stays at 90%) leaves the rest for your own chats. Each account's bars mark its own harness's line. Spells set them with `autoStopClaude` and `autoStopCodex`, and reach the rows through the `settings.autoStopBy` part.
- **Drain watch.** Settings → Usage → *Drain watch* (off by default) warns when an account's plan suddenly moves much faster for the same work: when its runs in the last two hours took twice or more the share of a window per unit of work (Claude Code's reported cost, else tokens) that its runs took over the two weeks before. The account's block in Plan budget gets a card: *Using your plan 3.1× faster than usual · Since 14:05, 4 runs took 18% of the weekly window for $2.10 of work. That much work usually takes 6%. 1 of them ended in an error and still took 3%.* **Copy report** puts the numbers in words for a support ticket, and **Dismiss** hides the card until a later run shows the drain again. With ntfy or Telegram set up, your phone hears of it too, at most once every two hours per window. New `POST /api/agents/{id}/drain/dismiss`; spells set it with `drainWatch` and reach it through the `settings.drainWatch` and `budget.drain` parts.
- **Addresses on a map.** Agents can answer with a `map` block: one or more places (street addresses, or coordinates) on a Google map in the answer, each with **Open in Google Maps** (the Maps app on a phone). With several, the list under the map picks which one it shows, numbered so a typed answer can name one, and `"route": true` adds **Route**: directions through them in order. Exports and chat apps get the places as Google Maps links. Spells reach it through the `map`, `map.head`, `map.stage` and `map.list` parts.

- **Babysitting mode (Terminals).** Settings → General → *Babysitting mode (Terminals)*: each task you send opens straight in the agent's own CLI, in a terminal inside wisp, already working on your prompt, instead of wisp's cards. wisp still makes its folder, worktree and title, and keeps the prompt as its turn. A follow-up from wisp's box is typed into the terminal; once the terminal ends, opening the task (or a follow-up) resumes the same conversation (Claude Code's is named by wisp with `--session-id`). Schedules, crew jobs, spells, goals, teams and compare runs still run in wisp. Off by default; spells set it with `terminalSessions`. With it off, start a prompt with **`/terminal`** (offered in the new-task box's `/` menu) and that one task starts and opens in its terminal; the CLI, the API and the phone read it too. The task opens the moment you send it, on its terminal's *Starting up* screen, rather than after its folder and worktree are ready.
- **wisp blocks off.** Settings → General → *wisp blocks*: off, agents answer in plain markdown instead of a `wisp` block of cards, and wisp shows it without the *answered in plain text* note. Crew jobs and spells keep their blocks (wisp acts on them). Spells set it with `blocks`.
- **See what a task changed, beside it.** The side panel has a **Changes** tab: every file the task changed since its branch left the base (committed or not, new files included), with A/M/D/R, `+n −n` in fixed columns, and each file's diff opening in place under its row (several at once, **Expand all**). Lockfiles and build output fold into one row; a file's `⋯` has *Open in editor* and *Copy path*. It follows the agent while it works, patching rows in place. A task without a worktree shows the edits wisp captured instead, and says so. Open it from the task's **Changes** button, `D`, *More*, right-click, ⌘K *Show changes*, or *All changes in this task* under a turn's files. Once the panel is open, its head is now tabs (**Changes · PR · Shell**, and **Watch** once there's a page), so you move between them without going back to the task; the place line says *3 uncommitted* (was *3 changed*). New `GET /api/sessions/{id}/changes`; spells reach it through the `panel.changes`, `panel.changes.summary`, `panel.changes.list` and `split.changes` parts, `panel.tab: "changes"` and `panel.changes.generated`.
- **Share a task, and import one.** Share… (in a task's menu and its More) puts the task on your wisp Grid at a link: anyone with it opens the task in a browser, drawn the way wisp draws a task page (the same reports, delegates and details column), or only people with the passkey you set, each delegated run on a page of its own. Someone signed in to the same grid pastes the link in New task: it shows as a card (who shared it, its turns, delegates, crew, connectors and skills, and what carries it on here), and Enter imports it with its history, work (branch and uncommitted changes, into their checkout of the repo), team and delegates' runs, the crew members it delegated to, its connectors (a command one starts switched off) and skills. A harness or model they don't have is swapped for the nearest one, and the card says so and lets them pick another. Secrets in the conversation are redacted; connectors never take their keys. Spells reach it through the `launcher.share` and `share.dialog` parts.
- **Phone app: swipe a crew member's thread to the left for its About page.** The column a wide pane shows beside the thread (schedule, delegates, notebook, brief, kit) comes in under your finger as a page of its own, the same way a task's side panel does; drag it to the right, tap Back or press the phone's Back to return to the thread. Spells turn it off with `crew.page.swipe: false` and restyle it through the `crew.about` part.
- **Phone app: swipe a task to the left for its Changes, PR and Shell.** On a task's page, drag to the left and the side panel comes in under your finger, on the tab you last looked at; drag it to the right to go back to the task. It follows the finger, the task slides and dims under it, and letting go past 40% or flicking finishes the move at your speed, otherwise it springs back. Diffs and the shell's key row keep their own sideways scroll, and the shell doesn't bring up the keyboard. Spells turn it off with `panel.swipe: false`.
- **My Stuff in the phone app's top bar.** The app's floating New task hides the row that held the My Stuff button, so My Stuff was only reachable through Search. It now has its own button beside Search. Spells reach it through the `stuff.topButton` part.

### Fixed

- **`/terminal` no longer refuses a task with a goal, a team or a crew member.** Typing `/terminal …` with *Done when* set, delegates picked, compare runs added or `@Ada` in the prompt used to show an error and keep the prompt. Now it starts the task in your terminal and leaves those out: you drive the agent there yourself. It's still refused where no terminal can open (Windows, or a harness that can't resume its sessions).

- **Changes and the editor, said in words.** A file's stage toggle in Changes says *Stage*, *Part* or *Staged* in Viewed's style (a bare +/− beside the +3 −2 counts read as one), and on a phone a file's Stage · Viewed · ⋯ sit on a line under its name so the name stays whole. An empty commit message shows the one Commit will use (the task's title). The harness picker's plan use is plain grey, amber only near your line. The editor's language support offer is a quiet *Add TypeScript support* in its head instead of a note that pushed the code down; hover cards sit above the name's line; references and definitions say *3 references to add*. Preview's Pick and the app's Issue have their words; the issue picker keeps one height from loading to loaded; trigger kinds are named like the others (*Webhook called*, *Slack mention*, *Linear issue created*).
- **Changes and the task page, tidied.** Each hunk in a diff has a head row like git's `@@` line (*Line 12*, and Revert), so Revert never sits on code; Viewed says its word on a phone too; Revert file… names the file it changes. The overlap notice above the reply box is one line with *+3 more*. wisp's rounds of a goal say which round they are (*Round 3: what's still missing*), a step quieter than your prompts. The editor shows Save only when there's something to save. Lists in Settings and dialogs open wisp's own menu on a computer (a phone keeps its own picker).
- **The task's bar says its names on a narrow page.** Wherever the task is too narrow for its details column (a phone, a window under 900px, the side panel open) its actions wrap under its facts with their names instead of bare icons beside labelled ones, and the branch takes the line above them, whole. On a phone the bar is one row (*Changes, Ship it…, Mark done, More*), with *Transcript*, *Rerun…* and *Take over* at the top of More (and nothing in More that the page already shows), so the first turn starts higher. Each of these actions now has one name everywhere, the side panel's tab names: *Changes*, *Shell*, *Take over* and *Transcript* in the bar, More, the right-click menu, ⌘K and the keys sheet (they were also *Show changes*, *Open shell*, *Take over in a terminal* and *Show the full transcript*). Side by side, the reply box keeps its bar when you click elsewhere, so the notices above it no longer jump 30px each time. The goal's bar never draws full before the goal is met (it showed 100% beside *0/1* and *Stalled*), and a paused goal's line says why it paused without repeating the check that the round wisp sent back already shows.
- **Labels read whole, one type scale again.** A task row's third line no longer cuts to *Claude Code · Default mo… · This compu…*: it names the model only when you picked one (its tooltip still says *Default model*), and a crew member's details drop it the same way. On a phone, a task's list of turns shows each prompt whole instead of *Make the tests p…* beside *Fake claude fin…*, and the goal's check line wraps to two lines instead of ending in *not_…*. A crew member's role takes two lines on its Org card. The 70 font sizes that had drifted off the scale (12px, 13.5px, 11px) are back on 11.5 / 12.5 / 13 / 14, so a screen has at most five sizes. ⌘K opens on your tasks first, with the crew under them; a crew member's chores (delegates, share, track record) show once you type. The row's hover *Save for later* is no longer a button inside the row's button (screen readers met a nested control; L and More still have it), and the time on a round wisp sent back reads at AA contrast. The task's bar says *Ship it…* and *Push to PR #12*, the words its menus and ⌘K use (it said *Ship it*, *Push to #12* and *Ship again*). A turn from today shows its time alone (*23:49*). On a phone, the overlap notice above the reply box is one row and *+ n more* (two rows covered a quarter of the answer), and in the app every control takes a 44px tap (switches, segments, fields, chips, dialog buttons, the mini bar), without moving anything. Screen readers get a name for every switch, list and field in Settings, every dialog's ×, and the harness picker's lists; Settings' tabs and the Run on menu hold only what those roles allow, and a link in a sentence is underlined. Removed lines in a diff no longer fade (their code fell under AA contrast). A comment's highlight on a diff line lights its number and sign, so the line keeps its added or removed colour, and on a phone the comment sheet sits square at the bottom. The phone app's Settings no longer offers *Open files in*, which only a desktop can do. Settings' headings are one size, and a bare `<small>` no longer drops to 10.4px.
- **Delegate faces no longer cover Send on the phone.** When the New task bar scrolled under the Send button, the delegates' faces showed on top of it; Send now covers them.
- **Round spinners in the Android app.** The spinner on **Opening** was squeezed into a flat oval (the button was too narrow for it and the word); it stays round now, and every spinner on the computers screen is an arc on a faint ring.

- **Buttons line up, in every state.** Lit, hovered and focused buttons had uneven boxes. Goal turned on in the prompt box was a 26×30 sliver on a phone, and 30px tall beside 32px Attach and Start later; Worktree, Delegate and the place were 32×30. In a task's reply box Attach and Start later were taller than Send; the sidebar's search was 32×30 beside 32×32 buttons; Cancel (28px) sat beside Save (30px) in every dialog; a crew member's Pause folded to a 36×30 oval on a narrow window, their Remove was 30×28 and their reply bar's buttons were shorter than its Send; Auto's logo sat off-centre on a phone and when comparing; a turn's tools and thumbs were 24×22; the task's icon actions on a narrow window were 28×24; the project bar's + was 28×26 and Other folder… as its icon an oval; a project's colour and remove buttons were 26 and 32px; My Stuff's view toggles were 28×24. Each is now a square, or one height per bar, and in the phone app the logo-only harness picks, Other folder… and My Stuff's options are full 44px targets again. `node qa/buttons.mjs` checks every button at rest, hovered and focused in 36 views, at three widths, light and dark, and in the phone app.
- **The Org chart pans on its own on a phone.** A chart wider than the screen (cards placed on a wider one) now scrolls sideways by itself; the page, the hint above it and the member's details under it stay put.
- **wisp opens and answers much sooner.** With 770 tasks: the desktop app shows your tasks about 0.95 s after you open it (was 3.7 s), `wisp` serves its page in 0.2 s (was 1.75 s), and the server holds 33 MB instead of 155 MB. A start reads a small light copy of each task (`sessions/.light/`) and leaves its turns' activity, file changes and raw answers on disk until something reads them (opening the task, a follow-up); the list and the usage stats never need them. The task list keeps each task's summary until the task changes, and an unchanged list is sent from the last one (0.7 ms instead of 15 ms). Saves are written in the background. After a restart wisp no longer checks every worktree's git status at once (thousands of `git` processes that held the server for seconds): a few at a time, newest first, once the page has loaded, with 3 `git` calls each instead of 5. The desktop app starts on the PATH it read last time and asks your login shell again behind it (half a second or more on every open), and wisp finds git without asking `xcrun`.
- **Launching a task doesn't wait on the network.** While you type, wisp fetches the project's repo (`POST /api/launch/warm`), so Enter starts the agent in its worktree at once instead of after a `git fetch` (half a second or more to GitHub).
- **Smaller answers, everywhere.** Big API answers go brotli-compressed to browsers (a 2 MB task: 134 KB instead of 540 KB gzipped, and faster to make), and the page's files at brotli's best, so the phone app and wisp Grid move a quarter of the bytes. The page asks for its first lists while its scripts load instead of after, asks for the machines once instead of twice, skips laying out the rows of the list that are off screen, and a dialog fading out no longer swallows the click you make under it. `node qa/desktop-perf.mjs` times the page in a desktop browser (load, opening tasks, New task, Settings, search, launch), as `qa/mobile-perf.mjs` does for the phone app.

- **Buttons sit where they should on phones.** In the phone app, Crew's **Org** sat 3px below the heading's line (a desktop rule loaded after the touch one), and the top bar's My Stuff icon sat 4px right of its ring. In phone browsers, a task's buttons (Transcript, Rerun, Take over, Changes, Ship, Done, More) sat at the left of their 44px tap areas, and Crew's **Org** and **+** sat 8px below the heading; section headings there now take a thumb (44px), as in the app.
- **Phone app: the follow-up box's Delegate button is centred.** With a crew member (or one delegate) picked, their face sat at the left of the 44px button with empty space on its right; the computer button had the same offset.
- **The phone app stays quick with thousands of tasks and on a bad connection.** Stress-tested with 4,000 tasks, a task of 400 turns, a 300 KB answer and a 130 KB prompt, at 600 ms per request and 1 Mbit/s: opening, Back, search and typing stay under 150 ms. A long task arrives with only its latest 8 turns whole (the older ones fill in when you unfold one: 64 KB instead of 2 MB); a long answer or prompt is laid out only where you read it; rows scrolled past aren't restyled on every page change; coming back, the list brings only what changed (3 KB instead of 680 KB); and when the network returns, the open task and the live updates reconnect at once instead of at the next retry.
- **Buttons in the phone app react as you touch them.** The pressed look used to show only once your finger lifted, 130–210 ms after the touch; now a button shows it within a frame and a task row after about 90 ms (a beat later, so starting a scroll doesn't flash it), as native buttons do.
- **Faster page changes in the phone app, most of all away from home.** Opening a task, Back and New task take a third of the work they did (measured with `node qa/mobile-perf.mjs` on a slowed phone CPU: Back about 420 ms → 30 ms, opening a task about 165 ms → 75 ms). Several style rules made any change to a row restyle the whole page, the project chips refit on every Back, and the phone now holds 20 finished tasks at a time instead of 60 (more come as you scroll). Over a slow tunnel: the tasks on screen are fetched ahead while you look at the list, so opening one shows it at once; a task is downloaded once per open instead of twice and without each edit's whole file from before it; and a task or list that hasn't changed comes back as a bodiless 304.
- **Terminals inside wisp feel like a native terminal.** A big paste (or typing while the agent starts up) no longer hangs the terminal: the pty helper waited on the paste going in while the program waited on its output going out. Keys go out without waiting for the last one to arrive, in order, so on a cloud computer or a slow link each key shows about one round trip later instead of two. Output is read about twice as fast (scrolling logs 2.4×, full-screen TUIs 1.9×), and reopening a terminal with a long history paints in about 100 ms instead of 300 (the older scrollback fills in while idle). Frames from programs that use synchronized output (Codex) are never shown half drawn, programs that ask are told when the terminal gets and loses focus (Claude Code, Codex), and the server no longer copies the whole kept output on every chunk it prints.
- **Babysitting mode's terminal fits its pane.** A task's CLI now starts at the size of the pane it opens in, so its first screen (banner, rules, boxes) isn't drawn 100 columns wide and left that way. On a phone, and after the window crossed 700px, the terminal measured its cells in the wrong font size: lines stopped short of the right edge (47 columns where 52 fit) or ran past it; it measures the font it really uses now. A quick burst of resizes could leave the CLI at a size it passed through; the last one wins now. Full-screen CLIs that paint their own background (opencode, Grok) fill the box to its edges instead of sitting in a lighter frame, and the task's header keeps its buttons at the pane's right edge with the list hidden. The measuring, resize and edge fixes also apply to Take over's terminal and the side panel's Shell.
- **A key after a terminal session ends resumes it, and nothing else.** As the CLI exited, the task moved from Working to Needs you and the terminal lost the keyboard, so the key meant to resume it ran wisp's shortcut instead (`a` archived the task, `e` marked it done). Moving a task between sections keeps its focus now.
- **Take over keeps its transcript when wisp was started from Claude Code.** A wisp started from a Claude Code terminal (`npm run dev:web` there) passed that session's markers to the terminals it opens, so the claude in them kept no transcript and couldn't be resumed (*No conversation found*). They're dropped now, as they already were for wisp's own runs, and there are more of them.
- **Take over without the JSON.** A Claude Code session you take over is now told to answer you in plain text, so its replies in the terminal no longer end in a `wisp` block meant for wisp's page.
- **Collapsing a file's diff in a report's Files.** Once a file's diff was open, clicking its chevron (or the row) tried to open the file in the editor instead of closing the diff. On a shared task's page, Open in editor did nothing at all: there's no editor there, so it's no longer shown.
- **Updating a published app on a Windows grid.** Publishing a new version right after someone opened the link could fail with *Something went wrong on the grid*: Windows still held the file the visitor had just read, so the app's folder couldn't be swapped. The grid now waits a moment and tries again.
- **A quick query in an app's data isn't stopped on a busy computer.** When wisp (or the grid) was too busy to read the answer in time, a query that took a millisecond could fail with *That query took over 5 s and was stopped*, and the database restarted. The limit now counts from when the query starts, and is checked only after any answer already waiting is read.
- **Coming up's rows line up.** On the home page the first row under Coming up sat a little to the left of the others, and the rows were shorter than Needs you's: they picked up the styles of the open task's "delegated from" trail, which uses the same class name. Those styles now stay in the trail.

## [0.50.0] - 2026-10-30

### Added

- **Open a delegate's task by itself.** A delegate's live row and its report's **→** (or **Open @… task**) open its whole run in the main pane, a subagent nested under its lead in the list, a crew member's job under the member. The head names the tasks above it (*Lead › Ada › job*; deeper chains fold into **…**), each a click away, and Esc, × or Back go up one level to the task you came from. A subagent has no follow-up box of its own: its dock sends you to the lead. Links to a subagent (`#/s/<id>`) open it under its lead. Spells reach it through the `delegate.go`, `delegate.row`, `delegate.dock` and `split.trail` parts.
- **Watch a running app.** `wisp_watch` can also show the real thing: an agent building a web app opens its dev server's address (`url`) beside the task, then `navigate`s to the page it just changed (a path like `/settings`, or another address) and `reload`s when hot reload misses an edit, while the caption says what it's doing. The caption line shows the address, with a button to open it in its own tab. It loads like a preview block (sandboxed, and on a wisp Grid at your box's preview address).
- **Publish an app from My Stuff to your wisp Grid.** Signed in to a grid (Settings → Cloud), or in your cloud wisp, an app's **⋯** menu and its bar while it runs offer **Publish to `<grid>`**: its files go up to the grid, which serves them at `<app>--app--<you>.<grid>` to anyone with the link, no sign-in, while your computer or cloud wisp sleeps (kept out of search engines). The link is copied, the card shows **Public** (click to copy it again), and once an agent changes the app **Update public link** puts the new version at the same address. **Unpublish** takes it down; removing the app from My Stuff does too, and your grid account page lists what you published, with Unpublish. New `POST`/`DELETE /api/stuff/{id}/publish`; spells reach it through the `stuff.public`, `stuff.frame.publish` and `stuff.block.public` parts.
- **Agents publish for you.** Ask *“publish it”* or *“deploy this so I can share it”* and the agent saves the app with `wisp_save` `publish: true` (or an id alone, for one already in My Stuff): it answers with the public link, and the app's card in the reply shows it with **Copy link**. `publish: false` takes it down.
- **Apps can keep data.** An app whose `wisp-app.json` says `"db": true` (or `"db": "seed.sql"`, made from that file the first time) gets its own SQLite database, used from its pages with `import { sql } from '/_wisp/db.js'`. Launched in wisp it lives beside the app and survives updates (**⋯ → Reset data** starts it over); published, the grid keeps one for everyone who opens the link (10 MB). Queries run in a separate process that's stopped after 5 s, without ATTACH or PRAGMA. Needs Node 22.13+.
- **Launch My Stuff apps in the phone app.** Connected through wisp Cloud (your cloud computer, or one of your computers via the grid), **Launch** opens the app inside the app, as on desktop, instead of saying apps need a browser. Over SSH it still can't reach the app's port and says so.

- **Watch agents work.** On long tasks (a migration across many files, a test or benchmark run, a data job) the agent can put a page beside the task with the new `wisp_watch` tool and keep it current as it goes, so you follow the work instead of waiting for the answer. The agent decides what it shows (a progress bar and checklist, a table filling in, a chart) and sends its progress as it works; the page updates in place without reloading. It opens by itself in the side panel when you're looking at the task, under a caption line saying what the agent is doing now, with **Run it yourself** under it (the command to run or check it, copied with its folder). Elsewhere, the task's **Watch** button, its working card, *More*, the right-click menu and ⌘K open it; on a phone it's a page with Back. The page runs sealed (no network, no wisp API; scripts and styles from https CDNs load) and stays on the task with its last state once the agent is done. Settings → **Watch agents work** turns it off. New `GET /api/sessions/{id}/watch`; spells reach it through the `split.watch`, `panel.watch`, `watch.caption` and `watch.run` parts, `panel.tab: "watch"`, the `watch` setting and the `eye` icon.

- **Agents drive wisp.** Ask the agent in a task to *“have codex review this”*, *“run the tests on my devbox”* or *“start a task to update the docs”* and it does: `delegate` now creates subagents on the fly on any harness, model, thinking level or machine (no team needed at launch; they join the task's team, show on its card, and naming one again continues it), `wisp_available` tells it what harnesses, machines, projects and presets you have, `wisp_launch` starts a new task (here or on a machine, with its own team) that shows as started by the asking task, and `wisp_task` follows up, stops, marks done, archives, renames or waits on another task. Subagents can't, at most 8 tasks start per turn, and a chain of agent-started tasks stops 3 deep. The external `wisp mcp` gains `machine` on `wisp_launch` (and on each subagent) and lists your machines in `wisp_agents`.

### Grid

- `POST /_grid/preview?port=&path=` on a wisp's or computer's address: a one-time link into its `p<port>--` preview, for a page signed in there but not to the grid (the phone app's).
- Published apps (`grid/sites.js`): `PUT`/`DELETE`/`GET /api/sites` with a device token, or the cloud wisp's own secret; files under `<data>/sites/`, served at `<slug>--app--<handle>.<domain>` (on-demand TLS allows those names). `grid.json` `"sites": { "enabled", "maxBytes", "perUser", "dbBytes" }` (on, 50 MB, 20 apps, 10 MB). Deleting an account deletes its published apps and their data.

### Fixed

- **Watch and previews in the phone app through wisp Grid.** A page on your computer's localhost (a running app in Watch, a preview block) stopped at the grid's sign-in, which can't be shown in a frame (*Webpage not available*). They now start at a one-time link that signs the frame in, as My Stuff apps do.
- **Interactive answers follow your computer's light or dark switch** while they're open, with the theme on *System*; they kept the colours they opened with until a reload.
- **Removing an app from My Stuff on Windows** no longer fails with *directory not empty* right after the app was running: wisp retries while Windows lets go of the files it just served, without holding them open itself.
- **Removing an app that keeps data** (`"db"` in its `wisp-app.json`) no longer fails on Windows with *resource busy or locked*: its database is closed before its file goes.

## [0.49.0] - 2026-10-29

### Added

- **Edit what's waiting before it goes.** A task waiting under *Scheduled* (from **Wait until …** near the usage limit, or Start later) opens as a page when you click it: change its prompt there (saved on ⌘Enter or when you click away), or Start now, Change start time… and Delete. Queued follow-ups and ones scheduled for a time get a pencil on hover to change them in place (Enter saves, Esc keeps the old text), and with two or more queued you drag one by its grip, or use **Move up** / **Move down** in its menu, to change the order they go out in. On hover a queued follow-up also offers **Send now**, which interrupts the run with it, and **Combine with the next one** (its menu has **Combine all into one**), so several go out as one message. On a phone, tap a row for the same menu. New `PATCH /api/sessions/{id}/queue` (`{ item, text }`, `{ order }`, `{ merge }` or `{ item, now: true }`); spells reach it through the `plan.head` slot and the `plan.page`, `plan.prompt`, `plan.details` and `composer.queued` parts.
- **My Stuff: a shelf for what your agents make.** Pictures, videos, docs, code, files and links you keep from tasks, on one page in the main pane (the tray beside **New task**, or *My Stuff* in ⌘K; a page with Back on phones). Every picture, video, doc and code block in a reply has a **Save to My Stuff** button beside Copy and Download; it lights up once saved, and clicking it again takes it out. Agents keep things for you too: ask *“save that to my stuff”* and they call `wisp_save`, and `wisp_stuff` lets them find and use what you kept (*“use the logo from my stuff”*). Each thing is a copy, so it stays when the task, its worktree or `/tmp` goes. Cards preview the thing itself (the picture, the video playing on hover, a doc's opening as a page, a snippet's first lines, a link's site), with where it came from. Search titles, notes, #tags, tasks and a doc's text; filter by kind or star; group by time or by task; grid or list. Star, rename, note and tag things, open them in the viewer or reader, download, show in folder, or remove them with Undo. Drop or paste files on the page to add your own; ⌘K finds each thing by name. Spells reach it through the `stuff.head` slot, the `stuff.*` parts and the `stuff.page` props (`view`, `group`, `cardWidth`).
- **What an agent saved shows in its answer.** A new `stuff` block puts a card for it in the reply: its picture, what it is, and **Launch** for an app (it opens inside wisp) or **Open** for the rest. Agents are told to add it, and wisp adds one itself for anything saved that turn the answer leaves out.
- **Save to My Stuff from full screen.** The picture and video viewer's bar has the same Save to My Stuff button as the reply, next to Copy and Download; it follows ← → through the reply's pictures and lights up with the reply's own.
- **@ things from My Stuff in any prompt.** Type `@` in the prompt box or a follow-up and the picker offers your crew, then what you kept, with a small picture of each; pick one and the agent gets its file (or folder, or link), a doc's text, and how to update it. **Use in a new task** on a card does the same from My Stuff. A chip in a sent prompt opens the thing.
- **Agents update what you kept.** `wisp_save` with an item's id saves a new version in place: same card, title, star and tags, a *v2* on it, and the version before kept, so **⋯ → Restore** puts it back (with Undo). `wisp_stuff` with an id reads one in full. When an agent saves or updates something while you're elsewhere, a toast says so, with **Open** (or **Launch**).
- **Apps in My Stuff.** For bigger things (slides, a demo, a small tool) agents build a static web app and save its folder with `wisp_save` `app` (and a screenshot as its `cover`). Its card shows the screenshot in a little window with **Launch**: wisp serves the app on a port of its own on 127.0.0.1, the same one each time so the app keeps its storage, and opens it inside wisp, full window, with Reload, New tab, Stop and Close in a bar on top (on wisp Cloud, at that port's preview address). It shows *Running* while it's up and stops after 30 minutes idle, or with **Stop**. Apps have their own origin, so they can't use wisp's API. HTML files in My Stuff now always download instead of opening in wisp's page.
- **Org: your whole crew on one page.** A small **Org** button on the *Crew* heading (and *Org* in ⌘K) opens a chart of who hands work to whom in the main pane (a page with Back on phones). Each member is a card, with arrows going to their delegates; agents on other harnesses show as logos on the card. Select a member and the rest of the chart becomes their picker: **+** on a card makes that member their delegate and **✓** takes them off. With a mouse you can also drag from a card's handle onto another. The column beside the chart lists who they hand work to and get work from, each with a ×, and **Add a delegate…** adds another member or an agent on any harness. Changes save at once and roll back with a toast if refused. **Open thread** opens the member, and Back returns to the chart. **Delegates…** (in a member's menu, ⌘K, or the delegates line on their page) opens Org with that member selected. Spells reach it through the `org.head` slot and the `crew.org` and `org.*` parts.
- **Org: arrange the chart yourself.** Drag a card anywhere on the Org page (on a phone, select it, then drag it; or move a focused card with the arrow keys, Shift for bigger steps). The others stay where they are, the arrows follow, and the arrangement is saved on your wisp, so it's the same after a reload and on your other devices. Someone added later lands under the rest. **Reset layout** in the header goes back to the automatic tiers (with Undo). A spell can keep the automatic layout with `org.chart` `movable: false`, and restyle or hide `org.reset`.

### Changed

- **Tasks start and answer sooner.** With Claude Code on Haiku, a quick question in a git project went from 2.6 s to 2.2 s (median of 8), and wisp's own part of it from 0.74 s to 0.22 s. wisp runs git by its full path (a bare `git` cost about 50 ms a call on macOS, and every task makes several), shows a Codex answer as soon as the turn completes instead of waiting about 0.4 s for codex to exit, and shares one `git fetch` between tasks started within a minute of each other in the same repo.
- **The retro costs far less.** On Claude Code it runs with its own system prompt and no tools, MCP servers, skills or saved session: a real retro went from 28k input tokens to 3k ($0.06 to $0.009 on Haiku) with the same reply. On Codex it runs on the smallest model at low effort, with codex's plugins, apps, subagents and browser off and a one-line base prompt (its request went from 74 KB to 31 KB). A one-shot task that just worked, with no follow-up, failure or question, gets no retro at all.
- **Quick answers come back as they are.** A fact, a number or a yes/no is plain text instead of a full report, so the agent doesn't spend a second writing blocks around "13".
- **Shorter instructions for the agent.** The branch-graph and interactive-app blocks are named in one line each, and the agent reads their fields with the new `wisp_blocks` tool when it uses one. Notes about wisp's stats, crew and connector tools are left to the tools' own descriptions. Instructions shrink by a quarter (14.7k to 11.1k characters). Codex 0.53 and later gets them as developer instructions, ahead of the task's folder, so they are cached from one task to the next.
- **Auto no longer runs your tests after the agent answers.** An Auto task used to stay in *Working* while wisp ran the project's tests itself, sent new failures back to the agent for a repair round and handed the task to a stronger pick if they still failed. The agent runs the tests it needs, so the task now lands in your queue as soon as it answers. Settings → Auto loses *Check with the tests* and *Repair rounds* (the `check` and `repairs` fields of `PATCH /api/router` are gone), and task rows no longer get the *Tests* / *N failing* pill. Auto still hands a run that errored to a stronger pick, moves on at a usage limit, and judges best of two by reading both diffs. The test command per project stays: the scout hands it to the agent and a goal check runs it.
- **Goals keep going until they're met.** A goal no longer has a number of rounds: wisp checks after every round and sends the task back until the check says the goal holds, can't be met, or the task goes in circles (two rounds that change nothing or get the same next step pause it). The rounds chips are gone from the prompt box's *Done when* line and the Set goal popover, the band says *Round 3* and the row shows the round, and **Resume goal** on a stalled goal gives it a fresh look. API: `goal.max` on `POST /sessions` and `PUT /sessions/:id/goal` is ignored. Spells: the `goalRounds` setting is gone; a spell that sets it still loads, with a warning.

### Fixed

- **Stop no longer throws away your queued follow-ups.** They stay above the reply box, marked *Held after Stop*, and nothing goes out by itself: the next message you send (or **Send now** on one of them) carries the queue on.
- **Goal in the prompt box shows when it's on, and starts off every time.** The Goal button lights up while the prompt carries a goal, and it no longer stays on for the next prompt: each one starts without a goal until you pick Goal (it comes back on if the launch fails).
- **A /btw question scrolls into view when you send it.** Side questions sit at the end of the turn they were asked during, so on a long task the one you'd just sent could land under the follow-up box or below the fold. The task now scrolls just enough to show it, clear of the box, and stays put if it already shows. The same goes for an `@Name` question to a crew member and a reply in their thread.
- **Typing on home no longer lags a frame.** The pulsing ring on home's *Reach this wisp from anywhere* tip animated a shadow, which repainted the page every frame and kept each keystroke in the launcher about two frames (32 ms) from the screen. The ring now runs on the compositor and rests while you type, so a keystroke shows within a frame (8 ms at the median).
- **The Transcript button shows when it's on again.** Since the compact row that pins on long tasks, switching to the full transcript lit up that row's copy of the button and left the task's own Transcript button looking off.

## [0.48.0] - 2026-10-29

### Changed

- **Task rows say what ran them and where.** Each row in the task list has a third line under the summary: the harness's logo and name, the model, and the machine (*This computer* or the machine's name), so you can tell runs apart without opening them. On a narrow list the model shortens first, then the machine. A spell can hide or restyle it as `card.run`; the compact density hides it with the summary.
- **Swiping a task left in the phone app saves it for later instead of archiving it.** The band says *Save for later* (*Unsave* on a saved task) and the row moves to *For later*, with Undo. Rows that can't be saved yet (still running, waiting on you) only give a little. A spell can bring back archive with `card.swipeLeft: "archive"`.
- **Home's *Coming up* opens in full.** Past the next five, everything else scheduled (tasks, reminders, follow-ups, check-ins, schedules) could only be counted (*5 of 23*), not reached. The heading now has **Show all 23**, which opens the rest in place under a heading per day (*Today*, *Tomorrow*, *Fri 31 Oct*); the heading stays on screen with **Show fewer** while you scroll, and home remembers which you picked.
- **Shell and PR open from the task, not from tabs.** The side panel's *Shell · PR* tabs, and the *Task · Shell · PR* segments on phones, are gone: the task is the page, and Shell and the PR are places it leads to. The task's action row has a **Shell** button (pressed while it shows; click again to put it away), and its PR row opens the PR. The panel's head says what it shows (*Shell*, *PR #412*). On a phone the task page has two rows under its actions that open each one as a page of its own, sliding in with Back to the task (the phone's Back and Esc too): the PR row, with its chevron and *Merge* at the right end, and a **Shell** row that says where it runs and *Running on Studio* while your shell is still going there.
- **More room to read a long task on a narrow screen.** The task's bar (state, branch, actions, PR and Shell rows) used to stay pinned at the top as you scrolled, taking about a third of a phone's screen. It now scrolls away with the task, and once it's gone a single row pins in its place: the state, the actions as icons, the PR as a chip in its colour, and the shell (with a dot while yours is running). Tapping the chip or the shell opens them; scrolling back up brings the full bar back.
- **The phone app's computers screen says which account is which.** Each wisp Cloud account is one card, headed by your picture with the GitHub or Google mark you sign in with, the handle, and that account (`@login` or the email) under it, instead of *wisp Cloud* on every row. Its computers are rows inside the card with *Online* or *Offline* under their names, where an offline one used to have a greyed-out button. The grid now tells devices how an account signs in (`identities` on `/api/me` and the device token); accounts signed in before pick it up on the next refresh.

### Fixed

- **A slow shell start says so instead of looking stuck.** The side panel's shell is your login shell, so it runs your startup files before its first prompt; with nvm or oh-my-zsh that can take several seconds, longer while agents keep the computer busy, and the panel just shimmered. After 4 seconds with nothing on screen it now says *Still starting zsh: it runs your ~/.zshrc first*.
- **A delegate on another machine says where it ran.** Its card in the lead's task could say *This computer* while the delegate actually ran on another machine: for a crew member who lives on another machine, and for delegates in a crew member's team. The card now names that machine, including on delegations made before this fix. A lead with delegates on other machines is also told what goes with the work: wisp sends the folder (commits, uncommitted edits, new files), so the lead doesn't commit or push for it. Git-ignored files and files outside the folder stay behind, so the lead pastes those into the prompt and names files by their path in the folder. The lead's absolute paths in a delegate's prompt are rewritten to paths in the folder, and the delegate is told it runs on another computer.
- **No done check or empty strip on the right of rows on phones.** In the phone app a swipe already marks a task done, so its rows no longer show the round check, and no row keeps a column for it: times and pills reach the edge. (A spell that sets neither swipe to done keeps the check.) In a phone browser, which has no swipe, the column is kept only while some row has the check.
- **Ending the side panel's shell keeps it ended.** *End shell* sometimes brought a new shell straight back (or ended it only after attaching to the old one), depending on which request reached wisp first. It now shows *Shell ended* with *Start again*, and a new shell starts only when you ask or come back to the task.
- **Hovering a task in the sidebar no longer shows its title through the buttons.** *Save for later* and *Mark done* sat over the end of the title, with letters peeking out around them; the title now ends in an ellipsis before them while they show.
- **The side panel's shell no longer opens on a dead screen.** Coming back to a task whose shell had ended (it exited, or wisp dropped it ten minutes later or on a restart) showed the old, often blank screen with a small *Shell ended* bar, so the terminal looked like it never loaded. Opening the panel, switching to the task or to the Shell tab now starts a new shell; a shell that ends while you watch still keeps its last screen and *Start again*.

## [0.47.0] - 2026-10-28

### Added

- **Several wisp Grids, and several accounts on one, in the phone app and on the desktop.** Once you're signed in, *Add an account* stays under your computers on the phone and in Settings → Cloud, for another grid or another account on the same one. Each account shows its handle and grid, with its computers grouped under it. The grid's *Let this device in?* page has *Use another account* when the browser is signed in as someone else, and GitHub now shows its account picker. On the desktop, two accounts on one grid each keep their own computers, and signing one out leaves the other's alone.
- **Swipe a task in the phone app's list to mark it done or archive it.** Swipe right for *Mark done* (*Reopen* on a done task), left for *Archive*. A band under the row names the action and fills (the phone ticks) once letting go will do it; a quick flick does it too. Both come with Undo. A spell can set either direction to *Save for later* or *Delete task*, or turn it off (`card` props `swipeRight` / `swipeLeft`); a swiped delete skips the confirm dialog and is sent only once its Undo toast goes away.
- **Comment on any part of an answer, then follow up from there.** Select words in an agent's answer and a *Comment* bubble comes up; hover a block (a diff, the tests, a callout…) for its comment button; on a picture, video, live preview or app, that button pins a spot (a video also keeps the second it was paused on). Each comment gets a numbered mark on the answer and a row above the reply box: click a row to jump back to its mark and edit it, × to drop it. They go out at the top of your next follow-up, with what you typed (or alone: Enter in an empty box sends them), each one saying which block it's about, the words you picked or where you pinned, and your note, so the agent knows exactly what you meant. *Send now* in a comment sends at once. The sent prompt shows them as numbered cards. On a phone the note opens as a sheet at the bottom. Spells can restyle or hide every piece (`composer.comments`, `answer.comment*`, `turn.comments`).

### Changed

- **On a phone, the prompt box names the model and nothing else.** Where it runs, the mode, Delegate and Goal are icons now (their menus and tooltips still name them), and the harness pill shows the model you picked (*Opus*) instead of only the harness's logo, or the harness's name while its model is the default. When the row doesn't fit, it scrolls sideways with Send pinned at the end.

### Fixed

- **Queueing a follow-up no longer pops a toast or squeezes "1 queued" into the prompt box.** The queued follow-up already shows above the box, so both were noise, and on a phone the count overlapped the Queue switch.
- **In the phone app, tapping your name in the avatar's menu goes to your computers.** It opened your wisp Grid account page in place of wisp, with no way back to it; now it does what the logo does. On a computer it still opens the account page.
- **The phone app notifies you about tasks on a cloud computer.** Opened through wisp Cloud (or your computer added to a grid), the app wasn't kept running once it left the screen, so Android froze it and no notification came when a task finished or asked. It now stays connected in the background as an SSH computer does, with the quiet *Connected to …* notification, until you're back on the computers list.

## [0.46.0] - 2026-10-28

### Added

- **A side panel beside the task: a shell in its worktree, and its PR without the noise.** The panel button in the task's header (or `]`) opens a third column with two tabs. **Shell** is your own shell in the folder the task was built in, on whichever computer ran it: this one, a machine over SSH, or a cloud computer (no terminal app needed there). Each task keeps its shell while you look at others; `⌃\`` goes into it from anywhere and back to the reply box. A line on top says where you are (computer, branch, commits ahead, files changed), and its *⋯* copies the path or branch, or opens the folder in your editor or terminal app. **PR #n** (or `P`) shows the PR in full: one verdict with its next step (*Merge*, *Fix conflicts*), each check with failing ones first and the rest folded, where each reviewer stands, the review threads and comments (bots' and outdated ones folded), and the files. *Ask to fix* on a failing check and *Send to agent* on a comment put it in the reply box, with the end of the failing log, for you to send. On a phone the task page gets *Task · Shell · PR* segments instead, with the key row's new *^C* and the verdict pinned at the bottom. *Open shell* and *Review PR #n* are in the right-click and More menus and ⌘K. A spell can reach it all (`panel.head` slot, `panel.*` parts, `width`, `tab`, `bots`).

### Changed

- **Agents show a new feature live.** When a task builds or changes a web page, the agent now leaves its dev server running and ends with a *preview* of the page that shows the change, so you can click through it in the answer; screenshots for desktop and phone come beside it, or alone when nothing can stay up.

### Fixed

- **A task in a window about 860px wide lists its details one a row again.** There the state ran into the harness and the model's name was cut off at the window's edge.
- **A task on a phone in landscape shows its actions again.** The details column sat pinned to the top of a screen too short for it, so Mark done, Ship it and the rest were below the window. A short pane now gets the bar over the task instead.
- **Back works across its whole button in a narrow Mac app window.** Its left edge sat under the window buttons' hover area, which took the clicks; it now starts clear of them.

## [0.45.0] - 2026-10-28

### Added

- **Report bugs from home, or with `/bug`.** The foot of the New task page has *Hit a bug, or wish wisp did something? Tell us*, which opens *Send feedback*. The dialog now asks what it is: *Idea or thought* (with 👍 / 👎) or *Something broke* (a bug report, which needs a few words). `/bug …` in any prompt box sends a bug report straight away; `/bug` alone opens the dialog on *Something broke*. As before, it goes to the wisp team with your wisp version, OS and, from a task, its harness and model, never your prompts or code. A spell can restyle or hide the home line (`split.feedback`).
- **Delete your wisp Cloud account from the phone app.** The cloud's sheet (*⋯* on it) now has *Delete your account* under *Sign out*. It opens the grid's account page at *Delete your account* in your browser, where you type your handle to confirm.

### Changed

- **trywisp.dev: sign in to wisp Cloud straight from the top of the page.** The hero's *Try it in the cloud* button is now *Continue with GitHub* and *Google*, which start the sign-in at once instead of opening another page first. The headline holds still (*working while you sleep.*), the intro is one sentence, and on a phone the `npx` box, which a phone can't run, makes way for *Send the link to my computer* (the share sheet, or the link copied). The page also records when the night demo is seen and replayed.
- **_Run on_ picks one place, then adds more.** In the prompt box's *Run on* menu, clicking a computer or machine now picks only that one and closes the menu. To run the same task on several places in parallel, use *+ also* on a row (or ⌘/Ctrl-click it). Once several are picked, *× drop* takes one out. *also* replaces *only*, so picking one place is the default and running on several is the extra step. The check is round while one place is picked and square once there are several.

### Fixed

- **Tidier spacing, borders and dividers.** A question or approval has one amber frame instead of two nested ones; the line under each reply is solid (dashed now only means empty or add); the details column's facts and actions share one icon column; home's Needs you and Tips line up with the prompt box's edges; no stray rules in the prompt box's tool row; on a narrow window a reply's tools sit right under its files, and the task bar sits 12px under its header. Overlap notices and the setup tip use the same lines and corners as their neighbours, and run rows lost their coloured side stripe.
- **A taken-over terminal stays with its task.** After *Take over* in wisp, opening another task, home or a crew member put the terminal away, and coming back to the task showed only its transcript (the terminal ran on behind *In terminal*). Now coming back to the task shows the terminal over it again, also after a reload, until it ends. *Back* on the terminal still puts it away while you read the task.
- **Crew members work as delegates of a task on another machine.** A lead running on a machine (wisp Cloud, an SSH machine) couldn't reach a crew member in its *Delegate* team, because the crew lives on the wisp you work from and the machine looked for the member in its own, empty, crew. Now the wisp you work from starts the member's job (on the member's own machine if it has one), and the lead gets its report.
- **Icons and text line up.** In the task list, the section chevrons and *New task*'s + now sit on the same column as the task dots, and *New task* starts where the titles do. A row's *Save for later* and *Mark done* buttons sit on the title's line. Home's *Needs you* and *Tips* headings line up with the dots under them, and the tips' text starts where the tasks' titles do. The checkpoint chip under a prompt has a smaller icon and its hash sits on the date's line, and *Recipes* sits on its hint's line.
- **Renaming a task keeps you on it.** Pressing Enter to save a new name closed the task, and Escape to cancel it went back to the list; both now just end the rename.
- **Ship it comes back for changes made after a task's PR merged.** Since agents attach the PRs they open (`wisp_pr`), a task's own PR merged on GitHub (by the agent, a spell or by hand) hid Ship it for good, so follow-up changes in the same task could only be shipped by asking the agent. It now shows again for uncommitted changes or commits made after the merged head, and opens a new PR for them. The squash-merged commit itself, and a branch GitHub deleted on merge, don't count as something to ship.
- **Phone app: signing in to wisp Cloud survives a grid restart.** If the grid answered with an error page while the app waited for you to tap *Allow* (it restarts during an update), the app stopped asking and kept spinning. It now asks again until the grid is back, and stops asking once you close the sign-in sheet.
- **Phone app: Connect then Disconnect at once no longer closes the app.** Disconnecting before Android had shown the *Connected to …* notification made Android 12 and later stop the app.
- **Phone app: tapping a task's notification opens that task, even after the screen was off.** If the app had been closed or was reconnecting, the tap left you on the computers list. It now keeps the task and opens it once wisp's page has loaded.
- **Phone app: one damaged SSH key no longer hides all of them.** The app lists the keys it can read and says which one it couldn't, instead of showing none.
- **Signing in to wisp Cloud from the phone app is one tap.** *Sign in to wisp Cloud* under your computers now offers *Continue with GitHub* and *Continue with Google*, like the first screen, instead of asking for a grid address; another grid is still behind *Use your own wisp Grid*.
- **The phone app's computers list fits a 360px phone.** The *Cloud*, *Your computer* and *Over SSH* pills stay whole and the address after them ends in `…`, the name gets more room, and buttons are 48px tall, Android's own size. A sheet keeps its title and × at the top while the keyboard is up, and every fold opens with the same ›. On the grid's device pages, *Allow*, *Don't allow*, *Continue* and *Back to the wisp app* are 48px on a phone, and so is *Sign out* at the top of the account page.
- **The Crew section's *Add someone to your crew* lines up with the tasks above it.** Its + sits where a task's dot does and its text starts where task titles do.
- **Menus stay on screen on phones, keyboard or not.** With the keyboard up, the harness and model picker, *Run on* and the mode menu opened off the top of the screen. Tapping their buttons now puts the keyboard away first, and every menu and popover keeps inside what's visible: above or below its button, whichever has room, scrolling inside when it's taller. It moves again when the keyboard goes up or down. Picking from one no longer brings the keyboard back up on a touch screen.
- **Long computer names no longer push the prompt box's buttons away.** The place button ends the name in `…` (96px in the phone app, 160px elsewhere), so the mode, harness and the rest of the bar keep their room. *Run on* and a follow-up's place menu end long names in `…` and stay inside the window, *only* shows on every row on touch screens, and the avatar menu gives the computer's name the row's width. A machine that isn't connected gets its own line under the project chips, so *in* no longer sits alone on a line above them.
- **The Recipes row lines up.** Its *Recipes* label sat above the recipe chips on phones, where the chips are taller; label and chips now share one centre line at every size.
- **A scheduled follow-up near the limit waits even right after a run.** When it came due while the last run's CLI was still closing, *Ask near the limit* let it through; now it's held like any other.
- **Phone polish.** On a narrow prompt box *Other folder…* is its icon, so the project chips keep one line. The New task button hides under dialogs, where it covered their last line. A tap no longer leaves a grey box on the result's buttons or on a Settings row.

## [0.44.2] - 2026-10-28

### Fixed

- **One message no longer turns into endless tasks between two computers on a grid.** Two computers on the same wisp Grid each list the other under Machines, so each relayed the other's tasks back to it, one `@machine` longer on every lap (`x@b@a@b…`). A single message filled both task lists until wisp ran out of memory. A wisp now shows only a machine's own tasks and never passes a machine's events on to another wisp; one updated computer is enough to stop the loop. `GET /api/sessions` takes `here=1` (this wisp's own sessions only).

## [0.44.1] - 2026-10-28

### Fixed

- **opencode 2 runs.** opencode 2.x (Homebrew's build, GitHub's latest release) refused every task with *invalid --log-level value ERROR*, or *You must provide a message* on early 2.0 builds. wisp now reads `opencode --version` and gives 2.x the flags it takes: a lowercase `--log-level`, and the thinking level on the model (`provider/model#high`) instead of `--variant`. opencode 1.x (npm, the install script) is unchanged.

## [0.44.0] - 2026-10-28

### Added

- **On a cloud computer, previews show the cloud computer's page.** A *preview* block for `localhost:5173` used to load `localhost` on your own device: a blank frame, or whatever you were running yourself. In a wisp Grid box it now loads that port in the box, at `p5173--<handle>.<domain>`. It signs you in on its own, sends its own cookies, follows redirects and keeps websockets working, so a dev server's live reload still runs. The address bar still says `localhost:5173`. Grid operators: on by default for the container driver, `hostd.previews` turns it on or off. Every new port is a new name for Caddy's on-demand TLS.
- **Previews of pages on your own computer, from your phone or any browser.** A computer added to the grid can show its own local pages too. Turn on **Settings → Machines → Show local pages in previews** (off by default). Opened through the grid, a *preview* of `localhost:5173` on that computer then loads from `p5173--<name>--<handle>.<domain>`. It comes through the computer's tunnel, websockets included, and only you, signed in, can open it. On the computer itself it still loads `localhost` directly. Switch it off and the grid gets a refusal at once. New `previews` on `POST /api/computer`; `/api/config` has `preview`.
- **Add a computer to wisp Cloud from a terminal.** The account page's *Add a computer* now offers two ways: **In a terminal**, one copyable command (`npx wisp-agents computer on`, with `--grid` filled in on your own grid) that signs in and adds the computer, and **With the app**, the Machines steps as before.
- **Agents can attach GitHub PRs to their task, as many as it needs.** A new `wisp_pr` tool takes a PR's URL (or its number in the task's repo). The PR shows on the task exactly like one opened with Ship it: the pill on the card, the PR row with its state, checks and reviews, kept current, and **Merge**, which now merges every open PR on the task. A PR on the task's own branch (the agent ran `gh pr create` itself) becomes the one Ship it pushes to. `remove` detaches one; `wisp_session` lists them.
- **Auto shows its pick before you send, and starts from what you pick yourself.** With Auto selected, the launcher shows what will run (*Opus · Medium*) as you type. Click it for why (*your usual for UI tasks (212 of yours)*, *87% expected from public benchmarks*), what else Auto weighed, and a model it suggests. Pick one there and the task runs on it, and Auto learns from the change. What the preview shows is what runs. Every task you launch on a harness of your choosing now counts as a vote per kind of task, and your existing tasks count from the first start. Replayed over 327 hand-picked launches, Auto picks the same harness, model and level 94% of the time, up from 1%. A model that looks better than your habit is suggested, not run behind your back. Settings → Auto adds *Learn from your picks* and **Never** / **Prefer** per model, also per kind of task. `node scripts/auto-replay.mjs` replays your own history. New `POST /api/router/preview`; launches take `autoPin` and `autoOverride`; `PATCH /api/router` takes `habits`, `blocked` and `prefer`.
- **Auto's starting numbers come from public benchmarks.** These replace the 121-run pilot that rated a free model 10 out of 10. `scripts/priors-sources.json` lists every score with its source and date: Anthropic's effort sweep, the Opus 5.5 system card, SWE-bench Verified, Terminal-Bench 2.0 and 2.1, SWE-bench Pro. `node scripts/priors.mjs` places each model and level against Claude Code's default and rebuilds `server/router-priors.json`. Thinking levels and model strength now come from the data, not one table for every model or the model's name. GPT-5.6 Sol and Terra count as frontier models, Gemini 3.8 Flash as everyday, Sonnet runs at high under Balanced, and x-high has to earn its 2.5× cost.

- **A new first screen in the phone app, with GitHub and Google right there.** It opens on a glimpse of agents at work (one working, one asking you something, one shipped), *Your agents, in your pocket*, and **Continue with GitHub** / **Continue with Google**. One tap goes to that sign-in in the browser and then straight to Allow for this phone; there's no grid address to type and no grid page in between. Another grid is behind *Use your own wisp Grid*; SSH stays folded at the bottom.
- **In the phone app, the wisp logo goes to your computers.** Tapping it in the top bar opens the computers list, like *Computers* in the avatar menu; the list no longer shows a Back button with the computer's name (the phone's Back still returns). Browsers and the desktop app are unchanged: the logo still goes home.
- **Tips on the phone app's computers screen.** Under your computers, a first-time user now sees what to do next and what the phone can do. Cards on top go once they're done: *Step in to start* opens the first computer, *Hear when an agent needs you* asks Android for notifications, and *Bring your own computer* says how to add one to the grid and opens the guide. Under them, three lines on what's possible (Cloud vs your computer, tasks keep running with the phone locked, Needs you, Auto, Goal, Ship it). Each line opens its guide on trywisp.dev; a new set shows each start and on **More tips**. A computer's row no longer ends in a cut-off *online*: the dot and its button already say so.
- **git and GitHub, pinned in Tips until they're set up.** Ship it needs git, the GitHub CLI and a GitHub sign-in. Until all three are there, on this computer or a cloud computer, a card stays on top of the tips on the home page (phones too) asking for the next one: *Install git*, *Install the GitHub CLI*, then *Sign in to GitHub*. It opens Settings → Agents at that row, which now shows git and gh when they're missing with this OS's install command to copy (`xcode-select --install`, `brew install gh`, `winget …`, `apt …`) and a Docs link. The card goes away once you've signed in, and checks again when you come back to the window after installing one in a terminal. New `GET /api/tools`. Spells: the `tips.tools` part.
- **Reach this wisp from anywhere, pinned in Tips.** A wisp on your own computer that isn't on a wisp Grid yet keeps a *Reach this wisp from anywhere* card on top of the tips: once it's on a grid you open it at your grid address from your phone or any browser, with no SSH and no open ports. It opens Machines at the grid sign-in (or *Add to grid* if you're signed in) and goes away once it's on. On a cloud computer the card explains its address instead, and opens Settings → Connect; it goes once you've opened it. Not in the phone app. Spells: the `tips.grid` part.

### Changed

- **A crew member's task shows their face, not the model's logo.** The task's details and each request in it show the member's avatar; hover it (or read the details column) for who ran it and on what: *Sana · Claude Code · Opus*.
- **A failed Auto run tries harder before it hands over.** A run that thought at low or medium and still fails its tests goes again at high on the same model (one at high, at x-high), before Auto moves the task to a stronger model. Anthropic measured this on Opus 5.5: 97% solved at $0.17 a task, against 95% at $0.29 with everything at high.

- **The setup card names no single agent.** A signed-out harness no longer reads *Sign in to Claude Code*: the card says *Sign in to a coding agent* (Claude Code, Codex or another) and offers opencode’s free models, which need no account, as a way to start.

### Fixed

- **A cloud computer that runs out of memory says so.** When a box goes over its memory (1.5 GB on wisp Cloud), the kernel kills it and the grid starts it again. The task that was running used to end with *Stopped: wisp was restarted while this was running*, which read as if you had stopped it. It now says *Out of memory*, with the limit and a tip to run fewer things at once, and links to the waitlist for bigger cloud computers.
