The link opens the account page's dialog with *A bigger machine* ticked (`#machines/bigger`). The grid logs these stops as `out of memory` and counts them apart from crashes.
- **The phone app says a task went.** Sending from New task now shows *Sent to Claude* (or the crew member) with **Open**, and the list scrolls the new row to the middle, clear of the New task button; before, the prompt page closed onto the list with nothing to show it worked.
- **Mark done in the phone app goes back to the list**, not to the New task page. Browsers and the desktop app still open New task.
- **Send anyway works for replies from an answer's buttons.** Near the usage limit, a follow-up sent with an answer's button (*Commit this and open a PR*) or by a spell showed the hold row, but **Send anyway** and **Wait until …** did nothing: they sent what was in the reply box, which was empty. They now send that follow-up.

## [0.43.0] - 2026-10-27

### Added

- **Automations stop at: keep part of your plan for yourself.** Settings → Usage → Plan budget has a new row, **Automations stop at** (Off, 50%, 75% or 90%). Once an account's 5-hour or weekly window passes that mark, work nobody started by hand waits for the window to reset: crew check-ins on their schedule, triggers (schedules, GitHub watchers, task watchers), a goal's next round, and follow-ups an agent or wisp scheduled. Each one says so where it lives. A goal round and an agent's follow-up move to *Scheduled* at the reset (*Goal round 4 waits for your plan*) and go on by themselves. A crew member's page says *Waits for your plan · Thu 21:00* and its next check-in comes after the reset. A trigger's row says *Skipped 4h ago for your plan*, and a GitHub item it skipped is picked up on a later poll. Anything you start or schedule yourself always runs, and so do *Check in now*, *Run now*, *Resume goal* and *Send now*. Each bar in Plan budget has a mark at the line, and a window past it reads *Automations wait · resets Thu 21:00*. wisp only holds its own work back: it never goes past a provider's limit or switches accounts. Off by default. Spells: the `autoStop` setting, and the `settings.autoStop` part.
- **Where your plan went.** Each time Claude Code or Codex reports its usage windows, wisp shares the change out among the tasks that ran on that account since the last report, by how long each one ran. A task's details show its share (*22% of weekly · 31% of 5-hour*). Under each account in Settings → Usage → Plan budget, *Where the weekly window went* lists the three tasks that used the most this period (click one to open it), the rest in one row, and *Outside wisp or earlier* for what wisp didn't run or didn't see. Shares are approximate: Claude reports once per run, so a turn's share arrives with the next report. Spells: `details.plan` and `budget.where`.
- **Ask near the limit.** Before a task or a follow-up would take a Claude Code or Codex plan's 5-hour or weekly window past a line you set (Settings → Usage, *Ask near the limit*: Off, 80%, 90% or 95%; 90% by default), wisp asks first. A row over the box says where the window is and where this would likely take it (*Claude Code’s 5-hour window is at 84%. A follow-up like this usually takes it to 88–96%.*) with **Send anyway**, **Wait until Thu 21:00** and × *Keep editing*; your draft stays put. After a yes, that window asks again only at 95%, then 99%, until it resets. Schedules, triggers, recipes, crew jobs and scheduled follow-ups can't ask, so they wait as a *Held* run in Needs you (**Start now**, **Wait until …**, **Skip this run**) and go by themselves at the reset. The estimate comes from your own runs: what similar ones cost, and how far the window moved per unit of work while wisp ran them. The harness picker shows a window past your line. wisp never switches accounts because of it. The API answers 409 with `hold` (send `confirmLimit: true` once the user agreed; `wisp launch --yes`), `POST /api/sessions/{id}/held` starts, waits or skips a held run, and spells reach the setting as `limitHold` and the picker's note as the `harness.usage` part.
- **See what your crew used, and what ate your limits.** Right-click a crew member (or its ⋯ menu, or ⌘K) → **Track record…**: how much of your 5-hour and weekly limits it used since they reset (*Since Fri 14:52, Scout used 14% of the tokens, the crew 36%*), its tokens and cost over 7, 30 or 90 days and per day, where they went by kind of job (its check-ins and triggers called out: *4 runs a day × 35k = 148k a day · 86% found nothing*), its biggest jobs (a click opens one), and how its jobs went in one line. One suggestion when there's enough to go on (*Checking in a quarter as often would use about 37k. Change schedule…*). Pick **Whole crew** in the title (or ⌘K / Settings → Usage → *Crew track record*) to rank members by usage next to your own tasks. Spells reach it as the `crew.record` part (its `tips` prop turns the suggestion off), `crew.record.subject` and `crew.compare`; the API as `GET /api/crew/stats`.
- **Search by topic.** Agents tag their task with a few topic words once they know what it's about (a new `wisp_tag` tool every harness gets), and ⌘K finds tasks by them on top of its fuzzy search: a plain word that names a tag ranks the task higher, `#auth` keeps only tasks tagged *auth* (`#au` is enough), and typing a word shows the matching topics with their task count; pick one to narrow to it. Rows show the tags they matched. `wisp_sessions` searches tags too. Spells reach the row's tags as the `palette.tags` part.
- **A setup card on top of Tips until an agent can run.** With no harness found (or only signed-out ones), home pins *Connect a coding agent* (or *Sign in to Claude Code*) above the tips, on phones and stacked windows too, and it opens Settings → Agents. It goes away by itself once one is ready. Spells reach it as the `tips.setup` part. opencode on its free models alone (no provider signed in, no API key) doesn't count as ready: the card asks for an account or a key, and Settings → Agents says *free models only*.

### Changed

- **The crew member you need comes first.** The Crew section no longer lists members in the order they joined: whoever is asking you or working for you is on top, then unread reports, then the members you send the most work (last 30 days), paused ones last. Who fills the rows before the rest fold into faces follows the same order, and so does the `@` picker. The order holds while your pointer is in the section, so nothing moves under the cursor.
- **The guides on trywisp.dev read like articles.** One 680px column of text, a byline with the reading time, the contents in the margin on wide screens, code with a Copy button, questions that open in place, and *Keep reading* cards. Seventeen guides gained a step-by-step animated figure that shows how the thing works (worktrees per agent, a goal's rounds, a task moving machines, the grid tunnel…), with a sentence per step, pause and step dots, and their last frame for people who prefer reduced motion. The guides list is grouped by topic.

- **Pick a time first, then type.** The clock beside Send (Start later, Send later) works on an empty box: a time you pick shows under the box (*Starts Tue 10:00*, × clears it) and Enter schedules the task once you've typed it, whatever times the text mentions. *Pick a time…* is now a field in its own row of the menu, with the time it reads where the others show theirs, Enter to take it and Escape to step back. Scheduled tasks and follow-ups now take their attached files along instead of refusing them.
- **Thinking is a slider in the harness picker.** Drag (or arrow) from Default up to the harness's top level: the track fills with a quiet accent tint, the knob's orb grows, small wisps brighten and drift faster the harder it thinks, and a hint says what the level means (*quick and light*, *digs deep*). Spells reach it as the `picker.thinking` part.
- **Phone polish for a task's page.** *Claude Code is working*, its time and its action count stay on one line (the count, then the time, give way on a narrow phone or with large text); the task's icon buttons (Transcript, Rerun, Take over, More) sit evenly with their icons centred and a pressed Transcript no longer looks off to one side; a tapped button no longer keeps a grey box; a finished turn's files and tools always start their own line; a long reply-box hint fades at the edge instead of making the empty box two lines tall; and a question being asked isn't repeated as *Asked you: …* under its own card.
- **Schedules in plain words.** A crew member's *Custom…* check-in and a scheduled trigger's *When* now take what you'd say (*every monday*, *8am every weekday*, *every 15 min between 9 and 6*) instead of a cron line, and read it back as you type (*= Weekdays at 8:00*). Lists show schedules in words too. Cron still works for those who know it, and agents, spells and the API can send words as well.
- **Small polish in the task list, the prompt box and a finished task.** Titles in the sidebar run to the time instead of stopping halfway beside the state and PR pills. The prompt box folds the place to its icon a little sooner, so the harness keeps its name (no more *Au…*). A finished task's result no longer repeats the time, cost and file count shown right under it. On a phone, a task's branch gets its own line instead of shrinking to *wi…*. A result whose heading is the task's title doesn't repeat it under the head, and in split view the reply box no longer repeats *Ship it* and *Mark done*, which the task's bar keeps in reach.
- **Calmer, more readable page.** One type scale (at most five sizes on a screen, down from ten), hints and times at WCAG AA contrast in light and dark, and colour only where it means something (Plan, Full auto and Worktree tint their icon, not their words). A task says each thing once: the result drops its own *Finished* pill, the bar drops the overlap count while the overlap notices show, and with the list beside the task the details drop the state and clock time the row and the prompt already show. On phones the task's bar fits in two rows, with the branch cut in the middle (*wisp/add-a-rat…-9c5e26*) or on its own line on a touch screen, and overlap reasons wrap instead of ending in *Also …*. In the app every control is at least 44px, and every Tab stop shows a focus ring.

### Fixed

- **A paused goal keeps its reason when wisp quits.** Quitting wisp, or pressing Stop on a task that wasn't running, changed a paused goal's reason to *You stopped it*. It now keeps the reason it was paused for.
- **A crew member's own jobs are in the task list.** Check-ins (scheduled or asked for), jobs you send a member and trigger jobs show in *Working*, *Needs you* and *Done* like any task, with the member's face; a follow-up on one moves it back to *Working*. They used to show only under the member, so a finished check-in or a followed-up job could vanish from the sidebar. Jobs run for another task (delegated, an `@` answer, a check on a task) stay under the member.
- **The harness keeps its name in the New task box side by side.** At about 1000px wide with a project picked, the harness pill shrank to `A…` while *Worktree* kept its label; Worktree now folds to its branch icon first (its tooltip still names it).
- **The / menu in the prompt box.** Typing `/` on New task now lists `/goal`, `/spell` and `/feedback` (and spells' own commands), as the reply box does; it used to show nothing, so `/goal all tests pass` worked only if you knew it. A bare `/goal` opens *Done when*.

- **A goal set right after a task lands gets checked.** */goal …* sent in the second or two while the agent's CLI was still exiting after its result left the goal at *working* forever: nothing checked it or sent the task back. Now it's checked at once, as on any idle task.
- **Crew → Show fewer folds the list again.** On a phone the tap left the section hovered and focused, which kept every row shown; now it folds back to the compact list at once.
- **Phone polish.** Menus (More, the project list) and search's rows are 44px tall on any touch screen, and search drops from the top as a full-width sheet instead of a card cut off at the bottom. A question in a working task gets the panel's whole width on a phone, so its answer box and the status line no longer squeeze into a column. In the app, the empty band above *Needs you* is gone, nothing peeks out from behind Send in the prompt box, and a crew member's *About* and Delegate list name the computer (*Studio*) instead of *This computer*. Said once: the reply box leaves */btw* out while the working panel's tip shows it, a crew member's *About* row drops the status the head already shows, and the project search reads *Search projects* with one project instead of *Search 1 projects*.
- **The phone app's top bar lines up again.** Search, your avatar and the project filter are one row of matching 32px controls: search gets the same round outline as the avatar, and the filter pill is no longer a 44px lozenge sitting lower than the rest (each keeps its 44px touch area).

### Added

- **For later: park a finished task to look at again.** **Save for later** (`L`, right-click, More, ⌘K, or the bookmark beside *Mark done* when you hover a row in *Needs you*) moves a task out of *Needs you* into a new *For later* section in the task list, newest saved first, with *Undo*. Nothing there runs or pings you, and it isn't counted as needing you. Anything new takes it back out: a follow-up, a reminder coming due, news on its PR; so do *Mark done*, *Reopen* and *Archive*. **Unsave** puts it back where it was. Crew jobs can be saved too (from their card or with **Save for later** in the member's thread); they move out from under the member into For later with its face. A crew member can also file a report there itself when it's worth reading but not urgent: the heading says *1 new* and nobody is notified. API: `forLater` on `PATCH /sessions/:id` and on a task. Spells: the `forLater` action (buttons and hooks), and the parts `section.forLater` and `card.quickLater`.
- **Agents can update your crew's notebooks and skills.** Ask any task to *give @gucci a skill for UI reviews* or *remember in Sana's notebook that…*: its agent reads the member in full (`wisp_crew` with `member`: brief, notebook and kit), adds to or rewrites their notebook (`wisp_crew_edit` `notebook` / `remember`), and proposes skills and instructions files with the new `wisp_crew_kit`. Proposed files never join the kit by themselves: they show under the answer in the same card as the crew page's (*Proposed for Gucci’s kit*: tick, read and edit each, then *Add to their kit* or *Dismiss*), and settle in the member's page too. A crew member's own job can propose files the same way; a subagent can't. Spells: `turn.kitOffer`.
- **Open a crew member's job from the task that delegated it.** While the lead waits, a crew member's row in its team panel (*@gucci · This computer · Polish review…*) now opens that member's job, and its report card has *Open @gucci’s job*. The job itself starts with where it came from, *Delegated from “…”* (or *Tagged in*, *Checking on*), a link back to that task. Spells: `delegate.live`, `delegate.open`, `task.from`.
- **Fork a task.** **Fork…** starts a new task that carries on from where a task got to, to try another way while the original is kept as it is. It's in the right-click menu, More, ⌘K and ⇧F, and **Fork from this turn** on each turn forks from an earlier point. The fork gets the conversation up to that turn and a copy of the files as they were then, uncommitted changes and new files included, in a worktree on a branch of its own, so it ships on its own. It can run on another harness, model or mode; whichever agent takes it first reads what happened before the fork. The fork links back with *Forked from “…”*, a line shows where its own turns start, and the original counts its forks (*2 forks*, which lists them). Works for tasks in a git repo and for tasks without a folder. API: `POST /sessions/:id/fork`, `forkedFrom` and `forks` on a task. Spells: the `fork` action, and the parts `card.forkedFrom`, `card.forks`, `turn.fork` and `turn.forkStart`.

### Changed

- **A crew member you `@`-mention stays in the sidebar.** The job you started with `@Ada …` in a follow-up now waits in *Needs you* until you mark it done, instead of filing itself away once it answers. Jobs that another task's agent delegates to a crew member still file themselves under *Done*.

### Fixed

- **Mark done and Reopen follow the task at once.** Reopening a done task from the list (right-click, ⌘K or E) left its bar and reply box on *Reopen* and *Follow up to reopen…* until the page reloaded; they now switch to *Mark done* straight away, and the button's tooltip names what it does. The More menu's *Archive* / *Unarchive* no longer offers the old choice after you archive or unarchive from the list, and ⌘K says *Reopen* like every other menu instead of *Move back to review*.
- **Marking a task done takes you to New task.** Mark done, Archive or Delete on the task you have open now goes to the prompt box instead of opening whichever task sat next in the list. From the list (a row's ✓, or E on a focused row), focus still moves down so triage carries on.
- **Answering from the task list in the phone app.** A question's answer buttons sit on their own line under it again, instead of a narrow column beside the row that pushed the list sideways and squeezed every label onto several lines.

## [0.42.0] - 2026-10-26

### Added

- **Ask an agent to add a connector.** Ask a task to *add PostHog* or *connect Linear* and the agent adds it to Settings → Connectors itself. Three new tools make this possible: `wisp_connectors` lists your connectors, `wisp_connector_add` adds one, and `wisp_connector_signin` asks you to finish. If the connector needs an account, a **Sign in** card appears in the task; if it needs a key, the card has a box to type it, and the value goes straight to wisp, never through the agent. The card closes by itself once the connector works, and the agent carries on. A connector that runs a command on your computer is added only after you say yes. Subagents and crew members' jobs can't add connectors.
- **Sign in to a connector instead of pasting a token.** A remote connector that needs an account, like PostHog or Linear, now shows **Sign in** on its row (also *Add and sign in* when you add one, and *Sign in with the browser* in its form). You approve wisp in the browser, and the tokens stay on this computer. wisp refreshes them when they run out, so tasks keep their tools without asking you again. PostHog is one of the examples in an empty list. This uses MCP's own sign-in (OAuth with dynamic client registration and PKCE), so it works with any server that supports it. An API key in a header still works for servers that don't. API: `POST/DELETE /connectors/:id/signin`.
- **Connectors: your MCP servers, for every harness.** Add an MCP server once in Settings → Connectors and every task's agent gets it, whatever the harness: Claude Code, Codex, Copilot, OpenCode and Grok. A server can be local (a command such as `npx -y @modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem ~/notes`) or remote (an `https://` URL, with headers). Env vars and headers are kept as secrets on this computer. They never go into config.json or an agent's command line: each harness starts a small wisp proxy that reads them. When you save, wisp starts the server once to check it, and the row shows its tool count or why it failed. Pasting a config's `mcpServers` JSON fills in the form. Servers already in Claude Code, Codex, Copilot, OpenCode or Cursor settings are offered for import, and a harness keeps using its own copy of a server with the same name. Plan mode runs without connectors. Cursor and Antigravity only read their own MCP settings, so they don't get them. API: `GET/POST /connectors`, `PATCH/DELETE /connectors/:id`, `POST /connectors/:id/test`, `POST /connectors/import`. Spells can restyle or hide the section as `settings.connectors`, `connectors.row` and `connectors.import`, but can't add connectors, because a connector runs a command.
- **Goals: a task that keeps going until it's done.** Turn on **Goal** in the prompt box and say what has to be true (*Done when: all tests pass and the page works on a phone*), or type `/goal …` in a task's reply box. After every round wisp checks the work itself, running the project's tests and having a model read the changes (not the agent's word for it), and while the goal isn't met it sends the task back with what's missing and what to do next. The task stays in Working and pings you once: when the goal is met, can't be met (with why), runs out of rounds (3, 5, 10 or 20), or goes in circles. A band on top of the task shows the goal, a progress track with how far the last round moved it, the round, and the last check in the checker's words; the row shows a ring and *4/10*. **Edit goal…**, **Pause goal**, **Resume goal** (with a fresh set of rounds) and **Drop goal** are in the band, More, right-click and ⌘K. Works on every harness, and a task Auto hands to a stronger pick takes its goal along. API: `goal` on `POST /sessions`, `PUT/PATCH/DELETE /sessions/:id/goal`. Spells reach it as `goal.band` (a slot too), `goal.text`, `goal.track`, `goal.check`, `card.goal`, `launcher.goal`, `launcher.goalLine` and the `goalRounds` setting.
- **Plan budget, and wait for the reset.** Settings → Usage shows how much of each account's 5-hour and weekly windows is gone, as Claude Code and Codex report it on every run, when each resets, and when you'd run out at this pace (*Resets Thu 21:00 · at this pace, runs out Wed 16:10*). A usage limit now asks what next on every harness, not only with several accounts, and *Wait until* uses the reset time the CLI reported. Turn on **Wait for the reset** there and a task, crew job or schedule that hits a limit waits under *Scheduled* and carries on in the same conversation when the window resets, instead of stopping. wisp never switches accounts for you. API: `budget` on `GET /agents`, the `limitWait` default. Spells reach it as `settings.budget` and the `limitWait` setting.
- **A compact Crew section.** Busy crew members (asking you, on a job, an unread report) always keep their row; when nobody is, two stay and the rest fold into one row of faces, *4 on call · 1 paused*. A face opens that member, the row shows everyone. Check-ins only animate the face, so scheduled members no longer pop in and out. Spells: `crew.strip`, and `rows` on `section.crew`.
- **More than one account per harness, for Claude Code and Codex.** If you have more than one plan, say your own and your employer's, add the second in Settings → Agents (**Add another Claude account**), as a personal or a work account. It signs in with the CLI's own sign-in and keeps its login in a folder of its own. Settings, skills, MCP servers and transcripts are shared with your first account. Pick the account for a task in the runner picker (an *Account* row under Thinking); wisp remembers your pick per harness. When an account reaches its usage limit, the task asks what to do: wait for the reset, continue on another of your accounts, hand the task to another agent, or get extra usage from the provider. wisp never switches accounts on its own. The task's details say which account ran it. API: `GET/POST /agents/:id/accounts`, `PATCH/DELETE /agents/:id/accounts/:acc`, `?account=` on the sign-in routes, `account` on launches and follow-ups, `turn.limit` on a turn that hit one. Spells reach it as `picker.accounts`, `runner.account`, `details.account` and `settings.accounts`.
- **Schedule work for later, in the same task.** Type *in 10 min check again* in a task's reply box (or use the clock button beside Send) and wisp sends that follow-up into the same conversation at that time, so the agent carries on with everything it knew. Agents can do it themselves with the new `resume_later` tool ("the deploy takes 20 minutes, I'll look again then") instead of sleeping; crew members' jobs too (their `wakeIn` still starts a fresh check-in). Follow-ups show above the reply box (*At 14:45 · set by the agent*), with **Send now**, **Change time…** and **Cancel**, also in the right-click menu, More and ⌘K. An agent can chain 10 at most before it has to report back to you.
- **"In 5 minutes do X" and "remind me … in 1 hour" in the prompt box.** A prompt that starts with a time is held and started then (a scheduled task); *remind me …* sets a reminder that needs no agent and lands in *Needs you* with a bell when due, with **Snooze…**. A chip under the box shows the time wisp read before you send; × or Alt+Enter runs it now, and text that only mentions a time is never delayed. Settings → *Read times in prompts* turns it off. Also **Start later** beside Launch.
- **See what's coming.** A *Scheduled* section in the list (split view and the feed) holds tasks and reminders that haven't started and finished tasks with follow-ups to come, soonest first; split view's home lists *Coming up*: those, crew check-ins and schedule triggers, with their times. A task with a follow-up shows a clock pill with its time. wisp started late (or Grid woke it) catches up: follow-ups a few minutes late tell the agent so, a day late they wait for you in *Needs you*. API: `POST/PATCH/DELETE /sessions/:id/later`, `GET/POST /later`, `PATCH/DELETE /later/:id`. Spells reach it as `section.scheduled`, `split.upcoming` (a slot too, with `count`), `card.later`, `composer.later`, `composer.sendLater`, `launcher.later` and the `readTimes` setting.

### Changed

- **CI failures follow up on the task already on them.** When a CI-failed trigger fires for a branch that already has a task (the one an earlier failure there started, or your task working in that branch's worktree), the failure is queued on it as a follow-up instead of starting another task. Archive the task or mark it done and the next failure starts a fresh one. Crew members' CI watchers do the same with their jobs. *Check now* says how many it started and how many it sent as follow-ups.
- **Toasts look new, and stack.** A light card instead of a dark (or solid red) pill: a glyph says what kind it is (a warning for errors, the message's own icon, a crew member's face), the action is a soft button, and a ring round × drains while it shows. Only the newest is in front; older ones tuck in behind it with their edges showing. Point at the stack (tap it on a phone) and it fans out, every clock stops, and **Clear all** closes the lot; × closes one without running its action, and on a phone you flick it away. The same message twice shows once, three at most. Spells reach them as `toasts`, `toast`, `toast.glyph`, `toast.action`, `toast.timer` and `toast.clear`.
- **Crew members you delegate to show their face.** The Delegate button, its presets, the live lines and the delegated reports show a crew member's avatar and role instead of the harness it runs on; other delegates keep their harness logo.
- **More room for your tasks: your account, Machines, Automations and Settings are behind one round avatar** in the corner of the task list's head (your wisp Grid account, its ring lit while connected). The sidebar's foot and the Machines and Automations tiles are gone. Spells reach it as `topbar.account` and `account.menu`; `sidebar.foot`, `sidebar.nav` and `sidebar.account` still work.
- **On a phone, a task opens as a page of its own.** In the Android app and in browser windows under 900px wide, the task list fills the screen; tapping a task opens it full screen with **Back** (the button, Esc, or the phone's Back) to the list, right where you left it. A crew member's thread and **New task** (the prompt box, with Needs you under it) open the same way; sending a task goes back to the list. The app starts on the list. Spells can restyle Back as `split.back`.
- **Split view is the only layout: the one-column feed is gone.** Settings → General → *Layout*, the `\` key and the palette's *Feed view* are removed. A spell that sets `settings.view` still loads, with a warning that it's ignored.
- **A crew member's notebook and brief lose their Show all.** The preview stays short; **Edit** opens the whole thing. A brief a spell sets has no Edit, so it keeps Show all.
- **A crew member's finished jobs stay under them in the sidebar.** Reading a report no longer makes the job vanish: under each member are their three most recent jobs, live ones first (asking you, working, an unread report), then what they finished, until you mark it done or archive it. *N more · open …* leads to the rest in their thread. Quiet check-ins still only show in the thread.

### Fixed

- **One click when you leave the follow-up box.** With the reply box open, clicking a follow-up suggestion, a pull request clash or any other button above it needed two clicks: the box folded on the press and moved the button away before the click landed. It now folds once the click has gone through.

## [0.41.0] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **Agents can point at another wisp task, and you open it with a click.** When an agent finds or names one of your tasks (*find the session where…*), its reply shows a card with the task's title, project, harness, state and when it last moved; click it to open the task. A task that's been deleted keeps its title and says so. Agents are told to use it whenever they name a task, and spells can restyle it (`task`, `task.open`).
- **Phones opening wisp from X, Instagram or Facebook get a way out:** those apps open links in their own browser, where Google won't sign anyone in and nobody is signed in to GitHub. wisp Grid's sign-in page and trywisp.dev now spot it and offer **Open in Safari** (or Chrome), **Copy link**, or the link by email (it joins the waitlist), and the Google button is left out there.
- **Share a crew member, or your whole crew.** **Share…** on a member (its ⋯ menu, right-click, ⌘K) gives who they are: name, face, brief, schedule, what starts them, mode, harness, delegates and, if you tick them, their skills and instructions and notebook. Their project, computer, log and jobs stay with you. **Copy** it, **Download** a `.crew.json`, **Create a gist** (secret, after you confirm) or save it **As a spell**. A scan for keys and tokens blocks a share that holds one and says where. On the phone, Share offers Copy and the gist.
- **Import someone's crew member.** Drop a `.crew.json` on wisp, paste it or a link (https, `gist:<id>`) in **Import crew members…** (⌘K, or *Import…* under *Start from* when adding a member), run `wisp crew import`, or ask an agent (*add the member from gist:abc123*). A review shows each member with the name they get (*Scout 2* when the name's taken), their harness here, their brief and kit, and their project; one that starts when CI fails needs one. They join paused, with their triggers. Also `wisp crew` and `wisp crew export`, and `POST /crew/import` / `POST /crew/export`.
- **Spells can bring crew members.** `crew` in a spell is a list of members, each with a brief, schedule, events, mode, kit and delegates. They join paused; the first **Resume** shows what they do and asks for their project. The spell keeps them up to date, and an update that changes what they do pauses them until **Resume** has shown you the change. Switched off, they leave the crew and come back as they were. **Make it mine** lets a member go from its spell; removing the spell asks whether to keep them. A spell with a member that starts on its own is marked *Runs commands*. Spells reach Share and Import as `crew.share` and `crew.import`; the review that gates a spell's own members can't be restyled or hidden.

### Changed

- **A livelier "is working":** the spinner ring is now a small dot grid with a ripple running out from its centre, and the working panel's *… is working* has a soft light passing over it, in the feed, split view, the crew and the site's demo.
- **A crew member's details fit the window without scrolling.** One line per fact, Check in now / Edit… / Remove in a row, shorter notebook and brief previews (Show all opens the rest), and one line per skill.
- **Split view's home shows what your crew needs from you.** A crew member's question or a report you haven't read now sits in *Needs you* above the tips, with the member's face, among your other tasks; clicking it opens the job under its member.

### Fixed

- **Ship it no longer fails now and then with `index.lock: File exists`.** wisp's own `git status` checks on a task's worktree took git's index lock while Ship it was committing there; they now read without locking.
- **A job you give a crew member shows under them at once.** Its *Starting up* card used to appear only when the server had started the job, a beat after you sent it; now it's there the moment you press Enter, and the real job takes its place.

## [0.40.0] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **Every section of the task list folds:** click *Needs you*, *Crew* or *Working* (or Tab to it and press Enter) to fold it away, like *Done* and *Archived*, in split view's sidebar and the feed. wisp remembers what you folded; *Needs you* folded still shows how many tasks wait on you, the task you have open stays in its row, and opening a task from a notification or link unfolds its section. Spells reach the new parts as `section.needs`, `section.working` and `section.heading`.
- **Your crew: named agents that keep working for you.** Add one from the **+** on the *Crew* section, above *Working* in split view and the feed: a name, a role, their job (the brief), how often they check in (every 30 min, hourly, weekday mornings, a cron, or only when asked), how many jobs they run at once, what they may do without asking (Plan, Edits or Full auto, from the launcher’s mode menu), their project, harness and computer, and their own delegates (any harness, or other crew members). Start from a template (CI watcher, PR reviewer, Issue triager, Dependency keeper, Release notes) or turn any task into one with **Add to crew…** (right-click, *More*, ⌘K).
- **Each member is a spirit.** A little will-o'-the-wisp you build from a body, a glow, eyes, a mouth and an extra (antenna, halo, crown, headphones…) or roll with the dice; an emoji works too. It breathes and blinks while idle, floats and looks around while working, hops when it needs you, and closes its eyes when paused.
- **A member opens like a task.** Click one and its thread fills the main pane (in the feed it unfolds in place): every job as a conversation, what was asked and where from (*You*, *Check-in*, *Tagged in “…”*, *Delegated by “…”*, a trigger), then its answer. Quiet check-ins fold into one line. A job that needs you shows its question with its choices and a box to answer right there; a finished one has **Open job** (the whole task, with a crumb back to the member) and **Follow up**. The box at the bottom hands them a job, and `@` brings another crew member into it. Beside the thread: their schedule and next check-in, where and how they run, their delegates, their notebook (**Edit** opens it in a dialog with room to read and write it) and brief, and the tasks they're part of. Esc goes from a job to the thread, then home.
- **Crew work has its own flow.** Their jobs no longer show in *Needs you*, *Working* or *Done*: only the live ones (working, asking you, a report you haven't read) sit under the member in the list, and the row says what kind of work it is (*Making the rounds* on a check-in, *Helping out “…”* when delegated, *Juggling 3 jobs*) rather than repeat the job’s own line, or *Asking you: …* or *Reported: …*, with an amber or blue dot on their face. Their notifications say who it is and open the thread at that job; none are shown while you're looking at it. Reading a report in the thread marks it read.
- **Crew members remember, without filling their context.** Every job starts fresh with your brief, their *notebook* (facts and last-seen state they keep with a hidden block at the end of each job) and a log of their recent jobs. A check-in that finds nothing is filed away quietly. They can ask to look again sooner (*Looks again 14:30*).
- **@Name in any prompt or follow-up.** A new task that starts with `@Ada` is Ada's job. A follow-up that starts with `@Ada` is answered on that task, under your message, without interrupting its agent, and **Reply to Ada** carries the conversation on there (the same job, so Ada remembers it). Named further in (`fix it, then have @Ada review it`), Ada joins the task's delegates. Both together (`@Ada and @Bo redo the crew section`): it's Ada's job, and Bo joins Ada's delegates for it, with Ada told to hand Bo their part; the same in a follow-up. A member can also send a follow-up to the agent of a task it was tagged in or delegated by, by itself (*@Ada (your crew’s CI watcher): …*), and its thread there moves along with it. Following up adds the member to that task's delegates, so its agent can hand the work back once it's done (*check my fix*); a job delegated that way reports to the agent and never follows up on it again, so the two can't bounce work back and forth. Triggers can **Send to** a crew member too.
- **Crew members can live on your other machines.** **Runs on** in the editor puts their jobs on one of your machines (the project and harness follow, from what's there); their memory, schedule and queue stay on this computer, and what each job learned comes back. **Add to crew…** on a task on a machine makes a member there.
- **Crew members start when things happen, not only on a clock.** *Also starts when* in the editor: **A task finishes**, **I mark a task done** (shipping it counts), **CI fails**. A task one finishes or is marked done in their project (any project, if they have none) becomes a job about that task, shown as *Finished “…”* or *Marked done “…”* in their thread; they read it, may send its agent a follow-up (*fix this*), and say nothing when all is well. The new *Verifier* template checks every finished task. Their own jobs never start them, a watcher's work never starts another watcher, and one task starts a member at most 3 times. Triggers gain the same two kinds (*Task finished*, *Task marked done*), sent to a member or to a new task in that task's folder. Spells reach the chips as `crew.editor.events`.
- **Ask any task to change your crew:** *add a crew member who reviews new PRs every hour*, *pause Ada*, *remove Quill*. Every agent wisp runs has `wisp_crew`, `wisp_crew_add`, `wisp_crew_edit` and `wisp_crew_remove` (a subagent or a member's own job can't change the crew), and `wisp mcp` has them too, for Claude Code, Codex or any MCP client outside wisp.
- **API:** `/crew`, `POST /crew/:id/jobs`, `POST /crew/:id/checkin`, `POST /sessions/:id/crew`, `POST /sessions/:id/crew-reply`, `crew` on triggers, trigger kinds `finished` and `done`, `on` on a member, `{ crew: id }` in `subagents`, `machine` and `team` on a member.
- **Crew members get their own skills and AGENTS.md files.** Under *Skills and instructions* beside a member's thread, **Add** an instructions file they follow on every job (an AGENTS.md just for them), or a skill: a how-to with *when to use it* that they read only when a job fits, like Claude's SKILL.md. Write one, pick from the skills and AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md files already on your computer (`~/.claude/skills`, `~/.codex/AGENTS.md`, …), upload `.md` files, or drop them on the section; pasting a whole SKILL.md fills in its name and description. A switch keeps one without using it. Works with every harness, and on members that run on another machine. Spells reach it as `crew.kit` and `crew.kitEditor`; the API as `kit` on a member and `GET /crew-kit`.
- **A crew member can build its own kit.** **Add → Generate with Ada…**, say what they should get great at (*Make yourself the best UI engineer for this project*, or pick a suggestion), and Ada runs a job that reads its brief, notebook and current kit and the project's docs and code, then proposes an AGENTS.md and a few skills written for this project. They show under that job in the thread with a box each: click one to read or edit it first, untick what you don't want, **Add to their kit** or **Dismiss**. A file with the name of one they have says *Replaces yours*. Nothing joins the kit until you pick it, and members can propose improvements to their own skills after any job the same way. Spells reach it as `crew.kitGenerate` and `crew.offer`; the API as `kit: true` on `POST /crew/:id/jobs` and `offers` on a member.
- **Two tasks changing the same files are flagged before you ship them:** when another active task (Working or Needs you) in the same repo changes the same files, both cards show an *Overlap* pill, and the warning above the follow-up box names the other task and the files, with **Open**. If merging both would really conflict (wisp merges their work as it stands with `git merge-tree`, uncommitted edits included), the pill turns amber and says *Clash*, with the conflicting files. The card lists the first two (clashes first) and *+ n more* opens them all; Ship it and Merge repeat the warning before you confirm. Only active tasks count: Done, stopped, archived and merged ones never do, and a warning clears as soon as the other task leaves. Runs of one compare are left out. Spells reach it as `card.overlap` and `card.overlapNote`.
- **Full screen goes through a reply's pictures and videos:** open a picture full screen, or a video with its new **Full screen** button, and the viewer gets previous / next arrows and a *2 / 3* count. ← / → and a sideways swipe also step through every picture and video in the reply, or through a message's attached pictures. A video plays there with its own controls. In the reply itself, pictures and videos still sit one under another. Spells reach the arrows as `viewer.nav`.

### Changed

- **Search puts recent things first:** in ⌘K and the project picker, a task you touched recently or a project you used recently now ranks above older ones that match the query about as well. Only a clearly better text match still wins over a newer one.

### Fixed

- **The JS SDK's deletes work:** `client.remove(id)` and `client.removeProject(id)` sent no body, which wisp refuses (*json only*); every write now goes as JSON.
- **Delegates survive an interrupting follow-up:** a follow-up sent (not queued) while the lead waits on its delegates used to cut the lead off from them. They kept working, but their reports were lost. Now the lead's new turn takes them over: it's told who is still working, `subagent_results` collects them, and their edits count on it. If the lead finishes before a delegate does, wisp sends the delegate's report to the lead as a follow-up of its own (*Sent by wisp when your subagents finished*). **Stop** still stops the delegates too.
- **wisp Grid's landing page answers link checkers:** a `HEAD` request to the sign-in page (or the account page) got a 404 while a normal visit worked, so ad review and link previews could take the page for dead. Both now answer `HEAD` like `GET`.

## [0.39.0] - 2026-10-25

### Changed

- **Automated tasks look different in the list:** a task a recipe started shows a bolt instead of the round status dot, and one a trigger started (a schedule, failed CI, a labelled issue) shows a circular arrow, in the project's colour and with the same pulse while it works. Hover it for the recipe's or trigger's name.
- **Agents' answers save you the follow-up you'd have typed:** wisp now tells agents which block to reach for. A code change ends with the next ship step as a one-click action, worded the way you ask for it. A UI change comes with a screenshot or live preview. A release or deploy lists every stage, done or not. A branch is checked against its base before it's handed back. An audit lets you pick which findings to fix. A long plan comes as a document. Warnings are kept for what needs your attention.
- **Signed out, the sidebar keeps your account row:** after signing out of a wisp Grid the foot still shows who you were, greyed, saying *Not connected · Sign in*; a click signs you back in. Never signed in, the same row offers the sign-in.
- **A new sidebar in split view:** *New task* is a small prompt box, with *Machines* and *Automations* side by side under it as tiles. Settings moved to the sidebar's foot, and when you're signed in to a wisp Grid (or on your cloud computer) the foot shows you: your avatar, name, handle and grid, with a ring that lights up while the grid is reachable. Click it to open your account. Group headings trail into a hairline, and the list sits on its own quieter surface. Spells reach the new parts as `sidebar.nav` and `sidebar.account`.

### Fixed

- **Ship it works as soon as a task is done:** for up to 3 seconds after a task showed as ready, *Ship it*, *Fix conflicts* and undo could answer *Still working* while the agent's CLI was still exiting. wisp now lets that CLI go and carries on. Deleting a wisp Grid account also waits longer for Windows to release the box's files.
- **The reply box names the harness you switched to:** while a task runs, picking another harness for the follow-up left the box saying *Steer Claude Code…*. It now says *Hand over to Codex — interrupts Claude Code and sends now* (or *Queue a follow-up for Codex* when queueing).
- **Deleting a running task just deletes it:** it no longer comes back under *Needs you* for a moment with a "ready" toast (and a phone or webhook notification) as its run is stopped.
- **wisp Grid shuts down cleanly with starts waiting:** stopping the grid while someone's wisp waited for room let that start carry on after the database had closed (`statement has been finalized`). Waiting starts now give up when the grid stops, and nothing is recorded after it has.
- **The account row in the sidebar shows a pointer:** hovering *wisp Grid · Not connected* showed a text cursor; it's a button. Clicking it puts you in the grid's address to sign in, not in Machines' SSH field.
- **The logo takes you to the prompt box:** clicking wisp's logo in the top bar closes the open task in split view and shows the prompt box, or scrolls the feed back up to it, focused.
- **Menu titles stay on one line:** *Add a delegate* (and *Run on*) no longer break in two next to their hint; the hint moves under the title when it doesn't fit.
- **Toasts no longer cover the reply box:** a toast such as *… is ready · Open* sat over an open task's follow-up buttons (Ship it, Mark done). It now rises above the reply box, including when you open a task while the toast is showing.
- **A phone browser's reply box fits on one line:** at phone width the follow-up's permission mode is just its icon, as in the Android app, so *Full auto* no longer wraps onto two lines. The floating *Collapse* button no longer sits on the open card; tap the card's head to collapse it.
- **Long links no longer run off the screen:** a prompt or answer with a long URL (or any unbroken word) now wraps inside its card instead of spilling past the edge on phones.
- **Compare on Windows no longer fails to make its worktrees now and then:** two checkouts into one repo at the same moment could catch each other half made (`failed to read .git/worktrees/…/commondir`). Worktrees in one repo are now made one after the other.
- **Edit diffs from Antigravity, Copilot, Codex, OpenCode, Cursor and Grok no longer show an empty "before" now and then:** a file read the instant the harness was rewriting it is no longer taken for its old content.
- **Codex lets you pick a model:** the picker only offered *Default*. It now lists the models Codex's own `/model` shows (read from its catalog in `~/.codex/models_cache.json`), each with the thinking levels it supports.
- **Thinking levels come from each harness's CLI:** wisp kept its own lists, so newer levels never showed up. It now reads them from the CLI itself: Codex's catalog (`codex debug models`, so *Ultra* on models that have it, and *Default* takes the levels of the model it runs), `--help` for Claude Code, Antigravity (now up to *Max*) and Copilot (*None* and *Minimal* to *Max*), and Grok's default model. OpenCode already read them per model; Cursor bakes the level into the model name.

## [0.38.0] - 2026-10-24

### Added

- **Delegate to your other machines:** the launcher's **Subagents** button is now **Delegate**, and each delegate picks where it runs: *With the lead* (as before), this computer, or any of your machines, with the harness, model and thinking level installed there. The lead's place is shown (and switchable) at the top. A delegate away from the lead gets a copy of the lead's work there (uncommitted edits too), runs hidden, and its changes come back to the lead's folder as a patch when it finishes (a three-way merge if the lead moved on; conflicts and patches that don't apply are reported to the lead). The lead hears each delegate's place in its team note. API: `machine` (`here` or a machine id) on each `subagents` member.
- **Install the Mac app with one line, without the "can't verify" warning:** `curl -fsSL https://trywisp.dev/install.sh | bash -s -- --app` downloads wisp.app from the latest release, checks it against `SHA256SUMS.txt` and puts it in /Applications, with no Node needed. curl doesn't quarantine what it downloads, so macOS opens the not-yet-notarized app straight away. `WISP_VERSION` pins a release here too.
- **Beta and nightly builds, and an update channel to follow them.** **Settings → General → Updates**: *Stable* (as before), *Beta* (previews of the next release, npm `wisp-agents@next`) or *Nightly* (a build of `main` every night the full test suite passes, npm `wisp-agents@nightly` and the apps on the [nightly release](https://github.com/WispAgents/wisp/releases/tag/nightly)). *Update to …* then offers the newest build of that channel, for npm, npx and the desktop app; a stable release that's newer still wins. Homebrew stays on stable. `install.sh` takes `WISP_VERSION=next` or `WISP_VERSION=nightly` (with `--app` too).

### Changed

- **Ship it opens on the click.** The dialog used to wait for wisp to read the worktree (git and gh) before showing, so the click seemed to do nothing for a second. It now opens at once in its final shape, with placeholders for the route and the message, and fills in when the worktree has been read.
- **trywisp.dev: every way in, at the top and the bottom.** The nav's *npx wisp-agents* pill is now **Get wisp ▾**: a menu with npx, npm, curl, brew and this OS's app (on a Mac, the curl install that skips the warning; elsewhere the download), each one click to copy, and a link to the full install section. The page's last section has the same tabs as the hero, kept in step with it.
- **trywisp.dev: npx comes first again, Download is the last tab.** Until the apps are signed, Download says what to expect on first open: on a Mac it offers the curl install (or the `.dmg`, with Privacy & Security → *Open Anyway*); on Windows, SmartScreen's *More info* → *Run anyway*; on Linux it offers the `.deb`, with the AppImage as the other choice.
- **trywisp.dev reads tidier.** The changelog page groups releases by week (the newest open, older weeks a click away; a link to a release opens its week). The hero no longer shows the version, the hero's task queue shows where the demo's rows will land instead of a blank box, and the footer stays on one row. The Cloud page and the guides say you can sign in with GitHub or Google, and the Cloud page has a *Continue with Google* button.
- **wisp Grid's pages follow its `name`.** The word beside the logo and the sign-in page's kicker come from `name` in grid.json (*wisp Grid* by default), so a grid named *wisp Cloud* says Cloud.
- **The repo has a SECURITY.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, issue templates and a pull request template.**

### Fixed

- **Settings → Spells on a phone:** a spell's *Acts for you* badge no longer squeezes onto two lines next to its name; the name, version and badge each stay whole and the badge moves under the name when the row is narrow.
- **Allow & retry right after a run ends.** Clicking it within a few seconds of the result, while the agent's CLI was still exiting, was refused with "The task has moved on since". It now lets that CLI go and starts the retry.
- **Updates order prereleases the semver way.** `0.38.0-beta.2` is newer than `0.38.0-beta.1`, and `0.38.0` newer than both. wisp used to drop everything after the `-`, so it saw `0.38.0-beta.1` and `0.38.0` as the same version and never offered the release.

## [0.37.0] - 2026-10-23

### Added

- **Sign in to opencode Zen and Go from Settings → Agents.** opencode's row has *Add key*: paste the API key from [opencode.ai/auth](https://opencode.ai/auth) for Zen, Go or both, and wisp saves it where `opencode auth login` would (`~/.local/share/opencode/auth.json`, next to your other providers). Their models then show up in the picker. *Keys* shows which ones are saved and removes them. It works for cloud computers too (Settings → Cloud). The free models still need no key.
- **`wisp computer on`: add this computer to your wisp Grid from a terminal.** It's the same as Machines → *Reach this computer from anywhere*, without opening the app, so it works on a server or over SSH. If you aren't signed in to a grid yet, it prints a code and a link and opens the link when it can. Allow the code in any browser, and it prints the computer's address on the grid once the tunnel is up. `wisp computer` shows whether it's on, `wisp computer off` takes it off, `--grid wisp.example.com` picks your own grid, and `--json` prints what the API returns.
- **Download wisp like any desktop app.** Every release now has a macOS `.dmg` (Apple silicon and Intel), a Windows installer (x64 and Arm) and a Linux AppImage and `.deb` (x64 and Arm), with Electron built in, so they need no Node.js. trywisp.dev's install boxes open on a **Download** tab that offers the build for your OS and links the others. The app updates itself from GitHub Releases: *Update to …* downloads the new build, checks it against the release's SHA-256, swaps it in and reopens wisp (a `.deb` points you to the download).
- **One release does everything.** Merging a version bump runs `.github/workflows/release.yml`: it builds the desktop apps, drafts the GitHub release with the CHANGELOG notes, the downloads and `SHA256SUMS.txt`, publishes `wisp-agents` to npm with provenance, publishes the release and bumps the Homebrew tap. Rerun it from the Actions tab to finish one that stopped half way; each step skips what's already done.
- **wisp Grid makes room instead of turning people away.** A box now starts when the host has the memory for it (`MemAvailable`, with `hostd.admit`), not only while fewer than `maxRunning` run. When there isn't room, the grid stops the box that has been quiet longest (nothing running, no page or terminal open, for 2 min) the same way the idle stop does, so its home and tasks are kept. If every box is busy, the start waits in line (*Waiting for room* on the waking page) instead of failing at once. Scheduled wakes wait behind people and never stop anyone's box.
- **Seats that grow by themselves: `auth.maxUsers: "auto"`.** The grid records how busy the host is every 5 minutes. While every seat is taken, nothing had to wait for room and the busiest moment of the last week stayed under 70% of what the host can run, it opens one more seat a day, up to `auth.seats.cap`. It never takes seats away.
- **Add a cloud machine, on the grid's account page.** Each account has one cloud wisp for now. A new row under it opens a dialog that shows the machine you have (CPU, memory) and lets you ask for more machines, a bigger one or GPUs, with a line on what you'd run. You see you're on the list right away, on the row and in the dialog; if the grid refuses, it goes back and says why. `wisp grid waitlist` lists who asked for what.
- **A waitlist in the grid.** People the full grid turns away at sign-in, and emails from trywisp.dev's form (`POST /api/waitlist`), go on a list. A seat that opens is held for a week for the oldest email on it; signing in with GitHub on that address gets it. `wisp grid waitlist` lists who's waiting and who has a seat held.
- **Three new guides on trywisp.dev for running agents from anywhere:** [Beyond Claude Code Remote Control](https://trywisp.dev/guides/claude-code-remote-control-alternative.html), [Use Codex from your phone](https://trywisp.dev/guides/use-codex-from-your-phone.html) and [Remote coding agent apps compared](https://trywisp.dev/guides/remote-coding-agent-apps-compared.html) (Happy, Grass, CloudCLI and wisp).

### Changed

- **wisp Grid and search engines.** The grid's landing page names itself canonical and serves a `robots.txt`; its other pages say `noindex`, and every answer from a person's wisp or computer address carries `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` (their `robots.txt` disallows everything).
- **No more "N seats left".** The grid's sign-in page, trywisp.dev and the Cloud page no longer count seats. They say the preview is open, or that it's full and take your email.
- **wisp Grid: GitHub and Google sign-in side by side.** When a grid offers exactly two ways in, the buttons share one row instead of stacking, so adding Google doesn't push the page down; on phones they read just *GitHub* and *Google*.

## [0.36.0] - 2026-10-23

### Added

- **Tips under the prompt box.** Three short tips about using wisp sit under Needs you on split view's home, rotating through a list of 34 on every visit or with *More tips*. Each opens the trywisp.dev guide section that explains it. Spells can hide or restyle them (the `tips` part) and set how many show (`count`).

### Fixed

- **Interrupting a run no longer flashes the task through Needs you.** A follow-up sent while the agent works used to drop the task into Needs you (and Finished) for a moment before it went back to Working. It now stays in Working until the follow-up's run ends, and only that run notifies. The same goes for a queued follow-up starting after the run before it.
- **Split view's home shows Needs you again**: up to five tasks under the prompt box, with the tips below them, so what's waiting on you is there even with the task list hidden.

## [0.35.0] - 2026-10-23

### Added

- **Live page previews in the chat.** An agent can answer with a `preview` block: the page it's working on (your dev server, a deployed URL), live in the reply, framed as a phone, tablet or browser window at the size it picked (`mobile`, `tablet`, `desktop` or any `WxH`) and scaled to fit. Switch sizes, rotate, reload or open it in a new tab from the block's bar. The page runs in a sandboxed frame on its own origin: it can't reach wisp, navigate it or open dialogs over it, and only http(s) links load.

### Changed

- **The phone app is easier to use with one thumb.** In wisp's page, a floating **New task** button opens the prompt box as a sheet from the bottom (Back, a tap outside it or sending closes it, and what you typed stays); its buttons keep their names and scroll sideways, with Send always in view, and a follow-up's bar does the same so Send stays inside its box. The project filter and the computer you're on are two matching pills in the top bar. A task's title gets the whole row, with its status, harness and time on the line under it. A question shows its choices (Yes / No, Allow / Deny) right in the row, so you answer it without opening the task. One task is open at a time, and every button is at least 44px. On the computers screen, tap anywhere on a row to open that computer (its settings are behind ⋯), the list no longer jumps when you tap one, wisp Cloud shows as online, and the first-run explanation goes away once you've opened a computer. Text and spacing follow one scale. Only the app changes: wisp in a browser or the desktop app looks the same.

### Fixed

- **`wisp ls --state` lists what the page does.** `--state=review` (with `=`) was dropped and listed everything, a misspelt state printed "No sessions.", and `--state review` included archived tasks the page no longer counts. Every `--flag=value` now works, an unknown state is an error, `--state needs` is review + waiting (the page's *Needs you*), and archived tasks only show with `--all`.
- **Desktop app: drag the window from the new task page.** In split view with no task open there was no header to grab, so the window wouldn't move from the main pane. The top of that pane now moves it again (beside the floating buttons when the task list is hidden).

- **Phone app: a way back from your computers.** *Add a machine* under the prompt, **Computers** in the top bar or *Your computers* in Settings now open the list on top of the wisp you were in: a **‹ <computer>** button at its top, or the phone's Back (button or edge swipe), takes you back to your prompt as you left it.

- **wisp Grid: the account page keeps up by itself.** A computer coming online or going offline, one being added or removed, a new signed-in device, or your cloud wisp waking or falling asleep now shows on the page as it happens, without reloading it. The grid also notices a computer that disconnects at once, instead of up to half a minute later.

- **wisp Grid: tunnel and sign-in hardening.** A computer that answers through its tunnel with an impossible HTTP status no longer crashes the grid for everyone; the grid answers 502 instead. Computers can't set cookies through the grid any more. Behind Caddy, rate limits now apply per client instead of to everyone at once, so one client can no longer use up the tunnel limit and keep every computer from reconnecting. The on-demand TLS check only answers Caddy itself, not the public. A sign-in `return` path with a tab or newline in it can no longer redirect off the grid.

- **Back in the phone app closes what's open.** Back from Settings, a menu or an open task used to leave wisp for the list of computers. Now it closes the newest of those first, and a tapped notification no longer leaves an extra step behind.
- **Undo after deleting a computer** in the phone app, and the *Removed* toast no longer disappears with the sheet it was shown on.

## [0.34.0] - 2026-10-23

### Added

- **Your own computers on wisp Grid.** In wisp on a desktop, **Machines** (or **Settings → Machines**) → *Reach this computer from anywhere*: sign in to a grid (grid.trywisp.dev or yours) and the computer joins it. It dials out and holds a tunnel open, so nothing listens on it and there's no SSH, Tailscale or open port. Your phone and your other computers signed in to the same account then open it at `<name>--<handle>.<grid>`, and its tasks run there with its own files and agents. Every computer on the grid shows up in the others' Machines, marked *Your computer · via grid* (next to *Cloud · on grid* and *Over SSH*); **Add to grid** / **Remove from grid** toggles it.
- **The phone app lists all of them.** Signed in to a grid, its first screen shows your cloud wisp and every computer you added, online or not, one tap to open. Connecting over SSH is still there, folded under *Connect over SSH instead*.
- **Guide: reach your own computer from anywhere** (trywisp.dev/guides/reach-your-computer-from-your-phone.html), and the README, wisp Cloud page, phone app README and privacy policy now cover computers on the grid and what passes through it.
- **A clearer grid account page.** It lists your cloud wisp and your computers with what each kind is, Open buttons and their state, three steps to add a computer, and which signed-in device is which computer. It works on a phone too.

- **Videos play in the chat.** When an agent makes or records a video, it shows it with a `video` block (mp4, webm, mov) and you watch it right in the reply, seeking and all. wisp keeps a copy in `~/.wisp/shots`, like screenshots, so a recording in `/tmp` doesn't vanish.
- **Show in folder** on pictures and videos in a reply opens Finder / Explorer with the file selected (where the agent saved it, or wisp's copy once that's gone). On another machine or a grid it copies the path.
- **Docs in replies.** An agent that writes a plan, report or spec can show it with a `doc` block: the reply has the file's opening as a page, with **Full screen** (a reader with the file's headings down the side and a bar that fills as you read) and **Show in folder**. wisp keeps the file's text with the turn, so it reads the same after the file changes. Spells can restyle it (`doc`, `doc.preview`, `doc.reader`, `doc.contents`).
- **Interactive answers.** An agent can answer with an `app` block: a small page you use right in the answer, like a curve with a live preview, a list of findings to pick from or results to filter. It runs in a sandbox with no network and no access to wisp or your files, follows wisp's light or dark theme, fits a phone's width, and keeps where you left it. It sends nothing on its own: what it offers shows in a strip under it (Send, Edit first, Dismiss). Where it can't run (`wisp show`, MCP, webhooks) its `fallback` blocks stand in. On by default; turn it off in Settings → General → **Interactive answers** (`defaults.apps: "off"`), or with a spell (`settings.apps`).
- **Subagents in a follow-up.** The follow-up box has a Subagents button: give a task that started without a team one from its next follow-up (any harness, model and thinking level, or a saved preset), change it, or clear it. Subagents the lead already used keep their conversation. The API takes `subagents` / `preset` on `POST /sessions/:id/reply`; spells can restyle or hide the button (`composer.team`).
- **Spells can draw apps too.** A spell's script can return an `app` block in its panels and in the block types it adds (see the *Rank block* example), and restyle apps through the `block.app` and `block.app.head` parts.

### Fixed

- **Phone-width follow-up box: Ship it is just its icon**, like Mark done next to it.
- **Phone app: a follow-up's mode is just its icon.** The Full auto / Plan / Edits button in the follow-up box drops its text in the app, so the bar fits on one row; the icon keeps its colour and the menu still names each mode.
- **Phone app opens on your computers.** It no longer jumps into the computer used last when it starts (which also opened ones that were off, or the cloud wisp instead of the computer you'd used). You pick one from the list, with the last one used at the top. The list's Online / Offline also updates by itself every 10 seconds while it's on screen.
- Code blocks in markdown no longer draw an inline-code box around every line.
- In split view, clicking a task in the list puts the cursor in its follow-up box, ready to type. `J` / `K` still keep the keys in the list. A box you were typing in stays open as you move between tasks instead of folding and opening again.

## [0.33.1] - 2026-10-23

### Changed

- **Small things that read more clearly.** Settings has a cog (the old icon looked like a theme switch). The prompt box names its mode ("Full auto" isn't just an amber sparkle any more) and where tasks run ("This computer"). Compare is a + on the harness pill rather than "vs". No folder and recipes have their own icons, so the sparkle doesn't stand for four things. The sidebar's foot keeps "Get the app" spelled out.
- The agent's own verdict on a task reads **Finished** (also in reports), so "Done" only means the list you move a task to.
- Project chips count the same tasks as Needs you (before, they only counted unread ones, so "All 1" sat above "Needs you 2"), and say so on hover.
- Times in the list show the full date on hover, and the open task's details say "28m ago", not "28m", next to how long it ran. Titles and summaries cut short in the list show in full on hover.
- The page says "tasks" everywhere it said "sessions" (search, Automations, Memory, Manage projects, Delete task), and the search palette's actions for the open task are under **This task**.
- Asking before deleting, stopping or merging is a small box by the button you clicked, with the action on its button ("Delete recipe", "Merge PR"), instead of the browser's OK / Cancel dialog.
- Hovering a review row in the split sidebar shows the done button beside the time, not over it.

## [0.33.0] - 2026-10-23

### Changed

- **A new logo: a handwritten w that ends in wisp's orb.** It replaces the orb everywhere: the app and favicon icons (web, docs site, desktop app, Grid), the Android launcher, themed and status bar icons, the Play store art and the share images, and the small mark beside "wisp" in the page, the site, Grid and the Android app. `node scripts/logo.mjs` draws every vector copy.
- The header shows just the logo now, without the word "wisp" beside it, in the page and the Android app.
- **The sidebar button next to Search hides the task list in split view.** The open task (or the prompt box) gets the whole window and the button waits in the corner to bring the list back; `[` does the same, and the browser remembers your choice. In the feed the same button opens split view; the feed itself is still `\`, the command palette or Settings → Layout.

### Fixed

- Leaving split view for the feed (the top bar button, `\`, Settings) did nothing but flip the button's icon: the layout stayed split until a reload.
- In the desktop app, the buttons left in the corner once the task list is hidden couldn't be clicked (the window's drag area under them took the clicks), so the list couldn't be brought back.

## [0.32.0] - 2026-10-23

### Changed

- **Take over opens in a terminal inside wisp by default** on macOS and Linux, instead of a terminal app. Pick Auto-detect or a terminal app in Settings → General → Take over in to go back; a setting you already chose is kept. Windows still opens a terminal app.
- **Each task keeps its scroll position across reloads.** Split view already took you back to where you were in a task when you opened it again; that place is now saved in the browser, and the open task's is written as the page goes away, so a reload (⌘R, wisp updating, the phone dropping the page) opens it on the same spot. In the feed, the open task you were reading comes back where it was too.
- The "Nothing needs you" line above the task list is gone, in the feed and in split view's sidebar: the group headings already say what needs you.

### Added

- **Copy a picture from a reply and paste it anywhere** (Slack, a doc, an issue): hover it for **Copy image** and **Download**, or open it and use the same buttons in the viewer, where ⌘C / Ctrl+C copies too. Any format is copied as a PNG, which every app pastes. A spell can restyle or hide the buttons (`image.tools`).
- **The address bar follows the open task** (`#/s/<id>`, the task opened last in the feed), so reloading keeps it open and a copied link opens it in another tab or browser. Opening a `#/s/<id>` address opens that task, as before.

### Fixed

- **Split view from the first paint**: the page no longer shows the feed while its scripts load and then jumps to split view (most visible on wisp Grid, where they come over the network).

## [0.31.0] - 2026-10-23

### Added

- **Light and dark mode in Settings → General → Theme**: System (follows your computer, as before), Light or Dark. It also switches from ⌘K, and a spell can set it (`settings.theme`). The page paints the chosen theme from the first frame.

### Changed

- **The page is faster.** Tasks open without waiting on the server: one you just had open (J / K, or a click), the row under the pointer, and in split view the rows either side of the open task are already loaded, so they show at once and update in the background instead of showing a Loading line. A live update to the task list costs about a twentieth of what it did, because unchanged rows, project chips and split-view measurements are left alone instead of rebuilt. The page loads the task list and machines once at startup instead of twice, and big API answers are gzipped: the task list is about 6× smaller over the phone's tunnel. Scripts and stylesheets are named by their content and cached for good, so a reload asks the server for none of them: on wisp Grid, a reload over the internet now shows your tasks in a fraction of the time, and Grid paints the tasks it saw last while the fresh ones load, as the phone app does.
- **The top of split view's details column is a tidy list:** one fact a row (state and time, the harness, the project with its colour, the branch), each behind a small dot or icon so the text lines up. Before, a pill, a tag and the time sat on uneven lines.

### Fixed

- Picking a project, or closing the harness / model, computer or mode picker (with or without a pick, by Escape, clicking its button again or clicking the box), puts the caret back in the prompt or follow-up box so you can keep typing.
- Opening a task in the feed no longer shifts its state pill and time sideways, and Done rows line up with the rows above them.
- In split view, the Mark done button on a selected or hovered row no longer leaves a clipped letter of the time beside it.
- The Queue toggle in a running task's follow-up box is now a switch: accent-coloured with the knob on the right when follow-ups queue, faint with the knob on the left when they send now. Spells can restyle it as `composer.queue`.
- In split view, when the open task moves to Working and its row is still in sight, the sidebar no longer scrolls to follow it.
- **Spell chips over the follow-up box work in split view.** Pressing one unfolded the box, the chip moved before the click landed, and nothing happened. Now only typing in the box unfolds it.

## [0.30.4] - 2026-10-23

### Changed

- **Mark done is green, like Reopen**, on the open task's bar, in split view's details column and next to the follow-up box, so the step that ends a task is easy to find.

## [0.30.3] - 2026-10-22

### Removed

- **Replay presentation.** It re-drew the task's report, which shows the same as before since reports reveal only the first time, so it looked like it did nothing. It's gone from *More*, ⌘K and the `R` key (`⇧R` still reruns).

### Fixed

- **No stray dot on flow diagrams.** Each wire's moving dot sat in the diagram's top-left corner until its animation started; it now stays hidden until it begins moving.

## [0.30.2] - 2026-10-22

### Changed

- **Merge and Fix conflicts sit in the same place on a PR.** The PR's next step always comes last: in split view's details column a full-width button after *Check now* (green Merge, red Fix conflicts), in the feed's PR row at its right end. Before, Fix conflicts came first and Merge last, so the button moved when the state changed.

## [0.30.1] - 2026-10-22

### Changed

- **Rerun… is in the open task's bar**, where Replay was (and in split view's details column): the same dialog as the task's right-click menu, `⇧R`. Replay presentation is under *More*, and `R` still does it.
- **An open task's *More* has everything its right-click menu has:** besides Rename, Copy task ID, Archive and Delete, now Save as recipe…, Merge PR, Compare runs, Replay presentation and the menu items spells add. With Rerun, Ship it and Mark done on the bar, split view's details column needs no right-click. *More* opens upwards when there's no room under it.

### Fixed

- **Picking a model, mode or computer keeps the follow-up box open in split view.** It folded back to one line as soon as the picker closed, so you had to click it again; it now stays open until you click or tab somewhere else, and after picking a model you're back typing in it.
- **Split view no longer throws an error every 30 seconds** while a task is open (its time moved to the details column; the refresh still looked in the header).

## [0.30.0] - 2026-10-22

### Added

- **↑ brings back the tasks you launched before.** In an empty prompt box, `↑` puts the last task you sent back in it, `↑` again the one before (the last 50 are kept); `↓` goes forward, and past the newest empties the box. Send one again with a tweak, or to another project, without typing it out. Once you edit it, the arrows move the caret as usual.

### Changed

- **What you type survives a reload.** A task half-written in the prompt box and unsent follow-ups are kept as you type, so wisp updating itself, `⌘R`, or the phone dropping the page no longer throws them away.
- **Marking an open task done opens the next one that needs you**, in the feed too (split view already did): read one report after another with `E`.
- **Split view is the default on big screens, and it's lighter.** With no layout picked, windows 1280px and wider open in split view and narrower ones in the feed (a layout you pick with `\`, the sidebar's layout button or Settings sticks; a feed saved before this goes back to the default once). The top bar is now the sidebar's head (search, layout, settings, the project filter) and *Get the app*, *Machines* and *Automations* sit at its foot, so the open task has the window's full height. Its header is just the title. On a wide window, a details column beside the task holds what used to be stacked over it: the harness by name, the project, when it last changed, the branch, the PR as a card of its own (state and checks, *GitHub*, *Merge*, *Fix conflicts*, Ship it's progress) and the actions with their keys. It stays put while the task scrolls, so they're in reach anywhere in a long report. The follow-up box is one line until you click into it. Sidebar rows give the title the width (the state and PR move under the time), and home is just the prompt box, since what needs you is in the sidebar beside it. Spells reach the new pieces: the `split.details` slot takes buttons, the `split.details` part a `width`, and `split.pr`, `split.actions` and `sidebar.foot` can be restyled or hidden.
- **The branch shows once per task.** A task's own worktree branch is in its header bar (the details column in split view); each message under it shows just the commit it started from (`ef90ec7`), not the branch again. A task in the project folder still names the branch when a turn ran on a different one.

### Fixed

- **Pictures in a reply can be zoomed, on a phone too.** Tapping one showed it a little bigger and no more: the phone app has no pinch zoom, and a pinch in a browser zoomed the whole page. It now opens full screen, where a pinch, a double tap or the wheel zooms in on that spot (up to the picture's own pixels and past), a drag moves around it, and − / + / 0 or the buttons at the bottom zoom too. A tap beside it, a swipe down, `Esc` or × closes it. Pictures attached to a task open the same way.
- **A file dropped next to the prompt box is attached instead of opened.** Dropped anywhere else on the page, the browser opened it in place of wisp, and the desktop app handed it to Preview. It now goes to the box you most likely meant: the follow-up box of the open task you dropped it on (or the one split view shows), else the prompt box, which lights up while you drag. A screenshot pasted while no box has focus lands there too, and a `.spell.json` dropped anywhere opens its review, as it was meant to.
- **A file dropped on the prompt box is uploaded once.** Every time the prompt box repainted (settings or harnesses loading), dropping a file uploaded it once more.

- **Signing in to a harness from Settings → Agents works on Windows.** wisp started `claude`, `codex` and `gh` directly, which can't run the `.cmd` shims npm installs them as there, so the sign-in failed at once. It now unwraps them the way it does for tasks.
